Let us consider the limiting case
b=0
in (
4.8
). Namely (cf. (
4.16
)) we have

(4.22)

(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-1/r),r}=T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

see (
3.5
). In the special case
r=q
, we have (cf. (
3.6
))

(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-1/q),q}=B^{s,0,1/q}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 4.6
.

The limiting cases
p=1,\infty
in Theorem
4.2
deserve special attention. Unlike in the case
p\in(1,\infty)
, the Sobolev spaces
W^{k}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
W^{k}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, do not fit into the scale of Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. However, the interpolation formulae (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) still are valid with
W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,p=1,\infty
. More precisely, let
p=1,\infty
and
q,r\in(0,\infty]
, then

(4.23)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r},\qquad s<k,\qquad b>0

and

(4.24)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r},\qquad s>k,\qquad b<0.

To prove (
4.23
), we make use of the well-known embeddings (cf.
[
Tri83
, Section 2.5.7]
)

B^{k}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{k}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

By (
4.8
), we derive

\displaystyle T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{k}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}\hookrightarrow(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{k}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The proof of (
4.24
) can be obtained similarly but now invoking (
4.9
).

4.3.
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces are closed under interpolation.

Our next result proves that real interpolation between spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
s,p
and
q
fixed produces a space of the same type.

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
0<p,q,r,r_{0},r_{1}\leq\infty,-\infty<s,b_{0},b_{1}<\infty
and
0<\theta<1
. Assume further
b_{0}\neq b_{1}
and
b_{0}b_{1}>0
and let
b=(1-\theta)b_{0}+\theta b_{1}
. Then

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Proof.

The assumption
b_{0}b_{1}>0
means that both
b_{0}
and
b_{1}
are either positive or negative. Assume first that
0<b_{0}<b_{1}
. Let
s_{0}>s
. According to Theorem
4.2
(i),

T^{b_{i}}_{r_{i}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b_{i}-1/r_{i}),r_{i}},\qquad i=0,1.

Let
\gamma:=b-1/r
. In light of Lemma
4.1
(iii), one has

\displaystyle(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt((B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b_{0}-1/r_{0}),r_{0}},(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b_{1}-1/r_{1}),r_{1}})_{\theta,r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\gamma),r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where the last step follows again from Theorem
4.2
(i).

The case
b_{0}>b_{1}>0
can be reduced to the previous case. More precisely, basic properties of the real interpolation method (cf. (
4.4
)) yield that

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=(T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{1-\theta,r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

This completes the proof under
b_{0},b_{1}>0
. The situation
b_{0},b_{1}<0
can be settled following similar ideas but now employing Theorem
4.2
(ii) and Lemma
4.1
(iv).

∎

In order to show that
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces are closed under interpolation, we will need the results from the next section.

4.4.
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces via interpolation

The goal of this section is to show that the scale
\{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}
can be generated as limiting interpolation involving the classical Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. To be more precise, we establish the following

Theorem 4.8

(Characterization of
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via limiting interpolation)
.

Let
A\in\{B,F\}
. Let
p\in(1,\infty),q,q_{0},r\in(0,\infty],s,s_{0}\in\mathbb{R},s\neq s_{0}
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
.

(i)

If
s_{0}>s
and
b>0
then

(4.25)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

(ii)

If
s_{0}<s
and
b<0
then

(4.26)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}.

The proof of Theorem
4.8
will rely on two interpolation lemmas, which are of independent interest.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
(A_{0},A_{1})
be a quasi-Banach pair and let
0<p<\infty
. Then

(4.27)

K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1}))\asymp\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx\bigg)^{1/p}

for
t>0
and
f\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0})+L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1})
.

Proof.

This result was already stated without proof in
[
Per84
, p. 218]
. For the sake of completeness, next we provide details of the proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
f(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{j}\chi_{\Omega_{j}}
where
a_{j}\in A_{0}\cap A_{1}
, the
\Omega_{j}
’s are measurable sets in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
of finite measure with
\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega_{j}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
and
\chi_{\Omega_{j}}
are the related characteristic functions.
This assumption allows us to avoid unnecessary complications related to measurability. Then

\displaystyle K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1}))^{p}

\displaystyle\asymp\inf_{f=f_{0}+f_{1}}(\|f_{0}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0})}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1})})

\displaystyle=\inf_{f=f_{0}+f_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\|f_{0}(x)\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}(x)\|_{A_{1}}^{p})\,dx.

Assume
f=f_{0}+f_{1}
with
f_{i}\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{i}),\,i=0,1
. In particular,
f(x)=f_{0}(x)+f_{1}(x)
which implies

\displaystyle K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}

\displaystyle\asymp\inf_{f(x)=a_{0}+a_{1}}(\|a_{0}\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|a_{1}\|_{A_{1}}^{p})

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{0}(x)\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}(x)\|_{A_{1}}^{p}

and integrating this inequality

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\|f_{0}(x)\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}(x)\|_{A_{1}}^{p})\,dx.

Taking now the infimum over all possible decompositions
f=f_{0}+f_{1}
, we arrive at

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx\lesssim K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1}))^{p}.

Conversely, given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, consider any decomposition
f(x)=f_{0}(x)+f_{1}(x)
with
f_{i}(x)\in A_{i},\,i=0,1
, such that

\|f_{0}(x)\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}(x)\|_{A_{1}}^{p}\lesssim K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}.

Integrating over all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we derive

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\|f_{0}(x)\|_{A_{0}}^{p}+t^{p}\|f_{1}(x)\|_{A_{1}}^{p})\,dx\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx.

In particular,

K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1}))^{p}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx.

∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
0<p<\infty
and
0<q,r\leq\infty
.

(i)

Assume
-\infty<s<s_{0}<\infty
and
b>0
. Then

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

(with the corresponding modifications if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

(ii)

Assume
-\infty<s_{0}<s<\infty
and
b<0
. Then

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

(with the corresponding modifications if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Proof.

(i): Specializing (
4.27
) with
(A_{0},A_{1})=(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q})
and using (cf. (
4.11
))

(4.28)

K(t,x;\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q})\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}[\min(2^{\nu s},t2^{\nu s_{0}})|x_{\nu}|]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},

we derive

(4.29)

K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))\asymp\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}[\min(2^{\nu s},t2^{\nu s_{0}})|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{1/p}.

Inserting this formula into the definition of limiting interpolation space (
4.6
),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[(1+j)^{b-1/r}K(2^{-j(s_{0}-s)},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q})]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s}\min(1,2^{(\nu-j)(s_{0}-s)})|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-s_{0})r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{\nu s_{0}}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-128.0374pt+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=j}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt=:I+II.

Next we prove

(4.30)

I\lesssim II,

which immediately leads to

(4.31)

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}\asymp II.

To proceed with (
4.30
), we consider two cases. Firstly, we assume
q\geq p
. Then, for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{\nu s_{0}}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\leq\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{\nu s_{0}}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}

which yields, by Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) (note that
s_{0}>s
),

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-s_{0})r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu s_{0}p}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jsr}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\|f_{j}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}\leq II.

Secondly, if
q<p
we can apply Minkowski’s inequality to get

\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{\nu s_{0}}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{1/p}\leq\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu s_{0}q}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

Accordingly, by Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
),

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-s_{0})r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu s_{0}q}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jsr}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\|f_{j}\|^{r}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{1/r}\leq II.

This completes the proof of (
4.30
).

Furthermore, we make the following claim

(4.32)

II\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Indeed, by basic monotonicity properties,

II\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

and thus the estimate
\gtrsim
in (
4.32
) is clear. Conversely, one can rewrite the previous estimate as

II\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{l=j}^{\infty}F_{l}(x)^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

where

F_{l}(x):=\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{l}}^{2^{l+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},\qquad l\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

If
q\geq p
then the embedding
\ell_{p}\hookrightarrow\ell_{q}
and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) (note that
b>0
) imply

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{l=j}^{\infty}\|F_{l}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\|F_{l}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

which gives (
4.32
).
On the other hand, if
q<p
we can apply Minkowski’s inequality together with Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) to estimate

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{l=j}^{\infty}\|F_{l}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\|F_{j}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

This finishes the proof of (
4.32
).

Putting together (
4.31
) and (
4.32
),

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}.

(ii): It follows from (
4.29
) (after replacing the roles played by
s
and
s_{0}
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{j(s-s_{0})}(1+j)^{b-1/r}K(2^{-j(s-s_{0})},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q})]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-s_{0})r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s_{0}}\min(1,2^{(\nu-j)(s-s_{0})})|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-128.0374pt+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-s_{0})r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=j}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s_{0}}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt=:\mathcal{I}+\mathcal{II}.

Furthermore, we claim

\mathcal{II}\lesssim\mathcal{I}

and

\mathcal{I}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}.

We omit the proofs of these estimates since they are obvious modifications of those in (
4.30
) and (
4.32
), respectively. Hence

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}[2^{\nu s}|f_{\nu}(x)|]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}.

∎

We are now ready to give the

Proof of Theorem
4.8
.

(i): We start by proving (
4.25
) with
A=F
and
q_{0}=q
, i.e.

(4.33)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

Since
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a retract of
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q})
, the interpolation space
(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}
can be identified, via the retraction method, with
(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}
. This space was computed in Lemma
4.10
(i). Accordingly, the desired formula (
4.33
) follows.

The formula (
4.25
) for
A=B
and
0<q_{0}\leq\infty
follows from the previous case (i.e., (
4.33
)) and the well-known interpolation formula (cf.
[
Tri83
, Theorem 2.4.2, p. 64]
)

(4.34)

(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q_{0}}=B^{(1-\theta)s+\theta s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for
\theta\in(0,1)
. More precisely, given
s_{0}>s
we choose
s_{1}>s_{0}
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that
s_{0}=(1-\theta)s+\theta s_{1}
. Then, by (
4.34
), Lemma
4.1
(i), and (
4.33
),

\displaystyle(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

\displaystyle=(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{1}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q_{0}})_{(0,b-1/r),r}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{1}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

It remains to show (
4.25
) with
A=F
and
0<q_{0}\leq\infty
with
q_{0}\neq q
. This is a simple consequence of the embeddings (
4.20
) and the corresponding assertion with
A=B
.

(ii): The proof of (
4.26
) follows similar ideas as those given in (
4.25
). Accordingly, we will only provide a sketch of the argument. We first concentrate on the case
A=F
with
q_{0}=q
and we claim that

(4.35)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}.

Indeed, this is an immediate consequence of Lemma
4.10
(ii) and the retraction method. Once formula (
4.35
) has been established, its extension from
F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to any
A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
s_{0}<s
and
0<q_{0}\leq\infty
can be obtained via Lemma
4.1
(ii) and (
4.20
).

∎

Remark 4.11
.

Similarly as in Remark
4.5
, the proof of Theorem
4.8
can be applied for the limiting case
b=0
. Namely, if
p\in(1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty],r\in(0,\infty),-\infty<s<s_{0}<\infty
, then (cf. (
3.7
))

(4.36)

(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-1/r),r}=T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Recall that
T_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\neq T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
; see Remark
3.7
.

Next we write down two important special cases of Theorem
4.8
. Namely

(4.37)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\qquad\text{and}\qquad T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

cf. Proposition
3.8
(iii) and (iv) for assumptions on the involved parameters. Accordingly, the following result is an immediate application of Theorem
4.8
.

Corollary 4.12

(
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via limiting interpolation)
.

Let
A\in\{B,F\}
.

(i)

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,q_{1}\leq\infty,s>0
and
b>-1/q
. Then

(4.38)

\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),A^{s}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}.

(ii)

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,q_{0}\leq\infty,s>0,s_{0}<s
and
b<-1/q
. Then

(4.39)

\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}.

Remark 4.13
.

The formula (
4.38
) was already obtained in
[
CDT16
, Theorem 3.4]
using the well-known fact

K(t^{k},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp t^{k}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\omega_{k}(f,t)_{p},\qquad t\in(0,1),

and reiteration formulae for limiting interpolation (cf. Lemma
4.1
). Furthermore this method also works with the limiting values
p=1,\infty
in (
4.38
).

On the other hand, (
4.39
) with
A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(i.e.,
s_{0}=0
and
q_{0}=2
) reads

(4.40)

\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b),q};

see
[
DHT20
, (2.13)]
for an alternative approach to (
4.40
) based on
K
-functionals and (fractional) moduli of smoothness.

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=B^{s,b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad s_{0}\neq s_{1},

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad b_{0}\neq b_{1}

4.5.
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces are closed under interpolation.

As an application of Theorem
4.8
, we establish the closedness of the scale of spaces
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
under classical real interpolation. More precisely, we establish the following

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,r,r_{0},r_{1}\leq\infty,-\infty<s,b_{0},b_{1}<\infty
and
0<\theta<1
. Assume further
b_{0}\neq b_{1}
and
b_{0}b_{1}>0
and let
b=(1-\theta)b_{0}+\theta b_{1}
. Then

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Proof.

Assume e.g. that
b_{0},b_{1}<0
. It is not restrictive to assume further
b_{0}<b_{1}
. Let
s_{0}<s
. By Theorem
4.8
(ii),

T^{b_{j}}_{r_{j}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b_{j}-1/r_{j}),r_{j}},\qquad j=0,1.

These formulae and Lemma
4.1
(iv) imply

\displaystyle(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt((F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b_{0}-1/r_{0}),r_{0}},(F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b_{1}-1/r_{1}),r_{1}})_{\theta,r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=(F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

where the last step follows from Theorem
4.8
(ii).

The case
b_{0},b_{1}>0
can be treated analogously, but now applying Theorem
4.8
(i).
∎

Remark 4.15
.

Theorems
4.8
and
4.14
can be extended to deal with
0<p<\infty
. We refer to Remark
7.14
below.

Remark 4.16
.

Theorems
4.2
and
4.7
as well as
Theorems
4.8
and
4.14

show some striking differences between classical interpolation (cf. (
4.2
) and (
4.3
)) and limiting interpolation (cf. (
4.6
)). Let
A,\tilde{A}\in\{B,F\}
. The following holds

(A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\tilde{A}^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r,b}=B^{(1-\theta)s+\theta s_{0},b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

provided that
s,s_{0},b\in\mathbb{R},\,s\neq s_{0},\theta\in(0,1),0<p<\infty,0<q,q_{0},r\leq\infty
; cf.
[
Tri83
, Section 2.4.2]
if
b=0
and
[
CF88
]
if
b\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, this formula tells us that any interpolation pair formed by Besov or Triebel–Lizorkin spaces with fixed integrability parameter
p
produces as an outcome the Besov space
B^{(1-\theta)s+\theta s_{0},b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Note that classical interpolation does not depend on the secondary parameters
q
and
q_{0}
. Importantly, these assertions may fail to be true dealing with limiting interpolation, as can be illustrated by

\displaystyle T^{b+1/r}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),r}

\displaystyle\neq(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),r}=T^{b+1/r}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where
q\neq p
, and

\displaystyle T^{b+1/r}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),r}

\displaystyle\neq(B^{s}_{p,\tilde{q}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),r}=T^{b+1/r}_{r}B^{s}_{p,\tilde{q}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where
q\neq\tilde{q}
.

4.6.
Interpolation in the case of non-fixed smoothness

Our next result extends
the classical embedding

(A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad A,\tilde{A}\in\{B,F\},

and, on the other hand, it complements
Theorems
4.7
and
4.14
to pairs of spaces
(T^{b}_{r}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b}_{r}A^{s_{1}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with
s_{0}\neq s_{1}
. In sharp contrast with the case
s_{0}=s_{1}
studied in these theorems, the resulting interpolation space with
s_{0}\neq s_{1}
is always a classical Besov space. In particular, we are now able to compute the interpolation space relative to
(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, which seems to be unknown in the literature; see Corollary
4.19
below.

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
A,\tilde{A}\in\{B,F\}
. Assume
0<p,q_{0},q_{1},r,r_{0},r_{1}\leq\infty\,(p<\infty\text{ when }A=F\text{ or }\tilde{A}=F),b_{0},b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\},s_{0},s_{1}\in\mathbb{R},s_{0}\neq s_{1},0<\theta<1
.
Let

s=(1-\theta)s_{0}+\theta s_{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad b=(1-\theta)b_{0}+\theta b_{1}.

Then

(4.41)

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=B^{s,b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

In particular, if
\frac{b_{0}}{b_{0}-b_{1}}\in(0,1)
then

(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\frac{b_{0}}{b_{0}-b_{1}},r}=B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 4.18
.

The outcome in (
4.41
) does not depend on
q_{0}
and
q_{1}
. This is in contrast with Theorems
4.7
and
4.14
.

Proof of Theorem
4.17
.

We will make use of embeddings between truncated and classical function spaces given in Corollaries
10.5
and
11.7
below. More precisely, we have

B^{s_{i},b_{i}}_{p,\min\{q_{i},r_{i}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{i}}_{r_{i}}A^{s_{i}}_{p,q_{i}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{i},b_{i}}_{p,\max\{q_{i},r_{i}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad i=0,1.

Therefore

\displaystyle(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt(B^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p,\max\{q_{0},r_{0}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{p,\max\{q_{1},r_{1}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}=B^{s,b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

where we have applied well-known interpolation properties of Besov spaces of logarithmic smoothness in the last step (cf.
[
CF88
, Theorem 5.3]
and
[
DT18
, Lemma 2.3]
). Analogously, one can prove

B^{s,b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow(T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}\tilde{A}^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,r}.

∎

Corollary 4.19

(Interpolation involving
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
0<\theta<1,1<p<\infty,s>0,0<q,q_{0},q_{1}\leq\infty,b_{0}>-1/q_{0}
and
b_{1}<-1/q_{1}
.

(i)

(4.42)

(\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\emph{Lip}^{s,b_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}=B^{\theta s,(1-\theta)(b_{0}+1/q_{0})+\theta(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

In particular, if
\theta=\frac{b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}}{b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}-(b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}})}
then

(\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\emph{Lip}^{s,b_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}=B^{\theta s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii)

(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\emph{Lip}^{s,b_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}=B^{\theta s,\theta(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(iii)

(\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}=B^{\theta s,(1-\theta)(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Proof.

(i): According to (
4.37
), we have

T^{b_{0}+1/r_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{0}}_{p,r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\qquad\text{and}\qquad T^{b_{1}+1/r_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,b_{1}}_{p,r_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then (
4.42
) follows from Theorem
4.17
.

(ii): Combining (
4.39
) (see also (
4.40
)) and Lemma
4.1
(vi), we get

\displaystyle(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\text{Lip}^{s,b_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}

\displaystyle=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b_{1}),q_{1}})_{\theta,q}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q,\theta(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}=B^{\theta s,\theta(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

where the last step makes use of well-known interpolation properties of classical Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces (see, e.g.,
[
CF88
, Theorem 5.3]
and
[
DT18
, (2.37)]
).

(iii): By (
4.38
) (with
A=F
and
q_{1}=2
) and Lemma
4.1
(v),

\displaystyle(\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}

\displaystyle=((L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b_{0}),q_{0}},H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\theta,q,(1-\theta)(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}=B^{\theta s,(1-\theta)(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

∎

4.7.
\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces via interpolation

Recall that the operator
\mathfrak{J}
is defined in (
4.10
). Since
\mathfrak{J}
is a retract from
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
\ell^{s}_{q}(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and from
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q})
, Theorem
4.2
(see, e.g., (
4.8
) with
A=B
) can be interpreted as

f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff\mathfrak{J}(f)\in(\ell^{s}_{q}(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}}(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

and

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{J}(f)\|_{(\ell^{s}_{q}(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}}(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}.

In the same fashion, Theorem
4.8
(specifically, (
4.25
) with
A=F
) can be rewritten as

f\in T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff\mathfrak{J}(f)\in(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

and

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{J}(f)\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}}.

Our next result shows that
\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be generated in terms of
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}})_{(0,b-1/r),r})
, i.e., after changing the roles of
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
((0,b-1/r),r)
-interpolation in the construction of truncated
F
-spaces given above.

Theorem 4.20

(Characterization of
\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via limiting interpolation)
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty),q,q_{0},r\in(0,\infty],s,s_{0}\in\mathbb{R},s\neq s_{0}
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
.

(i)

If
s_{0}>s
and
b>0
then

f\in\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{if and only if}\quad\mathfrak{J}(f)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}})_{(0,b-1/r),r}).

Furthermore

\|f\|_{\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{J}(f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}})_{(0,b-1/r),r})}.

(ii)

If
s_{0}<s
and
b<0
then

f\in\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{if and only if}\quad\mathfrak{J}(f)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};(\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}},\ell^{s}_{q})_{(1,b-1/r),r}).

Furthermore

\|f\|_{\mathfrak{T}^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{J}(f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};(\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}},\ell^{s}_{q})_{(1,b-1/r),r})}.

Proof.

The proof follows similar ideas as the proof of Theorem
4.2
. More precisely, it was already shown in (
4.15
) and (
4.17
) (modulo replacing
\|x_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
by
|x_{\nu}|
) that

(4.43)

\|x\|_{(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{0}}_{q})_{(0,b-1/r),r}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}|x_{\nu}|^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

provided that
s_{0}>s
and
b>0
. This proves (i) with
q_{0}=q
. The general case
q_{0}\in(0,\infty]
follows from reiteration formulas. Indeed, we make use of the well-known fact that (cf.
[
BL76
, Theorem 5.6.1]
and
[
Tri78
, 1.18.2]
)

\ell^{s_{0}}_{q_{0}}=(\ell^{s}_{q},\ell^{s_{1}}_{q})_{\theta,q_{0}},

where
s<s_{0}<s_{1}
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that
s_{0}=(1-\theta)s+\theta s_{1}
.

∎

5.
Characterizations of truncated Besov spaces in terms of approximation

The goal of this section is to establish characterizations of the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via approximation procedures. We restrict our attention to approximation procedures in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
p\in[1,\infty]
(and thus smoothness parameter
s>0
). However, many of the results presented in this section admit analogues in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
p>0
(and so
s>d\max\{\frac{1}{p}-1,0\}
).

Let us recall two well-known characterization of Besov spaces (see, e.g.,
[
Pi81
]
,
[
Tri83
, Section 2.5.3]
). Namely, if
p\in[1,\infty],q\in(0,\infty],s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
then

(5.1)

\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{js}(1+j)^{b}E_{2^{j}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

and if, additionally,
\alpha>0
and
r\in(0,\infty]
then

(5.2)

\|f\|_{B^{s+\alpha,b}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{j\alpha}(1+j)^{b}E_{2^{j}-1}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Here, for
X=L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we use the notation

E_{j}(f)_{X}:=\inf_{g}\|f-g\|_{X},\qquad j\geq 1,

where the infimum is taken over all entire functions
g
of exponential type
j
and

E_{0}(f)_{X}:=\|f\|_{X}
. The limiting case
s=0
in (
5.1
) characterizes
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf.
[
DT18
, Section 10.4]
). Our next result deals with the case
\alpha=0
in (
5.2
) by showing the connection with the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Let
\mu_{j}:=2^{2^{j}}
for
j\geq 1
and
\mu_{0}=0
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty],q,r\in(0,\infty]
and
s>0
.

(i)

If
b>0
then

(5.3)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{jb}E_{\mu_{j}}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

(ii)

If
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
then

(5.4)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Remark 5.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
5.1
(ii), letting
q=r
in (
5.4
), we have

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{q}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}(1+\nu)^{b}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\asymp\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where the last estimate follows from (
5.1
). Compare with Proposition
3.8
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

(i): Let
u>0
. In virtue of Theorem
4.2
(i), we have

(5.5)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s+1/u}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

Making use of the known estimate (see
[
Nil82
, Proposition 6.3]
)

K(2^{j/u},f;B^{s+1/u}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{u}\bigg)^{1/u},\qquad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

we can rewrite (
5.5
) as follows

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[(1+j)^{b-1/r}K(2^{-j/u},f;B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s+1/u}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jr/u}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{u}\bigg)^{r/u}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[(1+j)^{b-1/r}E_{2^{j}-1}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{jb}E_{\mu_{j}}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r},

where we have applied Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) in the penultimate step. The proof of (
5.3
) is complete.

(ii): The equivalence (
5.4
) with
b>0
follows from (
5.3
) and the fact that

E_{j-1}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=j}^{\infty}(\nu^{s}E_{\nu-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\frac{1}{\nu}\bigg)^{1/q},\qquad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

(cf.
[
Nil82
, Proposition 6.3]
). To be more precise, by Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) (note that
b>0
) and basic monotonicity properties, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{jb}E_{\mu_{j}}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=\mu_{j}}^{\infty}(\nu^{s}E_{\nu-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\frac{1}{\nu}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=\mu_{j}}^{\mu_{j+1}-1}(\nu^{s}E_{\nu-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\frac{1}{\nu}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Next we deal with (
5.4
) under
b<0
. Since (cf.
[
Nil82
, p. 322]
)

K(2^{js},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu sq}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},\qquad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

it follows from Theorem
4.2
(ii) (with
A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if
p\in(1,\infty)
) and (
4.24
) (with
k=0
if
p=1,\infty
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[(1+j)^{b-1/r}K(2^{js},f;B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{br}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}2^{\nu sq}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{1}{1+j}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

where we have applied Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) (taking into account that
b<0
) in the last estimate.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The above proof also deals with the limiting case
b=0
in (
5.3
) and (
5.4
). More precisely, if
s>0,1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q\leq\infty,
and
0<r<\infty
then (cf. (
3.5
))

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[E_{\mu_{j}}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

and

(5.6)

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg[\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg]^{r/q}\bigg\}^{1/r}.

Furthermore, if
b=0
and
r=\infty
then we have the trivial estimate (cf. (
3.8
))

\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}E_{\mu_{j}}(f)_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|f\|_{T^{*}_{\infty}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{\infty}[2^{\nu s}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

For
p\in[1,\infty]
we define the class

\mathfrak{U}_{p}:=\{a=(a_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:a_{k}\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\text{supp }\widehat{a}_{k}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|\leq 2^{k}\}\},

which is formed by
functions of exponential type
in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let
0<q\leq\infty
and
s>0
. A classical representation theorem in Besov spaces asserts (see, e.g.,
[
Tri83
, Section 2.5.3]
) that
f\in B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
f
can be decomposed as

(5.7)

f=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}f_{k}\quad\text{with}\quad(f_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathfrak{U}_{p}

(convergence being in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) such that

(5.8)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{ksq}\|f_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
). Furthermore

(5.9)

\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{ksq}\|f_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

where the infimum runs over all possible decompositions (
5.7
) satisfying (
5.8
).

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty],q,r\in(0,\infty],s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
.
Then
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
f
admits decomposition (
5.7
)
such that

(5.10)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
). Furthermore

(5.11)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}},

where the infimum runs over all possible decompositions (
5.7
) satisfying (
5.10
).

Proof.

Assume
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Let
(g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathfrak{U}_{p}
be such that

\|f-g_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim E_{2^{k}}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Consequently
f=\lim_{k\to\infty}g_{k}
. Define

f_{0}=g_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{k}=g_{k}-g_{k-1},\quad k\geq 1.

Clearly
f=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}f_{k}
with
(f_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathfrak{U}_{p}
. Furthermore

\|f_{0}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{and}\quad\|f_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim E_{2^{k-1}}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\quad k\geq 1.

Therefore, by (
5.4
),

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}E_{2^{\nu}-1}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty.

Conversely, assume
f
can be decomposed as (
5.7
) with (
5.10
). Given
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

E_{2^{k}}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\sum_{\nu=k}^{\infty}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Inserting this estimate into (
5.4
) and applying Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
), we get

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}\bigg(\sum_{l=\nu}^{\infty}\|f_{l}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}}^{2^{k+1}-1}2^{\nu sq}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Taking now the infimum over all possible decompositions (
5.7
) with (
5.10
), we achieve the desired result. ∎

Remark 5.5
.

(i) Assume
b>0
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
5.4
, the condition (
5.10
) implies automatically that the series
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}f_{k}
converges absolutely in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. For instance, if
q,r\geq 1
then we can apply Hölder’s inequality twice so that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|f_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r}}<\infty.

The proof is even easier if
q<1
or
r<1
.

(ii) Theorem
5.4
can be complemented by the following decomposition theorem in terms of classical Besov spaces. Namely, let
p\in[1,\infty],q,r\in(0,\infty]
and
s,b>0
, the function
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
f
can be decomposed as

(5.12)

f=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}f_{k}

(convergence in
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) such that
\text{supp }\widehat{f}_{k}\subset\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|\leq\mu_{k}\big\}
and

(5.13)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\|f_{k}\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
r=\infty
). Furthermore

(5.14)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\|f_{k}\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}},

where the infimum runs over all possible decompositions (
5.12
) satisfying (
5.13
). This result is an immediate consequence of (
5.3
) and the representation theorem for approximation spaces given in
[
FG01
, Theorem 1]
.

(iii) Theorem
5.4
with
b=0
can be obtained from similar ideas as the case
b\neq 0
but now relying on (
5.6
). More precisely, let
p\in[1,\infty],q,r\in(0,\infty]
and
s>0
. Then
f\in T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
f
admits decomposition (
5.7
)
such that

(5.15)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
). Furthermore

(5.16)

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}},

where the infimum runs over all possible decompositions (
5.7
) satisfying (
5.15
).

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty],\,q,r\in(0,\infty),s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then entire functions of exponential type are dense in
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Let
(P_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a
linear approximation scheme
on
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, that is,
P_{k}
is a linear operator acting on
f\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
P_{k}f
is an entire function of exponential type
k
and

(5.17)

\|f-P_{k}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim E_{k}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(i.e.,
P_{k}f
is an
almost best approximant of
f
). Let
Q_{k}:=P_{2^{k+1}-1}-P_{2^{k}}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
Q_{0}:=P_{2}
. Then Theorem
5.4
can be formulated as follows.

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty],q,r\in(0,\infty],\,s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Assume that
(P_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a linear approximation scheme on
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}Q_{k}f

(convergence being in
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) such that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|Q_{\nu}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
). Furthermore

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu s}\|Q_{\nu}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

Remark 5.8
.

The corresponding result for
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
according to (
5.16
) also holds true, i.e.,

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}\|Q_{\nu}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

Example 5.9

(De la Valleé-Poussin means)
.

A classical example of linear approximation scheme is given by
de la Valleé-Poussin operators
for
f\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,p\in[1,\infty]
, namely,

(5.18)

V_{t}f(x)=(\chi(t^{-1}|\xi|)\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad t>0,

where
\chi\in C^{\infty}[0,\infty)
is such that
\chi(u)=1
for
0\leq u\leq 1
and
\chi(u)=0
for
u\geq 2
. Hence Corollary
5.7
holds with
Q_{k}=V_{2^{k+1}-1}-V_{2^{k}},\,k\geq 1,
and
Q_{0}=V_{2}
.

Example 5.10

(Lizorkin-type representations)
.

A well-known fact in the theory of function spaces is that smooth partitions of unity in the Besov quasi-norms (
2.2
) can be replaced by characteristic functions related to cubes. The latter are called Lizorkin representations and have a long history, we refer the reader to
[
Tri83
,
ST87
]
and the references within. As an application of Corollary
5.7
we obtain Lizorkin representations for
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the periodic counterpart of
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Indeed, consider the set of all trigonometric polynomials of (cubic) degree less than or equal to
k
given by

\sum_{|m|_{\infty}\leq k}c_{m}e^{im\cdot x},\qquad c_{m}\in\mathbb{C}

where
m=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
|m|_{\infty}=\max_{i=1,\ldots,d}|m_{i}|
. It is well known (see, e.g.,
[
Gra08
, Corollary 3.5.2 and Theorem 3.5.7]
) that these sets determine a linear approximation scheme on
L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\,1<p<\infty
. In virtue of Corollary
5.7
(which also holds true for periodic functions and trigonometric polynomials) we obtain Lizorkin-type representations for
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
with
1<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s>0
and
b\neq 0
, namely,

(5.19)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}\bigg\|\sum_{m\in K_{\nu}}\widehat{f}(m)e^{im\cdot x}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}

where

K_{\nu}=\{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:|m_{i}|<2^{\nu+1},i=1,\ldots,d\}\backslash\{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:|m_{i}|<2^{\nu},i=1,\ldots,d\}

for
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
and
K_{0}=\{(0,\ldots,0)\}
. The equivalence (
5.19
) is still valid for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, but the proof will make use of lifting properties and it will be postponed until Corollary
8.3
.

6.
Characterizations by differences

6.1.
Characterizations of Besov spaces by moduli of smoothness

Recall the characterization of the Fourier-analytically defined Besov spaces
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
2.2
)) in terms of moduli of smoothness/differences: Let
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
. Then (cf. (
2.9
))

B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathbf{B}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

i.e.,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(t^{-s}(1-\log t)^{b}\omega_{k}(f,t)_{p})^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

(6.1)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}(2^{\nu s}(1+\nu)^{b}\omega_{k}(f,2^{-\nu})_{p})^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},

where
k>s
. Furthermore (cf. (
2.7
))

(6.2)

\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}(1-\log|h|)^{bq}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}dh\bigg)^{1/q}.

In this section we extend (
6.1
)-(
6.2
) to the setting of
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. To be more precise, we obtain the following

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,0<s<k
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)

(6.3)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}(2^{\nu s}\omega_{k}(f,2^{-\nu})_{p})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

and

(6.4)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}<|h|<t}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

(with the usual modifications if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Remark 6.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
with
r=q
and applying Fubini’s theorem, one recovers Proposition
3.8
(i).

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

The proof relies on limiting interpolation techniques. Assume first
b>0
. According to Theorem
4.2
(i) (under
p\in(1,\infty)
) and (
4.23
) (under
p=1,\infty
), the following formula holds

(6.5)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

Furthermore, using Holmstedt’s formula
[
H70
]
, we can compute the
K
-functional for the couple
(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
in terms of the
\omega_{k}(f,t)_{p}
. More precisely, we have

K(t^{1-s/k},f;B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp t^{1-s/k}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{0}^{t^{1/k}}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{1/q}

for
t\in(0,1)
(cf.
[
DT18
, Theorem 11.1(ii)]
). Inserting this into (
6.5
), after a simple change of variables, yields

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[(1-\log t)^{b-1/r}K(t,f;B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))]^{r}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/r}

(6.6)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

and, by monotonicity properties and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\lambda_{j}}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/q}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

Here
\lambda_{j}=2^{-2^{j}}
for
j\geq 0
. After applying Lemma
B.1
(cf. (
B.1
)), we achieve the first equivalence in (
6.3
) under the assumption
b>0
.

Assume
b<0
. In light of the interpolation formula (cf. Theorem
4.2
(ii) and (
4.24
))

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r}

and the fact that, for each
t\in(0,1)
,

K(t^{s/k},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp t^{s/k}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+t^{s/k}\bigg(\int_{t^{1/k}}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

(see, e.g.,
[
DT18
, Theorem 11.1(i)]
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-s/k}(1-\log t)^{b-1/r}K(t^{s/k},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))]^{r}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/r}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

A similar reasoning as in (
6.7
) leads to

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/q}

and thus, by Lemma
B.1
(cf. (
B.1
)),

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

This completes the proof of the first equivalence in (
6.3
).

The second equivalence in (
6.3
) follows easily from the first one using monotonicity properties.

By Lemma
B.1
(cf. (
B.2
)), the estimate (
6.4
) turns out to be equivalent to

(6.9)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}<|h|\leq\lambda_{j}}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Next we show (
6.9
). To proceed with, we will make use of the following well-known estimate for the modulus of smoothness

(6.10)

\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p}\asymp\bigg(u^{-d}\int_{|h|\leq u}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{1/q}

(see, e.g.,
[
KT20b
, (1.12)]
). Applying Fubini’s theorem, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}

\displaystyle\asymp\int_{0}^{t}u^{-sq-d}\int_{|h|\leq u}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\frac{du}{u}

\displaystyle\asymp\int_{|h|\leq t}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\int_{|h|}^{t}u^{-sq-d}\frac{du}{u}\,dh

\displaystyle\asymp\int_{|h|\leq t}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh.

Combining this with (
6.6
) and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
), if
b>0
then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{|h|\leq t}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}<|h|\leq\lambda_{j}}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

It remains to show (
6.9
) with
b<0
. We can proceed as follows: For each
t\in(0,1)
, we apply (
6.10
) so that

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}

\displaystyle\asymp\int_{t}^{1}u^{-sq-d}\int_{|h|\leq u}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\frac{du}{u}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\asymp t^{-sq-d}\int_{|h|\leq t}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh+\int_{t<|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}dh.

Thus

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{-sr}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(t^{-d}\int_{|h|\leq t}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t\leq|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=:I+II.

Assume momentarily

(6.11)

I\lesssim II.

By previous computations, we have

\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}\asymp II

and applying Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) (recall that
b<0
)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}<|h|\leq\lambda_{j}}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Inserting now this estimate into (
6.8
), we get

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}<|h|\leq\lambda_{j}}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r},

i.e., (
6.9
) holds.

To complete the proof of (
6.9
) with
b<0
, it remains to prove (
6.11
). Indeed, applying basic monotonicity properties and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
),

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{lsr}(1+l)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(2^{ld}\int_{|h|\leq 2^{-l}}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{lsr}(1+l)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(2^{ld}\sum_{\nu=l}^{\infty}\int_{2^{-(\nu+1)}<|h|\leq 2^{-\nu}}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{lsr}(1+l)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(2^{ld}\int_{2^{-(l+1)}<|h|\leq 2^{-l}}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(1+l)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{2^{-(l+1)}<|h|\leq 2^{-l}}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(1+l)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{2^{-(l+1)}<|h|\leq 1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp II.

∎

Remark 6.3
.

The method of proof can also be applied for the limiting case
b=0
in Theorem
6.1
; see Remark
4.5
. More precisely, if
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q\leq\infty,0<r<\infty
and
0<s<k
then

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}\right)^{1/r}

and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{|h|\leq t}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}\right)^{1/r}.

6.2.
Gagliardo–Slobodeckiĭ characterizations

Recall the well-known fact that the Besov spaces
B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be characterized in Gagliardo–Slobodeckiĭ form

(6.12)

\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dy\bigg)^{1/p}

for
p\in[1,\infty)
and
s\in(0,1)
. The space of all functions
f\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that the right-hand side of (
6.12
) is finite is commonly denoted in the literature as
W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the
fractional Sobolev space
. Specifically, for
p\in[1,\infty),0<s<k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(6.13)

\|f\|_{W^{s,b,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{(1+|\log|h||)^{bp}|\Delta^{k}_{h}f(x)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dh\bigg)^{1/p}.

Recall that
\Delta^{k}_{h}
denotes iterated differences (cf. (
2.5
)) and one can show that finiteness of (
6.13
) is independent of
k>s
. To simplify notation we simply write
W^{s,0,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Thus the extension of (
6.12
) to higher order smoothness reads
B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, i.e.,

(6.14)

\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\Delta^{k}_{h}f(x)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dh\bigg)^{1/p}

where
k>s
.

The characterization (
6.12
) is very useful in applications to trace theory, differential geometry and PDE’s (cf.
[
DiNPV12
]
and the references given there). In particular, it provides a natural way to introduce Besov spaces in the more general setting of metric measure spaces
[
BoPa03
]
.

Specializing now (
6.4
) with
p=q
we get the extension of (
6.12
) and (
6.14
) to the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty,0<r\leq\infty,0<s<k
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Let
\lambda_{j}=2^{-2^{j}}
for
j\geq 0
. Then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\left(\int_{t^{2}<|h|\leq t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\Delta^{k}_{h}f(x)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dh\right)^{r/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

(with the usual modification if
r=\infty
).

Remark 6.5
.

The logarithmic counterpart of (
6.14
) reads

B^{s,b}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=W^{s,b,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}.

This is a simple consequence of Proposition
3.8
and Theorem
6.4
with
r=p
. Let
b\neq 0
, by Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{|h|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1-\log|h|)^{bp}\frac{|\Delta^{k}_{h}f(x)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dh\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|\log|h||)^{bp}\frac{|\Delta^{k}_{h}f(x)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dh\right)^{1/p},

where the last equivalence follows from the fact that
\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(cf. (
2.5
)).

7.
Characterization via wavelets

7.1.
Preliminaries

We briefly discuss wavelet bases. For full treatment, we refer the reader to
[
Dau92
]
,
[
Mey92
]
and
[
Tri08
]
. As usual,
C^{u}(\mathbb{R})
with
u\in\mathbb{N}
collects all (complex-valued)
continuous functions on
\mathbb{R}
having continuous bounded
derivatives up to order
u
. Let

(7.1)

\psi_{F}\in C^{u}(\mathbb{R}),\quad\psi_{M}\in C^{u}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad u\in\mathbb{N},

be real compactly supported Daubechies wavelets with

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{M}(x)\,x^{v}\,dx=0\quad\text{for all
$v\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$ with $v<u$.}

Recall that
\psi_{F}
is called the
scaling function
(
father wavelet
)
and
\psi_{M}
the
associated wavelet
(
mother wavelet
). The extension
of these wavelets from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d},\,d\geq 2
, is based on the usual tensor procedure. Let

G=(G_{1},\ldots,G_{n})\in G^{0}=\{F,M\}^{d},

which means that
G_{r}
is either
F
or
M
. Let

G=(G_{1},\ldots,G_{n})\in G^{j}=\{F,M\}^{d*},\qquad j\in\mathbb{N},

which means that
G_{r}
is either
F
or
M
where * indicates that
at least one of the components of
G
must be an
M
. Hence
G^{0}

has
2^{d}
elements, whereas
G^{j}
with
j\in\mathbb{N}
has
2^{d}-1
elements. Let

(7.2)

\Psi^{j}_{G,m}(x)=2^{jd/2}\prod^{n}_{r=1}\psi_{G_{r}}(2^{j}x_{r}-m_{r}),\quad G\in G^{j},\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We shall assume that
\psi_{F}
and

\psi_{M}
in (
7.1
) are normalized with respect to
L_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then the system

\Psi=\Big\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}:\ j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ G\in G^{j},\ m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\Big\}

is an orthonormal basis in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

f=\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\,2^{-jd/2}\,\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

with

(7.3)

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\lambda^{j,G}_{m}(f)=2^{jd/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)\,\Psi^{j}_{G,m}(x)\,dx,

where

2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m}
are uniformly bounded functions (with
respect to
j
and
m
).

Let
\chi_{j,m}
be the characteristic function of the dyadic cube

Q_{j,m}:=2^{-j}m+2^{-j}(0,1)^{d}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with sides of
length
2^{-j}
parallel to the axes of coordinates and
2^{-j}m
as
the lower left corner.

It is well known that, under certain conditions on the smoothness parameter
u
(see (
7.1
)), classical Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin spaces admit characterizations via wavelet decompositions. Next we introduce the related sequence spaces. Let
p,q\in(0,\infty]
and
s,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. The space
b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}
is the collection of all sequences
\lambda=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
with
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j}
and

m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that

(7.4)

\|\lambda\|_{b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}}=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s-d/p)q}(1+j)^{\xi q}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p}\Big)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}<\infty

with the usual modification if
p=\infty
and/or
q=\infty
. In the special case
\xi=0
we simply write
b^{s}_{p,q}
. If
p<\infty
, we also define
f^{s}_{p,q}
via

(7.5)

\|\lambda\|_{f^{s}_{p,q}}=\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{jsq}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

with the usual modification if
q=\infty
.

For
p\in(0,\infty]
, let
\sigma_{p}:=d(\frac{1}{p}-1)_{+}.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
p,q\in(0,\infty]
and
s,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Assume that (
7.1
) holds with

u>\max\{s,\sigma_{p}-s\}.

Then
f\in B^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique, that is, the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by (
7.3
), and the operator

(7.6)

I:f\mapsto(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})

defines an isomorphism from
B^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
p<\infty
and
q<\infty
, then
\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
B^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

The proof of the previous result may be found in
[
Tri08
, Theorem 1.20]
(if
\xi=0
) and
[
Alm05
]
(if
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
).

The corresponding result for
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
reads as follows (cf.
[
Tri08
, Theorem 1.20]
).

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty),\,q\in(0,\infty]
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Assume that (
7.1
) holds with

u>\max\{s,\sigma_{pq}-s\},\qquad\sigma_{pq}=d\Big(\frac{1}{\min\{p,q\}}-1\Big)_{+}.

Then
f\in F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in f^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique, that is, the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by (
7.3
), and the operator
I
in (
7.6
) defines an isomorphism from
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
f^{s}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
q<\infty
then
\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

7.2.
Wavelet characterization of
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

The goal of this section is to provide the wavelet description of the spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. With this in mind, we define the related sequence spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}
as follows.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
p,q,r\in(0,\infty]
and
s,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. The space
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}
is the collection of all

(7.7)

\lambda=\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\in\mathbb{C}:j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad G\in G^{j},\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}

such that

(7.8)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}}=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k\xi r}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s-d/p)q}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p}\Big)^{q/p}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
p=\infty
and/or
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Remark 7.4
.

Setting
q=r
in (
7.8
), it is not hard to see that the sequence spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}
contain as special cases the classical spaces
b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}
(cf. (
7.4
)), that is,

T^{\xi}_{q}b^{s}_{p,q}=b^{s,\xi}_{p,q}.

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
p,q,r\in(0,\infty],s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Assume that (
7.1
) holds with
u>\max\{s,\sigma_{p}-s\}
. Then
f\in T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

(7.9)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique, that is, the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by (
7.3
), and the operator
I
in (
7.6
) defines an isomorphism from
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
p<\infty,q<\infty,r<\infty
, then
\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Remark 7.6
.

Let
q=r
. In light of Remark
7.4
, Theorems
7.1
and
7.5
, we recover Proposition
3.8
.

Proof of Theorem
7.5
.

Let
\xi>0
. By assumptions, we may choose
s_{0}>s
such that
u>\max\{\sigma_{p}-s,s_{0}\}.
Therefore, by Theorem
7.1
, the operator
I
defines an isomorphism between
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(respectively,
B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) and
b^{s}_{p,q}
(respectively,
b^{s_{0}}_{p,q}
). Applying the limiting interpolation method, we derive that

I:(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}\to(b^{s}_{p,q},b^{s_{0}}_{p,q})_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}

is also an isomorphism. Furthermore, by Theorem
4.2
(i), we have

(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}=T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

We make the following claim

(7.10)

(b^{s}_{p,q},b^{s_{0}}_{p,q})_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}=T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}.

Assuming momentarily the validity of such a formula, we would establish that

I:T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}

is an isomorphism. Moreover, the uniqueness of the representation (
7.9
) and its unconditional convergence in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are simple consequences of the embeddings
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Proposition
3.10
(iii)) and
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}\hookrightarrow b^{s}_{p,q}
(cf. (
7.10
) and (
4.7
)) together with the corresponding assertions for classical Besov spaces given in Theorem
7.1
. The unconditionality of
\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}\}
in
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,p,q,r<\infty,
follows easily from (
7.8
).

It remains to prove (
7.10
). We first note that
b^{s}_{p,q}
(respectively,
b^{s_{0}}_{p,q}
) can be (isometrically) identified with the sequence space
\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p}))
(respectively,
\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p}))
) (see (
7.4
)). Since

\displaystyle K(t,\lambda;\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))

\displaystyle\asymp

(7.11)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min(1,2^{j(s_{0}-s)}t)2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

for
t>0
(see (
4.11
)), we can apply Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{(\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}K(2^{-k(s_{0}-s)},\lambda;\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min(1,2^{(j-k)(s_{0}-s)})2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{-k(s_{0}-s)r}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}[2^{j(s_{0}-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt+\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(s-d/p)r}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\|\{\lambda^{k,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt+\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Furthermore, by Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) (recall
\xi>0
)

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k\xi r}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|\lambda\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}}.

Taking into account this estimate, the above computations lead to

\|\lambda\|_{(\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}},

i.e., the desired formula (
7.10
) holds true.

The proof with
\xi<0
can be obtained in a similar fashion, but now applying Theorem
4.2
(ii). In particular, we have to show that

(7.12)

(b^{s_{0}}_{p,q},b^{s}_{p,q})_{(1,\xi-1/r),r}=T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q},

where
s_{0}<s
is chosen so that
u>\max\{\sigma_{p}-s_{0},s\}
. Indeed, it follows from (
7.11
) and Hardy’s inequalities (
B.3
)-(
B.4
) that

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{(\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))_{(1,\xi-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}K(2^{k(s-s_{0})},\lambda;\ell^{s-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})),\ell^{s_{0}-d/p}_{q}(\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})))^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min(1,2^{-(j-k)(s-s_{0})})2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt+\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(s-s_{0})r}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}[2^{j(s_{0}-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt+\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(s-d/p)r}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\|\{\lambda^{k,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k\xi r}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}[2^{j(s-d/p)}\|\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}\|_{\ell_{q}(\ell_{p})}]^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|\lambda\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}}.

Hence (
7.12
) holds true.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

The case
\xi=0
in Theorem
7.5
is more delicate. In fact, the above proof still works in this case and shows that, under the assumptions of Theorem
7.5
,

f\in T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad r<\infty,

if and only if

\|\lambda\|_{T^{*}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}}:=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}2^{j(s-d/p)q}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p}\Big)^{q/p}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{1}{1+k}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty.

Furthermore

\|f\|_{T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{T^{*}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}}.

Note that
T^{*}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}\hookrightarrow T_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}=T_{r}^{0}b^{s}_{p,q}
(cf. (
7.8
)), but the converse embedding fails to be true.

Remark 7.8
.

The method of proof of Theorem
7.5
is flexible enough so that it can be applied to deal with another types of wavelets. For instance, under natural assumptions on the involved parameters, one can obtain characterizations of the spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via Haar wavelets. We shall not record here the construction of Haar wavelet bases and we refer the interested reader to
[
Tri08
, Section 2.5.1]
and
[
Tri10
, Section 2.3]
for further explanations and related literature. Let
\{H^{j}_{G,m}\}
be an orthonormal Haar wavelet basis in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
p,q,r\in(0,\infty],s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Assume that

\max\Big\{d\Big(\frac{1}{p}-1\Big),\frac{1}{p}-1\Big\}<s<\min\Big\{\frac{1}{p},1\Big\}.

Then
f\in T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}H^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique in the sense that the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=2^{jd/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)H^{j}_{G,m}(x)\,dx

and the operator

I:f\mapsto(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})

defines an isomorphism from
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{\xi}_{r}b^{s}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
p<\infty,q<\infty,r<\infty
, then
\{H^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
T^{\xi}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

The proof of this result follows the same lines as the proof of Theorem
7.5
and can be safely left to the interested reader.

7.3.
Wavelet characterization of
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

We first introduce the sequence spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
related to the classical spaces
f^{s}_{p,q}
(cf. (
7.5
)).

Definition 7.10
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty),\,q,r\in(0,\infty],\,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. The space
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
is the collection of all

\lambda=\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\in\mathbb{C}:j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad G\in G^{j},\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}

such that

\|\lambda\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}}=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{jsq}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
