q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Next we show that
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be identified with the sequence spaces
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
via the wavelet isomorphism.

Theorem 7.11
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty),q,r\in(0,\infty],s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Assume that (
7.1
) holds with
u>\max\{s,\sigma_{pq}-s\}
. Then
f\in T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique, that is, the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by (
7.3
), and the operator
I
in (
7.6
) defines an isomorphism from
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
q<\infty,r<\infty
, then
\{\Psi^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Remark 7.12
.

The previous result provides, in particular, a unified approach to the wavelet description of
\mathbf{B}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Proposition
3.8
). These spaces were investigated from different perspectives in
[
CDT16
, Theorem 5.5]
and
[
DHT20
, Theorem 6.5]
, respectively. Next we recall some known characterizations following the notation used there. Let
p\in(1,\infty)
and
q\in(0,\infty]
. Then
f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,\xi>-1/q,
if and only if

\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{\textbf{b}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}}:=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{\xi q}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{2}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

is finite. In this case

(7.13)

\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{\textbf{b}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}}.

On the other hand,
f\in\text{Lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,s>0,\,\xi<-1/q
, if and only if

\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{\text{lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}}:=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{\xi q}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{js2}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{2}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

is finite. In this case

(7.14)

\|f\|_{\text{Lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{\text{lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}}.

It is not hard do check that both spaces
\textbf{b}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}
and
\text{lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}
fit into the scale of truncated
f
-spaces introduced in Definition
7.10
. Specifically

T^{\xi+1/q}_{q}f^{s}_{p,2}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\textbf{b}^{0,\xi}_{p,q}&\text{if}\quad s=0\quad\text{and }\quad\xi>-\frac{1}{q},\\
&\\
\text{lip}^{s,\xi}_{p,q}&\text{if}\quad s>0\quad\text{and}\quad\xi<-\frac{1}{q}.\end{array}\right.

Accordingly, one can combine Theorem
7.11
and Proposition
3.8
to show (
7.13
) and (
7.14
).

Proof of Theorem
7.11
.

We shall only deal with the case
\xi>0
, since the case
\xi<0
can be obtained following similar ideas. Without loss of generality, we may choose
s_{0}>s
such that
u>\max\{s_{0},\sigma_{p,q}-s_{0}\}
. Thus, by Theorem
7.2
, the spaces
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are isomorphic to the sequence spaces
f^{s}_{p,q}
and
f^{s_{0}}_{p,q}
, respectively, and invoking the retraction theorem for interpolation (cf.
[
Tri78
, Theorem 1.2.4, p. 22]
), the spaces
(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}
and
(f^{s}_{p,q},f^{s_{0}}_{p,q})_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}
can be identified. According to Theorem
4.8
, the latter can be rephrased as
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be identified with
(f^{s}_{p,q},f^{s_{0}}_{p,q})_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}
via the wavelet map
I
(cf. (
7.6
)). Next we compute this interpolation space using the well-known fact that
f^{s}_{p,q}
can be identified with a complemented subspace of
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q}))
, where

\|(f_{j,m})\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q}))}:=\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jsq}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|f_{j,m}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Hence the identification of the space
(f^{s}_{p,q},f^{s_{0}}_{p,q})_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}
can be reduced to compute

(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q})),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q})))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r},

see
[
Tri78
, Section 1.17.1, pp. 118-119]
. To estimate the corresponding
K
-functional, recall that (cf. (
4.27
) and (
4.28
))

(7.15)

K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{0}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};A_{1}))\asymp\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K(t,f(x);A_{0},A_{1})^{p}\,dx\bigg)^{1/p}

and

(7.16)

K(t,(\xi_{j});\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q}),\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q}))\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min\{2^{js},t2^{js_{0}}\}\|\xi_{j}\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

Combining (
7.15
) and (
7.16
), we obtain

\displaystyle K(t,(f_{j});L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q})),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q})))

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min\{2^{js},t2^{js_{0}}\}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\right)^{1/p}

and thus

\displaystyle\|(f_{j})\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q})),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q})))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-184.9429pt\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}K(2^{-\nu(s_{0}-s)},(f_{j});L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q})),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q})))^{r}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-184.9429pt\asymp\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[\min\{1,2^{(j-\nu)(s_{0}-s)}\}2^{js}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\right)^{r/p}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-184.9429pt\asymp\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{-\nu(s_{0}-s)r}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\nu}[2^{js_{0}}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\right)^{r/p}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt+\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{j=\nu}^{\infty}[2^{js}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\right)^{r/p}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-184.9429pt=:I+II.

We claim that

(7.17)

I\lesssim II.

Assume momentarily that (
7.17
) is true. Therefore, monotonicity properties imply

\displaystyle\|(f_{j})\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s}_{q}(\ell_{q})),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell^{s_{0}}_{q}(\ell_{q})))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{\infty}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\right)^{1/r}.

Hence the proof will be finished if we show that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{\infty}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

(7.18)

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{2^{\nu+1}-2}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\right)^{1/r}.

It remains to show (
7.17
) and (
7.18
). Concerning the former one, we shall separate two possible cases. Firstly, if
p\geq q
we can apply Minkowski’s inequality so that

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\nu}[2^{js_{0}}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}]^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\,dx\right)^{1/p}\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\nu}2^{js_{0}q}\|f_{j}\|^{q}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))}\right)^{1/q},

which yields

I\leq\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{-\nu(s_{0}-s)r}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\nu}2^{js_{0}q}\|f_{j}\|^{q}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))}\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

It follows from Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) (noting
s_{0}>s
) that

I\lesssim\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu sr}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\|f_{\nu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))}^{r}\right)^{1/r}\leq II.

This completes the proof of (
7.17
) under the assumption
p\geq q
.

The proof of (
7.17
) with
p<q
follows similar ideas as above and thus only a sketchy proof will be provided. We have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{-\nu(s_{0}-s)r}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\nu}2^{js_{0}p}\|f_{j}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))}^{p}\bigg)^{r/p}\right)^{1/r}

and, by (
B.3
),

I\lesssim\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu sr}(1+\nu)^{(\xi-1/r)r}\|f_{j}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))}^{r}\right)^{1/r}\leq II.

To show (
7.18
), we can argue as follows. Using the fact that
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,0<p\leq\infty,
satisfies the
\min\{p,1\}
-triangle inequality (i.e.,
\|f_{1}+f_{2}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{p,1\}}\leq\|f_{1}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{p,1\}}+\|f_{2}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{p,1\}}
) and (
B.4
) (note that
\xi>0
)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{\infty}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{\infty}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg\|_{L_{p/q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\leq\left(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg(\sum_{l=\nu}^{\infty}\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{l}-1}^{2^{l+1}-2}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg\|^{\min\{p/q,1\}}_{L_{p/q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{r/(q\min\{p/q,1\})}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{2^{\nu+1}-2}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg\|_{L_{p/q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt=\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu\xi r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{\nu}-1}^{2^{\nu+1}-2}2^{jsq}\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\ell_{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 7.13
.

A similar comment to Remark
7.7
applies to wavelet characterizations of truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces. To be more precise, under the assumptions of Theorem
7.11
with
r<\infty
,
f\in T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
3.7
)) if and only if
\lambda\in T^{*}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
where

\|\lambda\|_{T^{*}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}}:=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{\infty}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{jsq}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Clearly
T^{*}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}\hookrightarrow T_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}=T^{0}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
(cf. Definition
7.10
), but the converse embedding is no longer true.

Remark 7.14
.

The restriction
p>1
in Theorem
7.11
comes only from Theorem
4.8
. In fact, the method of proof of Theorem
7.11
still works if
p\in(0,\infty)
to show that

(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r},\qquad A\in\{B,F\},

with

\xi>0
and
s_{0}>s
,
coincides with the set of all
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
which admits a wavelet representation

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). Furthermore

\|f\|_{(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,\xi-1/r),r}}\asymp\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}}.

A similar result holds true for
(A^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,\xi-1/r),r}
with
\xi<0
and
s_{0}<s
.

Remark 7.15
.

In the same vein as Remark
7.8
, the method of proof of Theorem
7.11
can be easily adapted to deal with Haar wavelet basis for
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Next we only state the result and leave the proof to the interested reader.

Theorem 7.16
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty),r\in(0,\infty],s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Assume that

(7.19)

\max\bigg\{d\Big(\frac{1}{q}-1\Big),\frac{1}{q}-1,\frac{1}{p}-1\bigg\}<s<\min\bigg\{\frac{1}{p},\frac{1}{q}\bigg\}.

Then
f\in T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}H^{j}_{G,m},\quad(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}

(unconditional convergence being in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
). This representation is unique in the sense that the wavelet coefficients
(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
are given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=2^{jd/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)H^{j}_{G,m}(x)\,dx

and the operator

I:f\mapsto(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})

defines an isomorphism from
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{\xi}_{r}f^{s}_{p,q}
. If, in addition,
r<\infty
, then
\{H^{j}_{G,m}\}
is an unconditional basis in
T^{\xi}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

The assumptions (
7.19
) in the previous result are natural, in the sense that they characterize the unconditionality of Haar wavelet basis in
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
under
p\in(1,\infty)
and
q\in(0,\infty)
. A detailed analysis of several properties of the Haar system in Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces was carried out in
[
SeUl17
,
GSU18
]
.

8.
Lifting property

If
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, then the
lifting operator

I_{\sigma}
is defined by

(8.1)

I_{\sigma}f:=[(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}\widehat{f}]^{\vee},\qquad f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

It is well known that
I_{\sigma}
is a one-to-one mapping from
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

8.1.
Lifting in truncated Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces

A basic result in the theory of function spaces is the so-called lifting property of Besov spaces (cf.
[
Tri83
, Section 2.3.8]
, see also
[
Mou01
, Proposition 1.8]
), which asserts that
I_{\sigma}
acts as an isomorphism from
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
B^{s-\sigma,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

(8.2)

\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{B^{s-\sigma,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Here
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q\in(0,\infty]
. The analogue for
F
-spaces reads as follows. Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R},p\in(0,\infty)
and
q\in(0,\infty]
, then

(8.3)

\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{F^{s-\sigma,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{F^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The next result shows the lifting property for the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
A\in\{B,F\}
. Let
s,\sigma,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q,r\in(0,\infty]\,(p<\infty\text{ if }A=F)
. Then
I_{\sigma}
acts as an isomorphism from
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{b}_{r}A^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

By (
3.1
), (
8.2
) and (
8.3
), we have

\displaystyle\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{I_{\sigma}f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|_{A^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|I_{\sigma}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg]\bigg\|_{A^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|_{A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

∎

Remark 8.2
.

The counterpart of Theorem
8.1
in terms of the periodic liftings

\mathfrak{I}_{\sigma}:f\mapsto\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1+|m|^{2})^{\sigma/2}\widehat{f}(m)e^{im\cdot x},\qquad\sigma\in\mathbb{R},

and the periodic spaces
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
, also holds true.

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
8.1
we are now able to extend the Lizorkin representations (
5.19
) for
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
from
s>0
to
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

Corollary 8.3
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\neq 0
. Then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}\bigg\|\sum_{m\in K_{\nu}}\widehat{f}(m)e^{im\cdot x}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}

where

K_{\nu}=\{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:|m_{i}|<2^{\nu+1},i=1,\ldots,d\}\backslash\{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:|m_{i}|<2^{\nu},i=1,\ldots,d\}

for
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
and
K_{0}=\{(0,\ldots,0)\}
.

Proof.

The case
s>0
was already shown in (
5.19
), so that it only remains to deal with the case
s\leq 0
. Given
s\leq 0
, we choose
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
such that
s>\sigma
and invoke Theorem
8.1
(cf. also Remark
8.2
) to get

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|\mathfrak{I}_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu(s-\sigma)q}\bigg\|\sum_{m\in K_{\nu}}(1+|m|^{2})^{\sigma/2}\widehat{f}(m)e^{im\cdot x}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}\bigg\|\sum_{m\in K_{\nu}}\widehat{f}(m)e^{im\cdot x}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{\frac{r}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

∎

8.2.
Lifting in
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

Unlike
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
8.2
)), the lifting property in the setting of spaces
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a delicate issue, which was already considered in
[
DT18
, Section 13.2]
. There it is shown that, for
p\in(1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty],b>-1/q
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,

(8.4)

I_{\sigma}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to B^{-\sigma,b+1/\max\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(8.5)

I_{\sigma}:B^{\sigma,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, as shown in
[
DT18
, Propositions 13.8 and 13.9]
, these results are sharp in the sense that the additional logarithmic smoothness
1/\max\{2,p,q\}
and
1/\min\{2,p,q\}
can not be improved. In particular, these assertions tell us that classical scale formed by
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
does not yield optimal lifting properties for
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Next we are able to remedy this defect using the new scale of spaces
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. To be more precise, according to Theorem
8.1
(ii) and Proposition
3.8
, we obtain the following

Corollary 8.4

(Lifting in
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R},p\in(1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty]
and
b>-1/q
. Then
I_{\sigma}
acts as an isomorphism from
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{-\sigma}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{-\sigma}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Among other relations, it will be established in Corollary
11.7
below that

B^{-\sigma,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{-\sigma}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{-\sigma,b+1/\max\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

so that both (
8.4
) and (
8.5
) are immediate consequences of the stronger assertion given in Corollary
8.4
.

To the best of our knowledge, lifting assertions for
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
have not been considered so far in the literature. Now we are in a position to give a precise answer via Theorem
8.1
(ii) and Proposition
3.8
. Namely, we obtain the following

Corollary 8.5

(Lifting in
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R},s>0,p\in(1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty]
and
b<-1/q
. Then
I_{\sigma}
acts as an isomorphism from
\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s-\sigma}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s-\sigma}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

8.3.
Sobolev-type characterizations

We start by recalling the well-known characterizations of classical Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces in terms of derivatives.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
A\in\{B,F\}
. Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q\leq\infty\,(p<\infty\text{ if }A=F)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

(8.6)

\|f\|_{A^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{A^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{A^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{A^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

For the proof of this result, we refer to
[
Tri83
, Theorem 2.3.8]
if
b=0
and the general case
b\in\mathbb{R}
may be obtained from
b=0
via standard lifting arguments
[
CaMou04a
, Proposition 3.2]
(see also
[
DT18
, Lemma 12.5]
).

Next we show that Proposition
8.6
can be put into the more general perspective of the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proposition 8.7
.

Let
A\in\{B,F\}
. Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q,r\leq\infty\,(p<\infty\text{ if }A=F)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

In particular, setting
q=r
and
A=B
in Proposition
8.7
one recovers (
8.6
) (cf. Proposition
3.8
).

Proof of Proposition
8.7
.

According to (
3.1
) and Proposition
8.6
, we get

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|D^{\alpha}\bigg(\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg)\bigg\|_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{D^{\alpha}f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg(\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg)\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}}\bigg)^{\vee}\bigg\|^{r}_{A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{T^{b}_{r}A^{s-m}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

∎

Writing down Proposition
8.7
for
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain the following

Corollary 8.8

(Sobolev characterization in terms of
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

In particular, Corollary
8.8
improves some estimates in
[
DRS79
, p. 70]
and
[
DT18
, Theorems 12.6 and 12.11]
involving
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Namely, it was shown there that

f\in B^{m,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\implies D^{\alpha}f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{for all}\quad|\alpha|\leq m.

However, this assertion can now be sharpened to

f\in T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\implies D^{\alpha}f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{for all}\quad|\alpha|\leq m,

and this estimate is in fact optimal. Note that

B^{m,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(see Corollary
11.7
below).

On the other hand, Proposition
8.7
for the special choice
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Proposition
3.8
) gives the following

Corollary 8.9

(Sobolev characterization for
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
b<-1/q
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s-m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s-m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s-m}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

In particular, if
s>m
then

\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}f}{\partial x_{l}^{m}}\bigg\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s-m,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

9.
Duality

Recall the well-known duality property for
A^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
:

(9.1)

(A^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=A^{-s,-b}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for
p,q\in(1,\infty)
and
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
; cf.
[
Tri83
, Section 2.11]
and
[
FL06
, Theorem 3.1.10]
. Furthermore, the limiting case
p=1
can also be incorporated in (
9.1
), as well as
q\in(0,1]
with
A=B
(cf.
[
Pe74
]
).

9.1.
Dual of truncated Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces

The goal of this section is to study the duality properties of the scales of spaces
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
, in the same spirit as (
9.1
) for the classical setting. To make this assertion rigorous, we first observe that

(9.2)

\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and the left-hand side embedding is dense if
p,q,r<\infty
. These follow immediately from Theorems
4.2
and
4.8
and the corresponding assertions (
9.2
) for classical spaces
A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(see, e.g.,
[
Tri83
, Section 2.3.3]
). As a consequence, it makes sense to investigate the duality properties of the spaces
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
within the dual pairing
(\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

Theorem 9.1
.

(i)

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty),r\in(0,\infty),s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

(9.3)

(T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=T^{-b}_{r^{\prime}}B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii)

Let
p,q\in(1,\infty),r\in(0,\infty),s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

(9.4)

(T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=T^{-b}_{r^{\prime}}F^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The proof of Theorem
9.1
relies on the limiting interpolation techniques. In particular, the following characterization of the dual space of a limiting interpolation space will be useful (see
[
CS15
, Theorems 5.6 and 5.8]
and
[
BC18
, Theorem 4.3]
).

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
A_{0},A_{1}
be Banach spaces with
A_{1}
continuously and densely embedded into
A_{0}
. Let
\theta\in\{0,1\},r\in(0,\infty)
and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}b>-\frac{1}{r}&\text{if}\quad\theta=0,\\
&\\
b<-\frac{1}{r}&\text{if}\quad\theta=1.\end{array}\right.

Then

(9.5)

((A_{0},A_{1})_{(\theta,b),r})^{\prime}=(A_{1}^{\prime},A_{0}^{\prime})_{(1-\theta,-b-1/\min\{1,r\}),r^{\prime}}.

Furthermore, if
\theta=0
and
b=-1/r
then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{((A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,-1/r),r})^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\asymp

(9.6)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-1}(1-\log t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}(1+\log(1-\log t))^{-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}K(t,f;A_{1}^{\prime},A_{0}^{\prime})]^{r^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

(i): Assume first
b>0
. According to Theorem
4.2
(i), we have

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

for
s_{0}>s
. Since
B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is densely embedded into
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(noting that
q<\infty
), we can invoke (
9.5
) (with
\theta=0
) and (
9.1
) to establish

\displaystyle(T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(B^{-s_{0}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,-b+1/r-1/\min\{1,r\}),r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=T^{-b}_{r^{\prime}}B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where we have applied Theorem
4.2
(ii) in the last step.

Assume now
b<0
. Let
s_{0}<s
. By Theorem
4.2
(ii), we have

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b-1/r),r},

and thus (
9.5
) (with
\theta=1
) and Theorem
4.2
(i) imply

\displaystyle(T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{-s_{0}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-b+1/r-1/\min\{1,r\}),r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=T^{-b}_{r^{\prime}}B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The proof of (ii) can de done similarly but now invoking Theorem
4.8
. This is left to the reader.
∎

9.2.
Dual of truncated Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces in the limiting case
b=0

This subsection is slightly out of the scope of our study since here we deal with the special limiting case
b=0
and we have to consider another truncated spaces. First, it is convenient to switch from
T_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
, to
T^{*}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
3.5
) and (
3.7
)). Second, recall that (cf. (
3.3
))

\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}<\infty\iff f\equiv 0.

However, the triviality of this functional can be overcome with the help of additional weights. For example, the modified functional

\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-r}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

defines a nontrivial space of distributions. In fact, our next result proves that, in particular, this space is the dual space of
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(recall that
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is defined by (
3.5
)).

Theorem 9.3
.

(i)

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty),r\in(0,\infty)
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\displaystyle(T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg\{f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-r^{\prime}/\min\{1,r\}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{-\nu sq^{\prime}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{r^{\prime}/q^{\prime}}\right)^{1/r^{\prime}}<\infty\bigg\}

(with the usual modification if
r\in(0,1]
).

(ii)

Let
p,q\in(1,\infty),r\in(0,\infty)
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\displaystyle(T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg\{f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-r^{\prime}/\min\{1,r\}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{-\nu sq^{\prime}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{1/q^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r^{\prime}}\right)^{1/r^{\prime}}<\infty\bigg\}

(with the usual modification if
r\in(0,1]
).

Proof.

The proof follows similar ideas as the method of proof of Theorem
9.1
but now relying on the duality formula (
9.6
). According to (
4.22
), we have
T_{r}^{*}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-1/r),r},\,s_{0}>s
, and thus, by (
9.6
) and (
9.1
),
\|f\|_{(T_{r}^{*}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}}
is equivalent to the following interpolation norm

\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-1}(1-\log t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}(1+\log(1-\log t))^{-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}K(t,f)]^{r^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}},

where
K(t,f)=K(t,f;B^{-s_{0}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.
Applying now the methodology developed in the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we have, by (
4.12
) and basic monotonicity properties,

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-1}(1-\log t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}(1+\log(1-\log t))^{-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}K(t,f)]^{r^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-327.20668pt\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}})r^{\prime}}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\frac{r^{\prime}}{\min\{1,r\}}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{-\nu s}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\frac{r^{\prime}}{q^{\prime}}}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-327.20668pt\asymp\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\frac{r^{\prime}}{\min\{1,r\}}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}[2^{-\nu s}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\frac{r^{\prime}}{q^{\prime}}}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}.

This proves (i).

Next we deal with (ii). By (
4.36
),

T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=(F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,-1/r),r}

provided that
s_{0}>s
. Then (
9.6
) and (
9.1
) imply

\|f\|_{(T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}}\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{-1}(1-\log t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}(1+\log(1-\log t))^{-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}}}K(t,f)]^{r^{\prime}}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}},

where
K(t,f)=K(t,f;F^{-s_{0}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),F^{-s}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. We can now invoke the retraction method in the same spirit as in the proof of Theorem
4.8
to obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\min\{1,r\}})r^{\prime}}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\frac{r^{\prime}}{\min\{1,r\}}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{j}[2^{-\nu s}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|]^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\bigg\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r^{\prime}}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\frac{r^{\prime}}{\min\{1,r\}}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{-\nu sq^{\prime}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\bigg\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}.

∎

9.3.
Dual spaces of
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

In light of (
9.1
), for
p\in[1,\infty),q\in(0,\infty)
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=B^{0,-b}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

However
B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\neq\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and thus it becomes a rather natural question to characterize the dual space
(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}
. Indeed, this question was first addressed in
[
CD15b
]
dealing with the case
q\geq 1
and later extended in
[
BC18
]
to cover also the case
q<1
. Next we recall these results.

Theorem 9.4

(
[
BC18
, Theorem 5.2]
and
[
CD15b
, Theorem 4.3]
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q<\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then
f\in(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}
if and only if
f\in H^{-1}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
I_{-1}f\in\emph{Lip}^{1,-b-1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Furthermore

\|f\|_{(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}}\asymp\|I_{-1}f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{1,-b-1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The previous result gives an interesting duality connection between
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{1,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, however it is still not satisfactory due to the following reasons. Theorem
9.4
is formulated in terms of the lifting operator
I_{-1}
(cf. (
8.1
)), but a careful examination of the methodology proposed in
[
CD15b
]
shows that the analogues of Theorem
9.4
in terms of
I_{-s}
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
s>0
also hold true, i.e.,

(9.7)

\|f\|_{(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}}\asymp\|I_{-s}f\|_{\text{Lip}^{s,-b-1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Accordingly,
(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}
is not uniquely determined by
\text{Lip}^{1,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Next we are able to remedy this defect via Theorem
9.1
.

Theorem 9.5

(Dual space of
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q<\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

(\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{0}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{0}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 9.6
.

Note that Theorem
9.4
(or more generally, (
9.7
)) follows immediately from Theorem
9.5
. Indeed, by Theorem
8.1
and Proposition
3.8
, we have

\|f\|_{T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{0}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|I_{-s}f\|_{T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{s}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|I_{-s}f\|_{\text{Lip}^{s,-b-1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

We also apply our method to characterize dual spaces of
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Theorem 9.7

(Dual space of
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q<\infty,s>0
and
b<-1/q
. Then

(\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{-s}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(T^{-b-1/q}_{q^{\prime}}F^{-s}_{p^{\prime},2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\prime}=\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Proof of Theorems
9.5
and
9.7
.

Apply (
9.4
) with
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Proposition
3.8
).
∎

10.
Embeddings between truncated Besov spaces

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
0<p_{i},q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,\,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

(10.1)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if
0<p_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty
and one of the following five conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
and
0<r_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
0<r_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

Proof.

According to Theorem
7.5
the validity of (
10.1
) is equivalent to the corresponding embedding between related sequence spaces introduced in (
7.8
), i.e.,

(10.2)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}.

Sufficient conditions
(i): Let
p_{0}\leq p_{1}
and
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
. We will split the embedding (
10.2
) as (recall (
7.4
))

(10.3)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow b^{s_{0},b_{0}-(1/r_{0}-1/q_{0})_{+}}_{p_{0},r_{0}}\hookrightarrow b^{s_{1},b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/r_{1})_{+}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}.

We start by proving the third embedding in (
10.3
). Let
\lambda=\{\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\}
be a sequence given by (
7.7
). For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we claim that

(10.4)

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

(10.5)

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt2^{k(1/q_{1}-1/r_{1})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1})r_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{r_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}.

Indeed, this estimate is a consequence of the embedding
\ell_{r_{1}}\hookrightarrow\ell_{q_{1}}
if
r_{1}\leq q_{1}
and Hölder’s inequality if
r_{1}>q_{1}
. Hence (cf. (
7.4
))

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}^{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/r_{1})_{+})r_{1}}\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1})r_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{r_{1}/p_{1}}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1})r_{1}}(1+j)^{(b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/r_{1})_{+})r_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{r_{1}/p_{1}}

\displaystyle=\|\lambda\|^{r_{1}}_{b^{s_{1},b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/r_{1})_{+}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}}.

Next we focus our attention to the first embedding in (
10.3
). Indeed, in the same spirit as (
10.4
) we have

\displaystyle 2^{-k(1/r_{0}-1/q_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-d/p_{0})r_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{r_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-d/p_{0})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{q_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}},

which yields

\|\lambda\|_{b^{s_{0},b_{0}-(1/r_{0}-1/q_{0})_{+}}_{p_{0},r_{0}}}\lesssim\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The second embedding in (
10.3
) is well known, in fact a more general assertion holds true

(10.6)

b^{s_{0},\xi}_{p_{0},u}\hookrightarrow b^{s_{1},\eta}_{p_{1},v}

under
p_{0}\leq p_{1},\,s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1},0<u,v\leq\infty
and
\xi,\eta\in\mathbb{R}
. For the sake of completeness, we provide below the short proof of (
10.6
). We have

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{b^{s_{1},\eta}_{p_{1},v}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1})v}(1+j)^{\eta v}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{v/p_{0}}\right)^{1/v}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s_{1}-d/p_{1}-s_{0}+d/p_{0})v}(1+j)^{(\eta-\xi)v}\bigg)^{1/v}\|\lambda\|_{b^{s_{0},\xi}_{p_{0},\infty}}

where the last sum is convergent since
s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1}
. Clearly this implies (
10.6
).

Sufficient conditions
(ii)–(iii): Let
p_{0}\leq p_{1},\,s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
q_{0}\leq q_{1}
. The desired embedding (
10.2
) will be achieved via the splitting

(10.7)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}.

The second embedding in (
10.7
) is clear:

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{1}}{q_{1}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-21.33955pt\leq\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{q_{0}}{p_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{1}}{q_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}.

On the other hand, the first embedding in (
10.7
) follows immediately from
\ell_{r_{0}}\hookrightarrow\ell_{r_{1}}
if
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
and from Hölder’s inequality if

b_{0}>b_{1}
and
r_{0}>r_{1}
.

Sufficient conditions
(iv)-(v): Let
p_{0}\leq p_{1},\,s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
q_{1}<q_{0}
. For each
k\geq 0
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt2^{k(\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}})}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{q_{0}}{p_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}})r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\Big(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{q_{0}}{p_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{1}}{q_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}},

where the last step is a simple application of Hölder’s inequality if
r_{0}>r_{1}
(and thus,
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
) and the fact that
\ell_{r_{0}}\hookrightarrow\ell_{r_{1}}
if
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Necessary condition for
p_{0}\leq p_{1}
: We will show that (
10.2
) implies
p_{0}\leq p_{1}
. Indeed, if
p_{0}>p_{1}
then we let

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}l^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j=0,\quad m=(l,0,\ldots,0),\quad l\in\mathbb{N},\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
\frac{1}{p_{0}}<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{p_{1}}
. The sequence
\lambda=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
satisfies

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}l^{-\varepsilon p_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p_{0}}}<\infty

but

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}l^{-\varepsilon p_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}=\infty.

Necessary condition for
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}\geq s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
: We proceed by contradiction, that is, we assume the validity of (
10.2
) under the assumption
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}<s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
. Let

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(j,0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}<\varepsilon<s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
. Then
\lambda=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\in T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}
since

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}-\varepsilon)q_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{0}}{q_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}2^{2^{k}(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}-\varepsilon)r_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}<\infty.

However, similar computations lead to

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}2^{2^{k}(s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}-\varepsilon)r_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\infty.

Necessary condition for
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}\geq b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
under the assumption
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
: Again we argue by contradiction. We assume that (
10.2
) holds with
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}<b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
. Define

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}j^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(j,0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}<\varepsilon<b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
. Elementary computations lead to

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon q_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{0}}{q_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{0}-\varepsilon+\frac{1}{q_{0}})r_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon q_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{1}}{q_{1}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}-\varepsilon+\frac{1}{q_{1}})r_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\infty.

Necessary condition for
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
under the assumptions
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
r_{1}<r_{0}
: The necessity of
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}\geq b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
was already obtained in the previous case. It remains to show that the limiting case
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}},\,s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
r_{1}<r_{0}
is not admissible in (
10.2
). To prove this, consider

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}(1+j)^{-(b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}})}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(j,0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
\frac{1}{r_{0}}<\beta<\frac{1}{r_{1}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}j^{-(b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}})q_{0}}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta q_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{r_{0}}{q_{0}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\beta r_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}<\infty

and, analogously,

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\beta r_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\infty.

Necessary condition for
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
under the assumption
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
: Assume
b_{0}<b_{1}
. Consider the lacunary sequence given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-2^{k}(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}2^{-k\varepsilon},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(j,0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
b_{0}<\varepsilon<b_{1}
. Therefore

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{0}-\varepsilon)r_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}<\infty

and

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}-\varepsilon)r_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\infty.

Necessary condition for
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under the assumptions
b_{0}=b_{1}
and
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
: Assume that (
10.2
) holds with
r_{0}>r_{1}
. Let

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-2^{k}(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}2^{-kb_{0}}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(j,0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
\frac{1}{r_{0}}<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{r_{1}}
. Therefore

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}b^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{0}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{0}}}<\infty

and

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r_{1}}}=\infty.

This gives the desired contradiction.
∎

10.1.
Embeddings between truncated and classical Besov spaces

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
10.1
and Proposition
3.8
, we obtain sharp relationships between classical Besov spaces
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and the new scale of spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Corollary 10.2
.

Let
0<p_{0},p_{1},q_{0},q_{1},r_{1}\leq\infty,\,s_{0},s_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{0},b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

B^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if
0<p_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty
and one of the following five conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
and
0<q_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
0<q_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

Corollary 10.3
.

Let
0<p_{0},p_{1},q_{0},q_{1},r_{0}\leq\infty,\,s_{0},s_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{0},b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if
0<p_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty
and one of the following five conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
and
0<r_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty,\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
0<r_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
.

Remark 10.4
.

Specializing Corollary
10.2
with
q_{1}=r_{1}
and applying Proposition
3.8
, we recover the well-known characterization for embeddings between Besov spaces of logarithmic smoothness (cf.
[
Leo98
]
and
[
DT18
, Section 6]
). More precisely, let
0<p_{i},q_{i}\leq\infty,\,s_{i},b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
for
i=0,1
, then

B^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if
0<p_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty
and one of the following three conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

Writing down Corollaries
10.2
and
10.3
in the special case
p_{0}=p_{1}
and
s_{0}=s_{1}
, we establish the following embedding result.

Corollary 10.5
.

Let
0<p,q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

(10.8)

B^{s,b-1/r+1/\min\{q,r\}}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b-1/r+1/\max\{q,r\}}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(10.9)

B^{s,b}_{p,\min\{q,r\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b}_{p,\max\{q,r\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 10.6
.

All the parameters in Corollary
10.5
are sharp.

11.
Embeddings between truncated Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
with fixed integrability parameter

Theorem 11.1
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

(11.1)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}>s_{1}
.

(ii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}\geq\max\{p,q_{0}\}
,   and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}\geq\max\{p,q_{0}\},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
,   and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}<\max\{p,q_{0}\},
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q_{0}\}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(v)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}<\max\{p,q_{0}\},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q_{0}\}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
,   and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Theorem 11.2
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}>s_{1}
.

(ii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq\min\{p,q_{1}\}
,   and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq\min\{p,q_{1}\},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
,   and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}>\min\{p,q_{1}\},
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{\min\{p,q_{1}\}}
.

(v)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}>\min\{p,q_{1}\},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{\min\{p,q_{1}\}}
,   and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Proof of Theorem
11.1
.

Sufficient conditions:
We claim that the following embedding is true

(11.2)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Indeed, using that
\ell_{q}\hookrightarrow\ell_{p}
if
q\leq p
and Minkowski’s inequality if
q>p
, we have

\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s\max\{p,q\}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|^{\max\{p,q\}}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{1/\max\{p,q\}}\lesssim\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Consequently, (
11.2
) is obtained.

Under one of the conditions stated in (i)–(v), it follows from Theorem
10.1
(with
p_{0}=p_{1}=p
) that

(11.3)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p,\max\{p,q_{0}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Combining (
11.2
) and (
11.3
), we achieve
T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Necessary conditions
s_{0}\geq s_{1}
;
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
under
s_{0}=s_{1}
;
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s_{0}=s_{1}
and
b_{0}=b_{1}
:
Assume (
11.1
) holds, or equivalently (cf. Theorems
7.5
and
7.11
),

(11.4)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}.

Let
\beta=(\beta_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
be a scalar-valued sequence and define
\lambda=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\beta_{k},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Simple computations show

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}2^{2^{k}(s_{1}-d/p)r_{1}}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}

and

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}2^{2^{k}(s_{0}-d/p)r_{0}}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}},

thus (
11.4
) implies

(11.5)

\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}2^{2^{k}(s_{1}-d/p)r_{1}}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}2^{2^{k}(s_{0}-d/p)r_{0}}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

For every
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we set
\beta
with
\beta_{N}=1
and
\beta_{k}=0
if
k\neq N
. According to (
11.5
),

2^{Nb_{1}}2^{2^{N}s_{1}}\lesssim 2^{Nb_{0}}2^{2^{N}s_{0}},\qquad N\in\mathbb{N}.

Therefore
s_{0}\geq s_{1}
and
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
provided that
s_{0}=s_{1}
. On the other hand, if
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
and
b=b_{0}=b_{1}
, then letting
\beta_{k}=2^{-kb}2^{-2^{k}(s-d/p)}\xi_{k}
in (
11.5
), we arrive at
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Necessary condition
b_{0}+1/p\geq b_{1}+1/q_{1}
under
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
:
For every
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we let

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s-d/p)},&\quad j\in\{2^{N}-1,\cdots,2^{N+1}-2\},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Clearly

(11.6)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s}_{p,q_{1}}}\asymp 2^{N(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}.

On the other hand

(11.7)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s}_{p,q_{0}}}=2^{Nb_{0}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{2^{N+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Furthermore, we claim that, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(11.8)

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{2^{N+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{2^{N+1}-2}2^{jd}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/p}.

Indeed, this becomes obvious if
x\not\in Q_{2^{N}-1,(0,\ldots,0)}
, so that the only non-trivial case to check occurs if
x\in Q_{2^{N}-1,(0,\ldots,0)}
. In this case, we denote by
j_{x}\in\{2^{N}-1,\cdots,2^{N+1}-3\}
such that
x\in Q_{j_{x},(0,\ldots,0)}
but
x\not\in Q_{j_{x}+1,(0,\ldots,0)}
and we set
j_{x}=2^{N+1}-2
if
x\in Q_{2^{N+1}-2,(0,\ldots,0)}
. Accordingly

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{2^{N+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{j_{x}}2^{jdq_{0}/p}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp 2^{j_{x}d/p}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{j_{x}}2^{jd}\bigg)^{1/p}=\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{N}-1}^{2^{N+1}-2}2^{jd}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/p}.

Inserting (
13.8
) into (
11.7
), we get

(11.9)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s}_{p,q_{0}}}\asymp 2^{N(b_{0}+1/p)}.

As a combination of (
11.4
), (
11.6
) and (
11.9
),

2^{N(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}\lesssim 2^{N(b_{0}+1/p)},\qquad N\in\mathbb{N}.

This implies
b_{1}+1/q_{1}\leq b_{0}+1/p
.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
and
b_{0}+1/p=b_{1}+1/q_{1}
:
We shall proceed by contradiction, i.e., we assume the validity of (
11.4
) with
r_{0}>r_{1}
and we will arrive at a contradiction. Indeed, consider the sequence
\lambda
given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s-d/p)}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
1/r_{0}<\varepsilon<1/r_{1}
. Then

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s}_{p,q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}=\infty

and, applying a similar reasoning as in (
13.8
), we have

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s}_{p,q_{0}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})p}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\varepsilon p}\bigg)^{r_{0}/p}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}2^{-k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})r_{0}}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{0}}2^{kr_{0}/p}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}<\infty.

This yields the desired contradiction.

Necessary conditions
b_{0}+1/q_{0}\geq b_{1}+1/q_{1}
under
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
:
Let
(b_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
be a scalar-valued sequence and consider the related lacunary Fourier series given by

f(x)\sim\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}b_{j}e^{i(2^{j}-2)x_{1}}\psi(x),\qquad x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\backslash\{0\}
is fixed. It is plain to check that

(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)=b_{j}e^{i(2^{j}-2)x_{1}}\psi(x)

for
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Therefore

(11.10)

\|f\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{0}}|b_{\nu}|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{r_{0}/q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

and

(11.11)

\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{1}}|b_{\nu}|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{r_{1}/q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}.

It follows from (
11.4
), (
11.10
) and (
11.11
) that

(11.12)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{1}}|b_{\nu}|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{r_{1}/q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{0}}|b_{\nu}|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{r_{0}/q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

Evaluating this inequality for the sequences
b^{N}=(b_{\nu}^{N})_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},\,N\in\mathbb{N}_{0},
defined by

b_{\nu}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-\nu s},&\quad\nu\in\{2^{N}-1,\ldots,2^{N+1}-2\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

we obtain

2^{N(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}\lesssim 2^{N(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}.

Hence
b_{1}+1/q_{1}\leq b_{0}+1/q_{0}
.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
and
b_{0}+1/q_{0}=b_{1}+1/q_{1}
:
Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
an arbitrary sequence. We apply the method constructed above (cf. (
11.12
)) for the special sequence
b_{\nu}=2^{-\nu s}2^{-j(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}\lambda_{j}
for
\nu\in\{2^{j}-1,\ldots,2^{j+1}-2\}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Thus,

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

which yields
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
11.2
.

The proof follows the same lines as the proof of Theorem
11.1
.
∎

As an immediate consequence of Theorems
11.1
and
11.2
, we obtain (see also (
3.18
))

Corollary 11.3
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,
and
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,\min\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 11.4
.

The exponents
\min\{p,q\}
and
\max\{p,q\}
in previous embeddings are sharp.

11.1.
Embeddings between truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces and classical Besov spaces with fixed integrability parameter

In Section
10.1
we have investigated the relations between truncated and classical Besov spaces. Now we turn our attention to the corresponding question for truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces and classical Besov spaces. More precisely, letting
r_{1}=q_{1}
and
r_{0}=q_{0}
in Theorems
11.1
and
11.2
, respectively, we get the following

Corollary 11.5
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}>s_{1}
.

(ii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}\geq\max\{p,q_{0}\}
,   and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}\geq\max\{p,q_{0}\},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
,   and
r_{0}\leq q_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}<\max\{p,q_{0}\},
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q_{0}\}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(v)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{1}<\max\{p,q_{0}\},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q_{0}\}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
,   and
r_{0}\leq q_{1}
.

Corollary 11.6
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,\,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

B^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following five conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}>s_{1}
.

(ii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq\min\{p,q_{1}\}
,   and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq\min\{p,q_{1}\},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
,   and
q_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}>\min\{p,q_{1}\},
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{\min\{p,q_{1}\}}
.

(v)

s_{0}=s_{1},\quad q_{0}>\min\{p,q_{1}\},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{\min\{p,q_{1}\}}
,   and
q_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Letting
s_{0}=s_{1}
in Corollaries
11.5
and
11.6
, we arrive at the analogue of Corollary
10.5
for truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.

Corollary 11.7
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

B^{s,b-1/r+1/\min\{p,q,r\}}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b-1/r+1/\max\{p,q,r\}}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

B^{s,b}_{p,\min\{p,q,r\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b}_{p,\max\{p,q,r\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 11.8
.

All the parameters in Corollary
11.7
are sharp.

11.2.
Embeddings for
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces

Let us show how embeddings from Section
11.1
can be applied to sharpen and extend embedding theorems for
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
available in the literature.

Recall the embeddings between Lipschitz and Besov spaces obtained in
[
DHT20
, Theorem 4.1]
(see also (
2.12
)): Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
b<-1/q
. Then

(11.13)

B^{s,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b+1/\max\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(11.14)

B^{s,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{2,p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b+1/q}_{p,\max\{2,p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

These embeddings are, in general, not comparable and they are optimal within the classical scale formed by
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, cf.
[
DHT20
, Remark 4.2]
.

Let us recall embedding theorems related to
\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
2.11
)): If
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
, then

(11.15)

B^{0,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{0,b+1/\max\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, these embeddings are sharp within the classical scale of
B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces, cf.
[
DT18
, Section 9.1]
.

Since (cf. Proposition
3.8
)

(11.16)

\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\text{ and }B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=B^{s,b}_{p,q,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

the embeddings (
11.13
)–(
11.15
) are contained in Corollary
11.7
. However, these embeddings admits non-trivial improvements in terms of truncated Besov spaces via Corollary
11.3
.

Corollary 11.9

(Embeddings between
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
, and
b<-1/q
. Then

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s}_{p,\max\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Corollary 11.10

(Embeddings between
\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
)
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\emph{\textbf{B}}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\max\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 11.11
.

The embeddings given in Corollaries
11.9
and
11.10
improves (
11.13
)–(
11.15
). Indeed, note that (cf. Corollary
10.5
)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s}_{p,\max\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s,b+1/\max\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap B^{s,b+1/q}_{p,\max\{2,p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

B^{s,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+B^{s,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{2,p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, all the parameters involved in the embeddings given in Corollaries
11.9
and
11.10
are optimal.

12.
Embeddings between truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

Theorem 12.1
.

Let
0<p_{0}\leq p_{1}<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty,s_{i}\in\mathbb{R},
and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

(12.1)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}<p_{1},
and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}<p_{1},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
,   and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}=p_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq q_{1},
and
b_{0}>b_{1}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}=p_{1},\quad q_{0}\leq q_{1},\quad b_{0}=b_{1}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(vi)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}=p_{1},\quad q_{1}<q_{0},
and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(vii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad p_{0}=p_{1},\quad q_{1}<q_{0},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Before we give the proof of Theorem
12.1
, we write down some of its immediate consequences. Namely, since (cf. Proposition
3.8
)

(12.2)

\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

as a special case of Theorem
12.1
we derive sharp embeddings between Lipschitz spaces.

Corollary 12.2

(Embeddings between Lipschitz spaces)
.

Let
1<p_{0}\leq p_{1}<\infty,0<q_{i}\leq\infty,s_{i}>0
and
b_{i}<-1/q_{i}
for
i=0,1
. Then

\emph{Lip}^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\emph{Lip}^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
,   and
b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}>b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}=b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
q_{0}\leq q_{1}
.

This result extends
[
DHT20
, Theorem 5.1]
where the proposed methodology restricts to the case
p_{0}=p_{1}
. See also
[
DHT20
, Theorem 5.3]
. The limiting cases
p_{0}=1
and/or
p_{1}=\infty
in Corollary
12.2
are also of great interest since they are related to the classical Lipschitz space
\text{Lip}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and the space
\text{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
formed by bounded variation functions; these cases were already investigated in detail in
[
DHT20
, Theorems 5.5, 5.7, 5.9 and 5.10]
.

Another consequence of Theorem
12.1
comes from the combination of (cf. Proposition
3.8
)

\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and (
12.2
). To be more precise, we can establish the following

Corollary 12.3

(Embeddings between Lipschitz spaces and Besov spaces of smoothness near zero)
.

Let
1<p_{0}\leq p_{1}<\infty,0<q_{i}\leq\infty,s_{0}>0,b_{0}<-1/q_{0}
and
b_{1}>-1/q_{1}
. Then

\emph{Lip}^{s_{0},b_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>-\frac{d}{p_{1}}.

Proof of Theorem
12.1
.

Sufficient condition
(i): Let
s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1}
. According to Corollary
11.3
and Theorem
10.1
, we have

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},\max\{p_{0},q_{0}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},\min\{p_{1},q_{1}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Sufficient conditions
(ii)-(iii): Assume
s_{0}-d/p_{0}=s_{1}-d/p_{1}
and
p_{0}<p_{1}
(in particular,
s_{0}>s_{1}
). We will show that, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(12.3)

\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{1}q_{1}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg\|\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

To prove (
12.3
), we make use of the following well-known inequality (see, e.g.,
[
BL76
, Theorem 5.6.1]
): let
\theta\in(0,1),q_{1}\in(0,\infty]
and
s_{1}=(1-\theta)s_{2}+\theta s_{0}
, then

\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}2^{\nu s_{1}q_{1}}|a_{\nu}|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{\nu s_{0}}|a_{\nu}|\bigg)^{\theta}\bigg(\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{\nu s_{2}}|a_{\nu}|\bigg)^{1-\theta}

for every complex-valued sequence
(a_{\nu})_{\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Consequently, given any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the previous inequality with
\theta=p_{0}/p_{1}
gives

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{1}q_{1}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\bigg(\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{2}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|\bigg)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}

(12.4)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\leq\bigg(\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{2}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}.

Furthermore, by the classical Nikolskii’s inequality for entire functions of exponential type (cf.
[
N51
,
N75
]
), we have

\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 2^{\nu d/p_{0}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and thus (noting that
s_{2}=s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}
)

\displaystyle\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{2}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(12.5)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}q_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Putting together (
12.4
) and (
12.5
),

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{1}q_{1}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\bigg(\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|\bigg)^{\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}\bigg\|\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}q_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1-\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}}}

and integrating on both sides of the previous estimate, we arrive at (
12.3
).

It follows from (
12.3
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{1}q_{1}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{1}}\right)^{1/r_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg\|\sup_{2^{j}-1\leq\nu\leq 2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{1}}\right)^{1/r_{1}}

(12.6)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}q_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{1}}\right)^{1/r_{1}}.
