On the other hand, it is plain to see that

(12.7)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}|a_{j}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{0}r_{0}}|a_{j}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

holds provided that either

b_{0}>b_{1}
or
b_{0}=b_{1}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

By (
12.6
) and (
12.7
), we get

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}q_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{0}}\right)^{1/r_{0}}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Sufficient conditions
(iv)-(vii): Let
p=p_{0}=p_{1}
and
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
. We have

\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{1}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. This is clear from the embedding
\ell_{q_{0}}\hookrightarrow\ell_{q_{1}}
if
q_{0}\leq q_{1}
and from Hölder’s inequality if
q_{1}<q_{0}
. Hence

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s}_{p,q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+})r_{1}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu sq_{0}}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s}_{p,q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where we have applied (
12.7
) in the last estimate.

Necessary condition
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}\geq s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
:
Assume (
12.1
) holds or equivalently, by Theorem
7.11
, working at the sequence level

(12.8)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}f^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}.

For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define the sequence
\lambda_{k}=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k})
given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad j=2^{k},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then

\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp 2^{kb_{0}}2^{2^{k}(s_{0}-d/p_{0})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}f^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp 2^{kb_{1}}2^{2^{k}(s_{1}-d/p_{1})}.

The validity of (
12.8
) implies that either
s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1}
or
s_{0}-d/p_{0}=s_{1}-d/p_{1}
and
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{0}=b_{1}
:
Let
\beta=(\beta_{k})
any scalar-valued sequence and define the related sequence

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-2^{k}(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}2^{-kb_{0}}\beta_{k},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Basic computations yield

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp\|\beta\|_{\ell_{r_{0}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}f^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\|\beta\|_{\ell_{r_{1}}}.

Therefore, by (
12.8
), we conclude
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Necessary condition
b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}\leq b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}
under
s=s_{0}=s_{1}
and
p=p_{0}=p_{1}
:
We let
\lambda=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})
where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-js_{0}}\beta_{k},&\quad j\in\{2^{k}-1,\cdots,2^{k+1}-2\},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Here
\beta=(\beta_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
denotes any scalar-valued sequence to be chosen.

Then

(12.9)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s}_{p,q_{0}}}=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

We can estimate

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(Q_{2^{k+1}-2,(0,\ldots,0)})}

\displaystyle\asymp 2^{k/q_{0}}2^{-2^{k+1}d/p}.

Conversely,

\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 2^{k/q_{0}}\|\chi_{2^{k}-1,(0,\ldots,0)}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp 2^{k/q_{0}}2^{-2^{k}d/p}.

Inserting these estimates into (
12.9
), we obtain

(12.10)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s}_{p,q_{0}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{0}+1/q_{0})r_{0}}2^{-2^{k}dr_{0}/p}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

Analogously,

(12.11)

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}f^{s}_{p,q_{1}}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})r_{1}}2^{-2^{k}dr_{1}/p}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}.

It follows from (
12.8
), (
12.10
) and (
12.11
), that

(12.12)

\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})r_{1}}2^{-2^{k}dr_{1}/p}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{0}+1/q_{0})r_{0}}2^{-2^{k}dr_{0}/p}|\beta_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

For an arbitrary
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we define

\beta_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad k\in\{0,\cdots,N\},\\
0,&k>N.\end{array}\right.

By (
12.12
), we get

2^{N(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}\lesssim 2^{N(b_{0}+1/q_{0})}

which yields
b_{1}+1/q_{1}\leq b_{0}+1/q_{0}
.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}},\,s=s_{0}=s_{1}
and
p=p_{0}=p_{1}
:
Given any sequence
(\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, applying (
12.12
) related to
(\beta_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=(2^{2^{k}d/p}2^{-k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}\xi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, we obtain

\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|\xi_{k}|^{r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|\xi_{k}|^{r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}.

This implies
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

∎

13.
Franke–Jawerth embeddings for truncated spaces

We start by recalling the classical Franke–Jawerth embeddings
[
Ja77
,
Fra86
]
(see also
[
Ma87
]
,
[
SiTri95
]
and
[
V08
]
). Let
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}\leq\infty,\,-\infty<s_{1}<s<s_{0}<\infty
with

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s-\frac{d}{p}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}.

Let
0<q,q_{0},q_{1}\leq\infty
. Then

(13.1)

B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if

q_{0}\leq p\leq q_{1}.

Theorem 13.1
.

Let
0<p_{0}<p_{1}\leq\infty,-\infty<s_{1}<s_{0}<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty
and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

(13.2)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<p_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad 0<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{1}<b_{0}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<p_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad 0<q_{0}\leq\infty,\quad b_{1}=b_{0}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<p_{0},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq\infty
and
b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}<b_{0}+\frac{1}{p_{0}}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{1}<p_{0},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq\infty,\quad b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=b_{0}+\frac{1}{p_{0}}
and
0<r_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

Theorem 13.2
.

Let
0<p_{0}<p_{1}\leq\infty,-\infty<s_{1}<s_{0}<\infty,0<q_{i},r_{i}\leq\infty
and
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
for
i=0,1
. Then

(13.3)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
.

(ii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty,\quad 0<q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{1}<b_{0}
.

(iii)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<q_{0}\leq p_{1}\leq\infty,\quad 0<q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad b_{1}=b_{0}
and
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

(iv)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<p_{1}<q_{0},\quad 0<q_{1}\leq\infty
and
b_{1}+\frac{1}{p_{1}}<b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}
.

(v)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}},\quad 0<p_{1}<q_{0},\quad 0<q_{1}\leq\infty,\quad b_{1}+\frac{1}{p_{1}}=b_{0}+\frac{1}{q_{0}}
and
0<r_{0}\leq r_{1}\leq\infty
.

Remark 13.3
.

Let
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
0<q_{0}\leq\infty
. Conditions (ii) and (iv) in Theorem
13.1
can be rewritten as

\quad b_{0}-b_{1}>\Big(\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}}\Big)_{+},

while conditions (iii) and (v) read as

b_{0}-b_{1}=\Big(\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}}\Big)_{+}\qquad\text{and}\qquad r_{0}\leq r_{1}.

A similar comment applies to Theorem
13.2
but now in terms of
(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}})_{+}
.

Proof of Theorem
13.1
.

Sufficient condition
(i): According to Theorems
10.1
and Corollary
11.3
,

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},\max\{p_{0},q_{0}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Sufficient conditions
(ii)–(v): Under these assumptions, it follows from Theorem
12.1
that

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

and thus (
13.2
) will be shown once the following

(13.4)

T^{b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

is established.

For
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, consider

f_{j}(x):=\sup_{\nu=2^{j}-1,\cdots,2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s_{0}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\,G\in G^{\nu}}|\lambda^{\nu,G}_{m}(f)|\chi_{\nu,m}(x).

Clearly

2^{\nu s_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{\nu}}|\lambda^{\nu,G}_{m}(f)|\leq\inf_{x\in Q_{\nu,m}}f_{j}(x)

for
\nu\in\{2^{j}-1,\cdots,2^{j+1}-2\}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{\nu}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{\nu,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0}-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\inf_{x\in Q_{\nu,m}}f_{j}(x)^{p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}

(13.5)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0}-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{-\nu d}l)^{p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}=:I_{j}.

As usual,
f^{*}
denotes the non-increasing rearrangement of
f
, cf. (
17.19
) below for precise definition.

To estimate
I_{j}
, we can make use of elementary monotonicity properties together with a simple change of variables in order to get

I_{j}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0})q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-\nu}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{1}}2^{\mu d}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}

and, since
p_{0}<p_{1}
,

I_{j}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0})q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-\nu}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{0}}2^{\mu dp_{0}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}.

Furthermore, we claim that

I_{j}\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0})p_{0}}\sum_{\mu=-\nu}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{0}}2^{\mu dp_{0}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}.

Indeed, this follows easily from
\ell_{p_{0}}\hookrightarrow\ell_{q_{1}}
if
q_{1}\geq p_{0}
and from Hölder’s inequality if
q_{1}<p_{0}
. Changing the order of summation and taking into account that
s_{0}>s_{1}
, we derive

\displaystyle I_{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-2^{j+1}+2}^{-2^{j}+1}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{0}}2^{\mu dp_{0}/p_{1}}\sum_{\nu=-\mu}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0})p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-2^{j}+1}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{0}}2^{\mu dp_{0}/p_{1}}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-s_{0})p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-2^{j+1}+2}^{\infty}f_{j}^{*}(2^{\mu d})^{p_{0}}2^{\mu p_{0}(d/p_{1}-s_{1}+s_{0})}\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\|f_{j}\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Inserting this into (
13.5
), we achieve

\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s_{1}-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{\nu}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{\nu,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\lesssim 2^{j(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}\|f_{j}\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Accordingly, by Theorem
7.5
(noting that
b_{1}\neq 0
),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s-d/p_{1})q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{\nu}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{\nu,G}_{m}|^{p_{1}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{1}}\bigg)^{r_{1}/q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+})r_{1}}\|f_{j}\|^{r_{1}}_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}=\|f\|_{T_{r_{1}}^{b_{1}+(1/q_{1}-1/p_{0})_{+}}F^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

This proves the desired embedding (
13.4
).

Necessary conditions
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}>s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{0}\geq b_{1}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
:
Assume that (
13.2
) holds, or equivalently (cf. Theorems
7.5
and
7.11
),

(13.6)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}.

For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define the sequence
\lambda_{k}=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k})
given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad j=2^{k},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then

\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp 2^{2^{k}(s_{1}-d/p_{1})}2^{kb_{1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp 2^{2^{k}(s_{0}-d/p_{0})}2^{kb_{0}}.

It follows from (
13.6
) that one of the following conditions is satisfied

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1},\\
&\\
s_{0}-d/p_{0}=s_{1}-d/p_{1}&\text{and}\quad b_{0}\geq b_{1}.\end{array}\right.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{0}=b_{1}
:
Let
\beta=(\beta_{k})
any scalar-valued sequence and define the related sequence

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-2^{k}(s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}})}2^{-kb_{0}}\beta_{k},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Elementary computations show that

\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp\|\beta\|_{\ell_{r_{1}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|(\lambda^{j,G}_{m})\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp\|\beta\|_{\ell_{r_{0}}}.

Since (
13.6
) holds, we conclude that necessarily
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
.

Necessary condition
b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}\leq b_{0}+\frac{1}{p_{0}}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
:
For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
\lambda_{k}=(\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k})
where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m,k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}})},&\quad j\in\{2^{k}-1,\cdots,2^{k+1}-2\},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then

\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\asymp 2^{k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}.

On the other hand

(13.7)

\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}=2^{kb_{0}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p_{0}}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Furthermore, we claim that, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(13.8)

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p_{0}}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jd}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}.

Indeed, this becomes obvious if
x\not\in Q_{2^{k}-1,(0,\ldots,0)}
, so that the only non-trivial case to check occurs if
x\in Q_{2^{k}-1,(0,\ldots,0)}
. In this case, we denote by
j_{x}\in\{2^{k}-1,\cdots,2^{k+1}-3\}
such that
x\in Q_{j_{x},(0,\ldots,0)}
but
x\not\in Q_{j_{x}+1,(0,\ldots,0)}
and we set
j_{x}=2^{k+1}-2
if
x\in Q_{2^{k+1}-2,(0,\ldots,0)}
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p_{0}}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{j_{x}}2^{jdq_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp 2^{j_{x}d/p_{0}}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{j_{x}}2^{jd}\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}=\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jd}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}.

Plugging (
13.8
) into (
13.7
) we arrive at

\|\lambda_{k}\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}\asymp 2^{k(b_{0}+1/p_{0})}.

Then the validity of (
13.6
) implies, in particular,

2^{k(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}\lesssim 2^{k(b_{0}+1/p_{0})},\qquad k\in\mathbb{N},

and thus
b_{1}+1/q_{1}\leq b_{0}+1/p_{0}
.

Necessary condition
r_{0}\leq r_{1}
under
s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
b_{1}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=b_{0}+\frac{1}{p_{0}}:
We shall proceed by contradiction, i.e., we shall prove that (
13.6
) with
r_{0}>r_{1}
is not true via a counterexample. Let

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-j(s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}})}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})}(1+\log(j+1))^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
1/r_{0}<\varepsilon<1/r_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}b^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{1}r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})q_{1}}(1+\log(j+1))^{-\varepsilon q_{1}}\bigg)^{r_{1}/q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}

(13.9)

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}=\infty.

Furthermore, similarly as in (
13.8
), one can show that, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jdq_{0}/p_{0}}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})q_{0}}(1+\log(j+1))^{-\varepsilon q_{0}}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-284.52756pt\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{jd}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})p_{0}}(1+\log(j+1))^{-\varepsilon p_{0}}\chi_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}(x)\bigg)^{1/p_{0}}

which implies

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}f^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kb_{0}r_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-(b_{1}+1/q_{1})p_{0}}(1+\log(j+1))^{-\varepsilon p_{0}}\bigg)^{r_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{0}}<\infty.

However, this contradicts (
13.9
) if (
13.6
) holds.
∎

Proof of Theorem
13.2
.

Sufficient condition
(i): It follows from Theorems
10.1
and Corollary
11.3
that

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},\min\{p_{1},q_{1}\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Sufficient conditions
(ii)–(v): To establish (
13.3
) under one of the assumptions given in (ii)–(v), it will be enough to show that

(13.10)

T^{b_{1}+(1/p_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

provided that
q_{1}\leq\min\{p_{0},p_{1},q_{0}\}
; see Theorems
10.1
and
12.1
.

Assume
q_{1}\leq\min\{p_{0},p_{1},q_{0}\}
and denote by
\alpha,\beta
and
\gamma
the dual exponents of
p_{0}/q_{1},p_{1}/q_{1}
and
q_{0}/q_{1}
, respectively, i.e.,

\frac{q_{1}}{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{\alpha}=1,\qquad\frac{q_{1}}{q_{0}}+\frac{1}{\gamma}=1,\qquad\frac{q_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{\beta}=1.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we can apply basic properties of rearrangements (cf.
[
BS88
, Chapter 2, Theorems 3.4 and 4.6]
) to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q_{1}}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q_{1}}\chi_{j,m}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}/q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1/q_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\leq\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q_{1}}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{*}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}/q_{1}}(0,\infty)}^{1/q_{1}}

(13.11)

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt=\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{q_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{j,l}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}/q_{1}}(0,\infty)}^{1/q_{1}}

where, for each
j\in\{2^{k}-1,\cdots,2^{k+1}-2\}
and
G\in G^{j}
, the sequence
(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})_{l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
denotes the non-increasing rearrangement of
(|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|)_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{j,l}
is the characteristic function relative to the interval
[2^{-jd}l,2^{-jd}(l+1))
.

By duality,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{q_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{j,l}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}/q_{1}}(0,\infty)}

\displaystyle=\sup\int_{0}^{\infty}g(x)\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{q_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{j,l}(x)\bigg)\,dx

(13.12)

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt=\sup\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{q_{1}}2^{j(s_{1}q_{1}-d)}g_{j,l}

where the supremum runs over all non-increasing and non-negative functions
g\in L_{\beta}(0,\infty)
with
\|g\|_{L_{\beta}(0,\infty)}\leq 1
and
g_{j,l}:=2^{jd}\int_{0}^{\infty}g(x)\tilde{\chi}_{j,l}(x)\,dx
. For any
g
satisfying these assumptions, we can apply Hölder’s inequality with exponents
\alpha
and
\gamma
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{q_{1}}2^{j(s_{1}q_{1}-d)}g_{j,l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{1}q_{1}-d)}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\tilde{\lambda}^{j,G}_{l})^{p_{0}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/p_{0}}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\alpha}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-d/p_{0})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}^{\infty}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\bigg)^{q_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{q_{1}/q_{0}}

(13.13)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\times\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd\gamma/\beta}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\gamma/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}.

Next we show

(13.14)

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd\gamma/\beta}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\gamma/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\lesssim 2^{k(1/q_{0}-1/p_{1})_{+}q_{1}}.

To be more precise, we will prove that

(13.15)

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd\gamma/\beta}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\gamma/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\lesssim 2^{k(1/q_{0}-1/p_{1})_{+}q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}

and

(13.16)

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}\lesssim 1.

Clearly (
13.15
) and (
13.16
) imply (
13.14
).

The proof of (
13.16
) follows from monotonicity properties of
g
. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\bigg(2^{jd}\int_{2^{-jd}l}^{2^{-jd}(l+1)}g(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=2^{\mu d}-1}^{2^{(\mu+1)d}-2}\bigg(2^{jd}\int_{2^{-jd}l}^{2^{-jd}(l+1)}g(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mu=0}^{\infty}2^{\mu d}\bigg(2^{jd}\int_{2^{-jd}(2^{\mu d}-1)}^{2^{-jd}2^{\mu d}}g(x)\,dx\bigg)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mu=0}^{\infty}2^{\mu d}g^{**}(2^{(\mu-j)d})^{\alpha}

and since
p_{0}<p_{1}
(or equivalently,
\beta<\alpha
)

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{\mu=0}^{\infty}2^{\mu d}g^{**}(2^{(\mu-j)d})^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd(1-\beta/\alpha)}\sum_{\mu=-j}^{\infty}2^{\mu d\beta/\alpha}g^{**}(2^{\mu d})^{\beta}\bigg)^{1/\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-\infty}^{\infty}2^{\mu d\beta/\alpha}g^{**}(2^{\mu d})^{\beta}\sum_{j=-\infty}^{-\mu}2^{-jd(1-\beta/\alpha)}\bigg)^{1/\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{\mu=-\infty}^{\infty}2^{\mu d}g^{**}(2^{\mu d})^{\beta}\bigg)^{1/\beta}\asymp\|g^{**}\|_{L_{\beta}(0,\infty)}\asymp\|g\|_{L_{\beta}(0,\infty)}\leq 1.

To deal with (
13.15
), we shall distinguish two possible cases. Firstly, if
p_{1}\geq q_{0}
(or equivalently,
\gamma\geq\beta
) then

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd\gamma/\beta}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\gamma/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}.

Secondly, under the assumption
p_{1}<q_{0}
(or equivalently,
\gamma<\beta
) we can apply Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd\gamma/\beta}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\gamma/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\lesssim 2^{k(1/p_{1}-1/q_{0})q_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}g_{j,l}^{\alpha}\bigg)^{\beta/\alpha}\bigg)^{1/\beta}.

As a combination of (
13.11
)–(
13.14
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}2^{js_{1}q_{1}}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{q_{1}}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}\bigg\|_{L_{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt2^{k(1/p_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-d/p_{0})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}^{\infty}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\bigg)^{q_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}

and thus, by Theorems
7.11
and
7.5
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}F^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b_{1}+(1/p_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+})r_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{j(s_{0}-d/p_{0})q_{0}}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}^{\infty}|\lambda^{j,G}_{m}|^{p_{0}}\bigg)^{q_{0}/p_{0}}\bigg)^{r_{1}/q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b_{1}+(1/p_{1}-1/q_{0})_{+}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The counterexamples provided in the proof of Theorem
13.1
also show the optimality of the conditions stated in (i)–(v). Further details are left to the reader.
∎

13.1.
Franke–Jawerth embeddings for
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

The characterization of Franke–Jawerth embeddings for Lipschitz spaces obtained in
[
DHT20
, Theorem 4.4 and Remark 4.5]
reads as follows: Let
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}\leq\infty,1<p<\infty,0<q,u\leq\infty,s>0,b<-1/q
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

(13.17)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}},b+\xi}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff\xi\leq\frac{1}{\max\{p,q\}},\\
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}},b+\frac{1}{q}}_{p_{1},u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff u\geq\max\{p,q\},\\
B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}},b+\xi}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff\xi\geq\frac{1}{\min\{p,q\}},\\
B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}},b+\frac{1}{q}}_{p_{0},u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff u\leq\min\{p,q\}.\end{array}\right.

Since
T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{q}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, these embeddings are now an immediate consequence of Theorems
13.1
and
13.2
. Despite the fact that (
13.17
) is optimal within the scale of classical Besov spaces, our next result shows that it admits improvements in terms of the new scale of truncated Besov spaces.

Corollary 13.4
.

Let
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}\leq\infty,1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,
and
b<-1/q
. Then

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}}}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}}}_{p_{1},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 13.5
.

According to Corollary
10.5
,

(13.18)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}}}_{p_{1},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}},b+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q\}}}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}},b+\frac{1}{q}}_{p_{1},\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

(13.19)

B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}},b+\frac{1}{\min\{p,q\}}}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}},b+\frac{1}{q}}_{p_{0},\min\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}}}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Therefore Corollary
13.4
sharpens (
13.17
).

Remark 13.6
.

All the parameters in Corollary
13.4
are sharp.

Concerning Franke–Jawerth embeddings for
\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, it was obtained in
[
DT18
, Theorem 3.6, Propositions 9.7 and 9.8]
that if
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}\leq\infty,1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,b>-1/q
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, then

(13.20)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}},b+\xi}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff\xi\leq\frac{1}{\max\{p,q\}},\\
B^{-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}},b+\xi}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\textbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})&\iff\xi\geq\frac{1}{\min\{p,q\}}.\end{array}\right.

The counterpart of Corollary
13.4
for
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(recall that
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) reads as follows.

Corollary 13.7
.

Let
0<p_{0}<p<p_{1}\leq\infty,1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,
and
b>-1/q
. Then

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{0}}}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{-\frac{d}{p}+\frac{d}{p_{1}}}_{p_{1},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 13.8
.

The embeddings given in Corollary
13.7
improve (
13.20
) via (
13.18
) and (
13.19
) with
s=0
.

Remark 13.9
.

All the parameters in Corollary
13.7
are sharp.

14.
Embeddings in the space of locally integrable functions

As usual,
L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
stands for the space of functions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
which are integrable on any bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. This space is interpreted as the set of all regular distributions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

A central question working with spaces of distributions is to characterize when they contain only regular distributions. In the setting of classical spaces
A^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),A\in\{B,F\},
this question has been completely settled in
[
SiTri95
]
and
[
CaLeo13
]
(see also
[
CaFar06
]
). For convenience of the reader, these results will be recalled in Section
14.1
. Then in Sections
14.2
and
14.3
we turn our attention to the new scales of spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, respectively.

14.1.
Embeddings for classical Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

Theorem 14.1

(
[
CaLeo13
, Theorem 4.3]
)
.

Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
0<p,q\leq\infty
. Then

B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\emph{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions holds

(i)

0<p\leq\infty,\qquad s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(ii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq 1,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(iii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 1<q\leq\infty,\qquad b>1-\frac{1}{q}
,

(iv)

1<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq\min\{p,2\},\qquad b\geq 0
,

(v)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad p<q\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(vi)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
.

Theorem 14.2

(
[
CaLeo13
, Theorem 4.4]
)
.

Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R},0<p<\infty
and
0<q\leq\infty
. Then

F^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\emph{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions holds

(i)

0<p<\infty,\qquad s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(ii)

0<p<1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(iii)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(iv)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
.

Dealing with embeddings for
A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
(i.e.,
b=0
in Theorems
14.1
and
14.2
), we refer to
[
SiTri95
, Theorem 3.3.2]
.

14.2.
Embeddings for
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

Theorem 14.3
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q,r\leq\infty
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{\emph{loc}}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions holds

(i)

0<p\leq\infty,\qquad s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},\qquad 0<q,r\leq\infty,\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
,

(ii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq 1,\qquad 0<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(iii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 1<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq 1,\qquad b\geq 1-\frac{1}{q}
,

(iv)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 1<q\leq\infty,\qquad 1<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>1-\frac{1}{q}
,

(v)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq p,\qquad 0<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(vi)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad p<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq p,\qquad b\geq\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(vii)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad p<q\leq\infty,\qquad p<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(viii)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad 0<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(ix)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq 2,\qquad b\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(x)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad 2<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
.

Remark 14.4
.

(a): Setting
q=r
in Theorem
14.3
one recovers Theorem
14.1
for
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,b\neq 0
(cf. Proposition
3.8
).

(b): The conditions (v)–(x) (i.e.,
1<p<\infty,s=0
and
b\neq 0
) given in Theorem
14.3
can be summarized as

b\geq\frac{1}{\min\{2,p,q\}}-\frac{1}{q}\qquad\text{if}\qquad r\leq\min\{2,p,q\}

and

b>\frac{1}{\min\{2,p,q\}}-\frac{1}{q}\qquad\text{if}\qquad r>\min\{2,p,q\}.

The conditions (ii)–(iv) (i.e.,
0<p\leq 1,s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)
and
b\neq 0
) can be rewritten as

b\geq\frac{1}{\min\{1,q\}}-\frac{1}{q}\qquad\text{if}\qquad r\leq 1

and

b>\frac{1}{\min\{1,q\}}-\frac{1}{q}\qquad\text{if}\qquad r>1.

Proof of Theorem
14.3
.

Sufficiency part:
(i): Let
s_{0}\in\big(d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},s\big)
. According to Corollary
10.3
one has
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{0},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(which still holds true in the limiting case
b=0
) and thus
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Theorem
14.1
).

(ii): Assume
0<p\leq 1,s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)
and
0<q\leq 1
. It follows from the triangle inequality and the Nikolskii inequality for entire functions of exponential type that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(14.1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Since
q\leq 1
one can estimate the last term by

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

We distinguish now two possible cases. If
r\leq 1
and
b\geq 0
then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

On the other hand, if
1<r<\infty
and
b>0
, we can apply Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The case
r=\infty
and
b>0
is even easier and we omit the proof.

(iii), (iv): Let
0<p\leq 1,s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)
and
q>1
. Applying Hölder’s inequality in (
14.1
),

(14.2)

\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(1-1/q)}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|^{q}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{1/q}

(with the obvious modification if
q=\infty
). Assume first
r\leq 1
and
b\geq 1-1/q
(i.e., (iii) holds). Then, by (
14.2
),

\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|^{q}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Suppose now that
1<r<\infty
and
b>1-1/q
(i.e., (iv) holds). An application of Hölder’s inequality in (
14.2
) yields

\|f\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d(1/p-1)q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|^{q}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The case
r=\infty
and
b>1-1/q
can be done similarly.

(v): Let
1<p\leq 2,s=0,
and
q\leq p
. In virtue of the Littlewood–Paley theorem

(14.3)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(\cdot)|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad 1<p<\infty,

we obtain, by Fubini’s theorem,

(14.4)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\bigg)^{1/p},\qquad 1<p\leq 2.

Since
q\leq p
, by (
14.4
),

(14.5)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\bigg)^{1/p}.

If
r\leq p
and
b\geq 0
then

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

On the other hand, if
p<r<\infty
and
b>0
, it follows from (
14.5
) and Hölder’s inequality that

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Standard modifications work for the case
r=\infty
and
b>0
.

(vi), (vii): Assume
1<p\leq 2,s=0
and
p<q\leq\infty
. According to (
14.4
) and Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\bigg)^{1/p}

(14.6)

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(1/p-1/q)p}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{p/q}\bigg)^{1/p}

(where the usual interpretation is made if
q=\infty
).

If
r\leq p
and
b\geq 1/p-1/q
then, by (
14.6
),

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

On the other hand, if
p<r\leq\infty
and
b>1/p-1/q
, the desired estimate follows from (
14.6
) and Hölder’s inequality.

(viii): Under
2<p<\infty
and
s=0
: by the Littlewood–Paley theorem (cf. (
14.3
)) and Minkowski’s inequality

(14.7)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Further, if
q\leq 2
then

(14.8)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{2/q}\right)^{1/2}.

Assume that one of the following conditions is satisfied

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}r\leq 2\quad\text{and}\quad b\geq 0,&\\
2<r\leq\infty\quad\text{and}\quad b>0.\end{array}\right.

Therefore the right-hand side in (
14.8
) can be dominated by
\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

(ix), (x): Let
2<p<\infty,s=0,
and
2<q\leq\infty
. By (
14.7
) and Hölder’s inequality

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(1/2-1/q)2}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{2/q}\right)^{1/2}.

Furthermore, the last term can be dominated by
\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
: if
r\leq 2
(i.e., (ix) holds) we use the fact that
\ell_{r}\hookrightarrow\ell_{2}
and if
r>2
(i.e., (x) holds) then we apply Hölder’s inequality.

The limiting case
p=\infty
in (viii)–(x) is an immediate consequence of the embeddings

(14.9)

T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{0}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

B^{0}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(cf. Theorem
14.1
). The proof of (
14.9
) can be derived by using similar techniques as above and it is left to the interested reader.

Necessary part:
We first deal with the embedding

(14.10)

T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad 1<p<\infty.

Necessary condition
b\geq 1/p-1/q
in (
14.10
) with
p<q
:
Assume that (
14.10
) holds with

(14.11)

b<1/p-1/q

(cf. (vi)). Consider

(14.12)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

where the coefficients
\lambda^{j,G}_{m}
are to be chosen. According to (
14.11
) (recall also
p<q
) there exists
\beta
such that

(14.13)

1<\beta<p^{\prime}\min\{1-b-1/q,1-1/q\}.

Let

\kappa_{j}=\sum_{l=0}^{j}(1+l)^{-\beta}.

Observe that

\kappa=\lim_{j\to\infty}\kappa_{j}<\infty

since
\beta>1
(cf. (
14.13
)) and
\kappa_{j}<\kappa_{j+1}
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Define, for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

(14.14)

R_{j}:=\{x=(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d}):\kappa_{j-1}\leq x_{1}<\kappa_{j},\quad 0\leq x_{l}\leq 1,\quad l=2,\ldots,d\}.

We now subdivide
R_{j}
into dyadic cubes
Q_{j,m},\,m\in M_{j}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d},
of side length
2^{-j}
. Note that

(14.15)

|M_{j}|=2^{j(d-1)}[2^{j}(\kappa_{j}-\kappa_{j-1})]\asymp 2^{jd}(1+j)^{-\beta}.

Define

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{\beta/p-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N},\quad m\in M_{j},\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where

(14.16)

\max\{b+1/q,1/q\}<\varepsilon<-\beta/p^{\prime}+1

(cf. (
14.13
)). Invoking Theorem
7.5
and (
14.15
),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{-jdq/p}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon q+\beta q/p}|M_{j}|^{q/p}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

where we have used (
14.16
) in the last step. By construction,
f
is compactly supported on
[0,\kappa]\times[0,1]^{d-1}
. Furthermore

\|f\|_{L^{1}([0,\kappa]\times[0,1]^{d-1})}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{R_{j}}|f(x)|\,dx\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\beta+\beta/p-\varepsilon}=\infty

where the divergence of the last sum follows from (
14.16
). This gives the desired counterexample.

Necessary condition
b>1/p-1/q
in (
14.10
) with
p<\min\{q,r\}
:
According to the previous case, it is enough to consider the limiting value
b=1/p-1/q
. We take
\beta
and
\varepsilon
such that

1<\beta<p^{\prime}\Big(1-\frac{1}{r}\Big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\beta-1<\varepsilon<\frac{\beta}{p}-\frac{1}{r}.

We modify the preceding counterexample as follows. Define

\kappa_{j}=\sum_{l=0}^{j}(\log(1+l))^{-\beta}\frac{1}{1+l}.

Note that the sequence
\{\kappa_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is increasing and convergent (since
\beta>1
). Let
\kappa=\lim_{j\to\infty}\kappa_{j}
. The corresponding sets
R_{j}
are given by (
14.14
) with

|M_{j}|\asymp 2^{jd}(\log(1+j))^{-\beta}\frac{1}{1+j}.

Consider the function
f
given by (
14.12
) where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(\log(1+j))^{\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N},\quad m\in M_{j},\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Applying Theorem
7.5
we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-q/p}(\log(1+j))^{\varepsilon q-\beta q/p}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{\varepsilon r-\beta r/p}2^{k(b+1/q-1/p)r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

where we have used
b=1/p-1/q
and
\varepsilon<\beta/p-1/r
. However
f\not\in L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since (recall that
\beta-1<\varepsilon
)

\|f\|_{L^{1}([0,\kappa]\times[0,1]^{d-1})}\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\log(1+j))^{\varepsilon-\beta}\frac{1}{1+j}=\infty.

Necessary condition
b\geq 1/2-1/q
in (
14.10
):
Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a fixed function, non-zero everywhere, satisfying

(14.17)

\text{supp }\widehat{\psi}\subset\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|\xi|\leq 2\}

and let
f
be the function whose Fourier series is lacunary of the form

(14.18)

f(x)\sim\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}e^{i(2^{j}-2)x_{1}}\psi(x),\qquad x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is a given sequence of complex numbers. It is plain to see that
(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(x)=\lambda_{j}e^{i(2^{j}-2)x_{1}}\psi(x)
. Accordingly

(14.19)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|\psi\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}|\lambda_{\nu}|^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

On the other hand, by Zygmund’s theorem for lacunary Fourier series (see, e.g.,
[
Gra08
, Theorem 3.7.4]
)

(14.20)

\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Assume that (
14.10
) holds with

b+1/q<1/2.

Then the Fourier series
f
(cf. (
14.18
)) where

\lambda_{j}=j^{-\varepsilon},\qquad j\geq 3,

and
b+1/q<\varepsilon<1/2
satisfies (cf. (
14.19
))

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\asymp\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}<\infty

but (cf. (
14.20
))

\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})}^{2}\asymp\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}j^{-2\varepsilon}=\infty.

This yields the desired counterexample.

Necessary condition
b>1/2-1/q
under
2<\min\{q,r\}
in (
14.10
):
According to the preceding case, it only remains to show that (
14.10
) does not hold whenever
b=1/2-1/q
and
2<\min\{q,r\}
. In this situation, the counterexample is provided by the lacunary Fourier series (
14.18
) with

\lambda_{j}=j^{-1/2}(\log j)^{-\varepsilon},\qquad j\geq 3,

where
1/r<\varepsilon<1/2
. Indeed, by (
14.19
),
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
but
f\not\in L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})
(cf. (
14.20
)).

Next we focus on

(14.21)

T^{b}_{r}B^{d(1/p-1)}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad 0<p\leq 1.

Necessary condition
b\geq 1-1/q
in (
14.21
) with
q>1
:
We shall proceed by contradiction, i.e., we assume that (
14.21
) holds with
b<1-1/q
. Let

(14.22)

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{jd}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
\max\{1/q,b+1/q\}<\varepsilon<1
. Then, by Theorem
7.5
, the function
f
given by (
14.12
) and (
14.22
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{d(1/p-1)}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon q}\bigg)^{r/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}<\infty.

Furthermore

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,1]^{d})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\backslash Q_{j+1,(0,\ldots,0)}}|f(x)|\,dx

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{j}2^{kd}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon}\bigg)|Q_{j,(0,\ldots,0)}\backslash Q_{j+1,(0,\ldots,0)}|

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}=\infty.

This contradicts (
14.21
).

Necessary condition
b>1-1/q
in (
14.21
) with
\min\{q,r\}>1
:
According to the previous case, it only remains to construct an extremal function in the limiting case
b=1-1/q
. Let
f
be given by (
14.12
) with

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{jd}(1+j)^{-1}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
1/r<\beta<1
. In light of Theorem
7.5
, since
q>1
and
\beta>1/r
we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{d(1/p-1)}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-q}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta q}\bigg)^{r/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\beta r}<\infty.

However,
f\not\in L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,1]^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jd}\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{j}2^{kd}(1+k)^{-1}(1+\log(1+k))^{-\beta}\bigg)

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+\log(1+k))^{-\beta}\frac{1}{1+j}=\infty

where we have used that
\beta<1
in the last step. This yields the desired counterexample to (
14.21
).
∎

Remark 14.5
.

A careful inspection of the above proof shows that restriction
b\neq 0
in Theorem
14.3
is only applied in necessary assertions (cf. Remark
7.7
). To be more precise: Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
0<p,q,r\leq\infty
. Then

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

provided that one of the following conditions holds

(i)

0<p\leq\infty,\qquad s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},\qquad 0<q,r\leq\infty,\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(ii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq 1,\qquad 0<r\leq 1,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(iii)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq 1,\qquad 1<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(iv)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 1<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq 1,\qquad b\geq 1-\frac{1}{q}
,

(v)

0<p\leq 1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 1<q\leq\infty,\qquad 1<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>1-\frac{1}{q}
,

(vi)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq p,\qquad 0<r\leq p,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(vii)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq p,\qquad p<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(viii)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad p<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq p,\qquad b\geq\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(ix)

1<p\leq 2,\qquad s=0,\qquad p<q\leq\infty,\qquad p<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(x)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad 0<r\leq 2,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(xi)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad 2<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(xii)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq 2,\qquad b\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(xiii)

2<p\leq\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad 2<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
.

As a byproduct of Theorem
14.3
we obtain the following

Corollary 14.6
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q,r\leq\infty
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
.

(i)

Assume
p<\infty
. The following statements are equivalent

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{\emph{loc}}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\max\{1,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii)

The following statements are equivalent

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{\emph{loc}}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and (cf.
[
Tri83
, p. 37]
)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow bmo(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Remark 14.7
.

The analogue of Corollary
14.6
for the classical spaces
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
was obtained in
[
SiTri95
, Corollary 3.3.1]
(see also
[
CaLeo13
, Corollary 4.6]
where Besov spaces of generalized smoothness are treated).

Proof of Corollary
14.6
.

(i): It is obvious that the embedding into
L_{\max\{1,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies the corresponding one into
L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Concerning the converse implication, i.e., if
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then, by Theorem
14.3
, one of the conditions (i)–(x) given in Theorem
14.3
is satisfied. A perusal of the proof of Theorem
14.3
shows that, under such an assumption,
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\max\{1,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii): It follows from the proof of Theorem
14.3
that
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
implies
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if
s>0
and
T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{0}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
14.9
)). Hence the desired result is a consequence of the well-known facts that
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow bmo(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
B^{0}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow bmo(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf.
[
SiTri95
, p. 114]
).
∎

14.3.
Embeddings for
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

Theorem 14.8
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R},b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\},0<p<\infty,
and
0<q,r\leq\infty
. Then

(14.23)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{\emph{loc}}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

provided that one of the following conditions holds

(i)

0<p<\infty,\qquad s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},\qquad 0<q,r\leq\infty,\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(ii)

0<p<1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq 1,\qquad b\geq 0
,

(iii)

0<p<1,\qquad s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big),\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad 1<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(iv)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad 0<r\leq\min\{2,p\},\qquad b\geq 0
,

(v)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 0<q\leq 2,\qquad\min\{2,p\}<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
,

(vi)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad 0<r\leq\min\{2,p\},\qquad b\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(vii)

1\leq p<\infty,\qquad s=0,\qquad 2<q\leq\infty,\qquad\min\{2,p\}<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
.

Furthermore, the conditions
(i)–(vi)
are necessary. If, in addition,
r>2
then the condition
(vii)
is also necessary.

Proof.

Sufficiency part:
(i): Assume
0<p<\infty
and
s>d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+}
. Let
s_{0}\in\big(d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)_{+},s\big)
. Therefore, by Theorems
11.1
and
14.3
,

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii), (iii): Let
0<p<1
and
s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)
. In virtue of Theorems
13.1
and Remark
14.5
, under assumptions given in (ii) and (iii), we have

T^{b}_{r}F^{d(\frac{1}{p}-1)}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{1,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(iv)–(vii): We concern with
p=1
and
s=0
. According to Corollary
11.7
, we have

T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{1,\max\{1,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Therefore the desired result can be reduced to study the validity of
T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{1,\max\{1,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Accordingly, this embedding holds true provided that one of the conditions (iv)
–(vii) is satisfied.

(iv)–(vii): Assume
1\leq p<\infty
. By the (quasi-)triangle inequality in
L_{p/2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg\|_{L_{p/2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{1/2}

(14.24)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}.

Furthermore, we claim

(14.25)

\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Assuming momentarily the validity of (
14.25
), it follows from (
14.24
) that
T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, which in turn gives
T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Theorem
14.2
).

Next we show (
14.25
). Suppose first
0<q\leq 2,0<r\leq\min\{2,p\}
and
b\geq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Secondly, assume
0<q\leq 2,\min\{2,p\}<r\leq\infty
and
b>0
. By Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Thirdly, assume
2<q\leq\infty,0<r\leq\min\{2,p\}
and
b\geq 1/2-1/q
. By Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(1/2-1/q)\min\{2,p\}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\leq\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Fourthly, if
2<q\leq\infty,\min\{2,p\}<r\leq\infty
and
b>1/2-1/q
. Applying twice Hölder’s inequality, the following is achieved

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(1/2-1/q)\min\{2,p\}}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\min\{2,p\}}\bigg)^{1/\min\{2,p\}}

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|(\varphi_{j}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Necessary condition
b>0
if
0<p\leq 1
and
s=d\big(\frac{1}{p}-1\big)
in (
14.23
):
Given
0<p_{0}<p
, by Theorem
13.2
, we have

T^{b}_{r}B^{d(1/p_{0}-1)}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{d(1/p-1)}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and thus (
14.23
) yields

T^{b}_{r}B^{d(1/p_{0}-1)}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{loc}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

This implies
b>0
(cf. Theorem
14.3
).

Necessary condition
b>0
if
1<p<\infty
and
s=0
in (
14.23
):
We shall proceed by contradiction, i.e., we assume (
14.23
) holds for some
b<0
. Then choose
\beta
such that

(14.26)

1<\beta<-bp^{\prime}+1.

Let

\kappa_{j}=\sum_{l=0}^{j}(1+j)^{-\beta}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\kappa=\lim_{j\to\infty}\kappa_{j}.

and consider the rectangles
R_{j}
defined by (
14.14
). For each
j
, we denote by
\{Q_{j,m}:m\in M_{j}\}
the collection of all (dyadic) cubes of side length
2^{-j}
contained in
R_{j}
. Recall that
|M_{j}|\asymp 2^{jd}(1+j)^{-\beta}
(cf. (
14.15
)). Let

(14.27)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\eta_{j},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N},\quad m\in M_{j},\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

with the scalar-valued sequence
\{\eta_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
to be chosen.

According to Theorem
7.11
and using the disjointness of supports of dyadic cubes with fixed side length, as well as the disjointness of the
R_{j}
’s, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{m\in M_{j}}|\lambda^{j,(M,\ldots,M)}_{m}|^{q}\chi_{j,m}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg\|\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}\sum_{m\in M_{j}}|\lambda^{j,(M,\ldots,M)}_{m}|\chi_{j,m}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|\eta_{j}|^{p}2^{-jd}|M_{j}|\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}

(14.28)

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\beta/p)r}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}|\eta_{j}|^{p}\bigg)^{r/p}\bigg)^{1/r}.

On the other hand

(14.29)

\|f\|_{L^{1}([0,\kappa]\times[0,1]^{d-1})}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{R_{j}}|f(x)|\,dx\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}|\eta_{j}|(1+j)^{-\beta}.

Set

\eta_{j}=(1+j)^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{where}\qquad\beta-1<\varepsilon<-b-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{\beta}{p}.

Here we recall that
\beta<-bp^{\prime}+1
(cf. (
14.26
)). In light of (
14.28
) and (
14.29
), we derive

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\beta/p+\varepsilon+1/p)r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

and

\|f\|_{L^{1}([0,\kappa]\times[0,1]^{d-1})}\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(1+j)^{\varepsilon-\beta}=\infty.

This gives the desired counterexample.

Necessary condition
b\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
if
2<q,\,1\leq p<\infty,
and
s=0
in (
14.23
):
We shall construct extremal functions showing that (
14.23
) is no longer true provided that
b<1/2-1/q
. Indeed, consider the lacunary Fourier series

(14.30)

f(x)\sim\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}e^{i(2^{j}-2)x_{1}}\psi(x),\qquad x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of complex numbers to be chosen and
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\backslash\{0\}
satisfies (
14.17
). Elementary computations lead to

(14.31)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{k=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}|\lambda_{k}|^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

On the other hand, by (
14.20
),

(14.32)

\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}.

Let
\lambda_{j}=(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}
where
b+1/q<\varepsilon<1/2
. It follows from (
14.31
) and (
14.32
) that

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

but

\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon 2}\bigg)^{1/2}=\infty.

Necessary condition
b>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q}
if
2<\min\{q,r\},\,1\leq p<\infty,
and
s=0
in (
14.23
):
According to the previous case, it only remains to deal with the limiting value
b=1/2-1/q
. Let
\lambda_{j}=(1+j)^{-1/2}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta}
where
1/r<\beta<1/2
and the related Fourier series
f
given by (
14.30
). It follows from (
14.31
) and (
14.20
) that

\|f\|_{T^{1/2-1/q}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}j^{-\beta r}\bigg)^{1/r}<\infty

and

\|f\|_{L_{1}([0,2\pi]^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}(\log j)^{-2\beta}\frac{1}{j}\bigg)^{1/2}=\infty.

This shows
T^{1/2-1/q}_{r}F^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\not\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

∎

The analog of Corollary
14.6
for
F
-spaces reads as follows.

Corollary 14.9
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R},0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
and
0<r\leq\infty
(in case the parameters satisfy condition
(vii)
given in Theorem
14.8
we assume that
r>2
). Then the following statements are equivalent

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{\text{\emph{loc}}}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\max\{1,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

15.
Embeddings in the space of continuous functions

Let
C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be the space of all complex-valued uniformly continuous functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, equipped with the usual sup-norm. It is a well-known result that

(15.1)

B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}0<p\leq\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad-\infty<b<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{d}{p},\\
0<p\leq\infty,\quad 0<q\leq 1,\quad b\geq 0,\quad\text{and}\quad s=\frac{d}{p},\\
0<p\leq\infty,\quad 1<q\leq\infty,\quad b>\frac{1}{q^{\prime}},\quad\text{and}\quad s=\frac{d}{p}.\end{array}\right.

The analogue for
F
-spaces reads as follows

(15.2)

F^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}0<p<\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad-\infty<b<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{d}{p},\\
1<p<\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad b>\frac{1}{p^{\prime}},\quad\text{and}\quad s=\frac{d}{p},\\
0<p\leq 1,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad b\geq 0,\quad\text{and}\quad s=\frac{d}{p}.\end{array}\right.

For the proof of these results, we refer to
[
SiTri95
, Theorem 3.3.1]
if
b=0
,
[
K81
]
and
[
CaMou04b
, Proposition 3.13, Example 3.14]
for
b\in\mathbb{R}
(even in the more general setting of function spaces with generalised smoothness).

The goal of this section is to extend (
15.1
)-(
15.2
) into the scale of truncated spaces
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Theorem 15.1
.

Let
p,q,r\in(0,\infty],\,b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

(15.3)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

0<p\leq\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 0<r\leq\infty,
and
s>\frac{d}{p}
.

(ii)

0<p\leq\infty,\quad 0<q\leq 1,\quad 0<r\leq 1,\quad b\geq 0,
and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(iii)

0<p\leq\infty,\quad 1<q\leq\infty,\quad 0<r\leq 1,\quad b\geq\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(iv)

0<p\leq\infty,\quad 0<q\leq 1,\quad 1<r\leq\infty,\quad b>0
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(v)

0<p\leq\infty,\quad 1<q\leq\infty,\quad 1<r\leq\infty,\quad b>\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

The space
C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be replaced by
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in (
15.3
).

Specializing Theorem
15.1
with
q=r
one recovers (
15.1
) (cf. Proposition
3.8
).

Remark 15.2
.

The if-part of Theorem
15.1
works with
b\in\mathbb{R}
. On the other hand, the proof of the corresponding only-if part will rely on certain extremal functions constructed via wavelets (cf. Theorem
7.5
), so that the restriction
b\neq 0
appears naturally in this methodology; see Remark
7.7
.

Remark 15.3
.

Note that the conditions (ii)–(v) in Theorem
15.1
can be unified, for
0<p,q,r\leq\infty
, as

b>1-\frac{1}{\max\{1,q\}}

(where the limiting value
b=1-\frac{1}{\max\{1,q\}}
is also admissible if
r\leq 1
).

Proof of Theorem
15.1
.

Sufficiency part:
(i): Let
s_{0}\in(\frac{d}{p},s)
. By Corollary
10.3
,
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{s_{0},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Thus the desired result follows from the classical embedding
B^{s_{0},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
15.1
)).

Next we deal with the limiting case
s=d/p
. According to the Nikolski
\breve{\text{\char 16\relax}}
inequality for entire functions of exponential type (cf.
[
N75
, p. 126]
and
[
Tri83
, p. 18]
)

\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 2^{\nu d/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Therefore

(15.4)

\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(ii): If
q,r\in(0,1]
and
b\geq 0
then the right-hand side of (
15.4
) can be estimated from above by

\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(iii): Let
q\in(1,\infty],\,r\in(0,1]
and
b\geq 1/q^{\prime}
. By Hölder’s inequality

(15.5)

\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu d/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim 2^{j/q^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
). Inserting this estimate into (
15.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j/q^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}=\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(iv): Let
q\in(0,1],\,r\in(1,\infty]
and
b>0
. Applying Hölder’s inequality with exponent
r
, it follows from (
15.4
) that

\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

This gives the desired embedding.

(v): Let
q,r\in(1,\infty]
and
b>1/q^{\prime}
. By (
15.4
) and (
15.5
), one has

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j/q^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-92.47145pt\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu dq/p}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(b-1/q^{\prime})r^{\prime}}\right)^{1/r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip-92.47145pt\asymp\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Necessary condition
b>0
in (
15.3
) with
r>1
and
s=d/p
:
Consider

(15.6)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-jd/2}\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

where the coefficients
\lambda^{j,G}_{m}
are given by

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
1/r<\varepsilon\leq 1
. By Theorem
7.5
, if
b<0
then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r}<\infty,

but
f\not\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
since

f(0,\ldots,0)\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon}=\infty.

Necessary condition
b\geq 1/q^{\prime}
in (
15.3
) with
q>1
and
s=d/p
:
Assume
b<1/q^{\prime}
and let
\varepsilon
be such that
b+1/q<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Consider
f
given by (
15.6
) with

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Applying Theorem
7.5
, we have

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}<\infty.

However
f
is not continuous since

f(0,\ldots,0)\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}=\infty.

Necessary condition
b>1/q^{\prime}
in (
15.3
) with
q>1,r>1
and
s=d/p
:
In light of the preceding counterexample, it only remains to construct an extremal function in the limiting case
b=1/q^{\prime}
under the additional assumption
r>1
. To do this, we let
\varepsilon
satisfying
1/r<\varepsilon\leq 1
and take
f
defined by (
15.6
) with

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{-1}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

According to Theorem
7.5
,

\|f\|_{T^{1/q^{\prime}}_{r}B^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\asymp\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(1/q^{\prime}-1+1/q)r}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{-\varepsilon r}<\infty,

but

f(0,\ldots,0)\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-1}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\varepsilon}=\infty.

Necessary condition
s\geq d/p
in (
15.3
):
We can proceed by contradiction, i.e., assume that there exists
s<d/p
such that

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

We choose
s_{0}
such that
s<s_{0}<d/p
. It follows from Corollary
10.2
that

B^{s_{0},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

but this contradicts (
15.1
). One can also apply Theorem
7.5
to construct explicit extremal functions showing the necessity of
s\geq d/p
in (
15.3
). Indeed, if
s<d/p
then the function
f
defined by (
15.6
) with

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

satisfies
f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
but
f\not\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

∎

Theorem 15.4
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty),q,r\in(0,\infty],\,b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

(15.7)

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})

if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

0<p<\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 0<r\leq\infty,
and
s>\frac{d}{p}
.

(ii)

0<p\leq 1,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 1<r\leq\infty,\quad b>0,
and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(iii)

0<p\leq 1,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 0<r\leq 1,\quad b\geq 0
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(iv)

1<p<\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 1<r\leq\infty,\quad b>\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

(v)

1<p<\infty,\quad 0<q\leq\infty,\quad 0<r\leq 1,\quad b\geq\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
,  and
s=\frac{d}{p}
.

The space
C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be replaced by
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in (
15.7
).

Proof.

Sufficiency part:
(i): By Corollary
11.3
,

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and thus Theorem
15.1
yields

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for
s>d/p
.

(ii)–(v): Assume
s=d/p
. In virtue of Theorems
13.1
and
15.1
, the following holds

T^{b}_{r}F^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{\infty,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Necessary conditions:
We first deal with the case
s=d/p
, i.e., we assume

(15.8)

T^{b}_{r}F^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Let
0<p_{1}<p
. By Theorem
13.2
, we have

(15.9)

T^{b}_{r}B^{d/p_{1}}_{p_{1},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Putting together (
15.8
) and (
15.9
), we obtain

T^{b}_{r}B^{d/p_{1}}_{p_{1},p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and, by Theorem
15.1
, this implies the validity of one of the conditions stated in (ii)–(v).

It remains to show the failure of the embedding

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad s<\frac{d}{p}.

Indeed, this is an immediate consequence of the fact that
T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
s_{0}>s
(cf. Theorem
13.2
) and Theorem
15.1
.
∎

Remark 15.5
.

The proof above shows that the if-part of Theorem
15.4
still remains true in the borderline case
b=0
; cf. Remark
15.2
.

A special case of Theorem
15.4
refers to embeddings of the Lipschitz spaces
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=F^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{s}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. Proposition
3.8
) into the space of continuous functions.

Corollary 15.6
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
b<-1/q
. Then

\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow C(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff s>\frac{d}{p}

where
C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be replaced by
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

In particular, the above result exhibits another striking difference between
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and the standard Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin scale
A^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, namely, the tuning parameter
b
enables to establish embeddings from
A^{d/p,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
C(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
15.1
) and (
15.2
)), but this never happens in the Lipschitz setting.

16.
Characterizations and embeddings for general monotone functions

16.1.
Definition and basic properties of general monotone functions

We recall the definition of the general monotone functions given in
[
LiT11
,
Ti07
]
. A complex-valued function
\varphi(z),z>0,
is called
general monotone
if it is locally of bounded variation and for some constant
C>1
the following is true

(16.1)

\int_{z}^{2z}|d\varphi(u)|\leq C|\varphi(z)|

for all
z>0
. The set of all general monotone functions is denoted by
GM
. Examples of general monotone functions are:
decreasing functions,
quasi-monotone
functions
\varphi
(i.e,
\varphi(t)t^{-\alpha}
is non-increasing for some
\alpha\geq 0
), and increasing functions
\varphi
such that

\varphi(2z)\lesssim\varphi(z)
. It is readily seen that (
16.1
) implies

(16.2)

|\varphi(u)|\lesssim|\varphi(z)|\quad\text{ for any }\quad z\leq u\leq 2z,

which subsequently gives

(16.3)

|\varphi(z)|\lesssim\int_{z/c}^{\infty}\frac{|\varphi(u)|}{u}du,\qquad c>1.

For later use we recall the following lemma on multipliers of general monotone functions (see
[
LiT11
, Remark 5.5]
).

Lemma 16.1
.

Let
\varphi,\alpha\in GM
, then
\varphi\alpha\in GM
.
