Now we are in a position to give the main definition in this section.
First, we recall that
the Fourier transform of a radial function
f(x)=f_{0}(|x|)
is also radial,
\widehat{f}(\xi)=F_{0}(|\xi|)

(see, e.g.,
[
Gra08
, Appendix B5]
) and it can be written as the Fourier–Hankel transform

(16.4)

F_{0}(s)=\frac{2\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)}\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{0}(t)j_{d/2-1}(st)t^{d-1}\,dt,

where
j_{\alpha}(t)=\Gamma(\alpha+1)(t/2)^{-\alpha}J_{\alpha}(t)
is the
normalized Bessel function (
j_{\alpha}(0)=1
),
\alpha\geq-1/2
, with
J_{\alpha}(t)
the classical Bessel function of the first kind of order
\alpha
.

Let
\widehat{GM}^{d}
be the collection of all radial functions
f(x)=f_{0}(|x|),\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
such that the corresponding
F_{0}
given by (
16.4
) belongs to the class
GM
, is positive and satisfies the condition

(16.5)

\int_{0}^{1}u^{d-1}F_{0}(u)du+\int_{1}^{\infty}u^{(d-1)/2}|dF_{0}(u)|<\infty;

see
[
GT12
]
.
In other words,

\widehat{GM}^{d}
consists of radial functions

f(x)=f_{0}(|x|)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which are defined in terms of the inverse Fourier–Hankel transform

(16.6)

f_{0}(z)=\frac{2}{\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}\right)(2\sqrt{\pi})^{d}}\int_{0}^{\infty}F_{0}(s)j_{d/2-1}(zs)s^{d-1}\,ds,

where the function
F_{0}\in GM
and satisfies condition (
16.5
).
We note that, by
[
GLT11
, Lemma 1]
, the integral in (
16.5
)
converges in the improper sense and therefore
f_{0}(z)
is continuous for
z>0
.
If
d=1
we simply write
\widehat{GM}
.

In the discrete case,
\widehat{GM}
coincides with the well investigated class of
L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
-functions
f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)
such that the sequences of their Fourier coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}},\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in GM
, the
discrete general monotone condition
, that is,

(16.7)

\sum_{k=n}^{2n-1}|\Delta d_{k}|\leq C|d_{n}|

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
(
\Delta d_{k}:=d_{k}-d_{k+1}
) (cf. (
16.1
)); see
[
Ti07
,
LiT11
]
and the references therein.
In particular, the widely studied class (see, e.g.,
[
Zy02
, Chapters V, XII]
) of Fourier series with monotonic coefficients belongs to
\widehat{GM}
.

16.2.
Characterization of spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

Working with the
\widehat{GM}^{d}
class, we provide an effective criterion which characterizes
functions from
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in terms of their Fourier transform.

Theorem 16.2
.

Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Let
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}+

(16.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

(with the usual modifications if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Remark 16.3
.

The analogue of Theorem
16.2
for the Besov spaces
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
was obtained in
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.6]
: Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
, and
-\infty<s,b<\infty
. Let
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
. Then

\|f\|_{B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)q}(1+j)^{bq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/q}.

This also follows by setting
r=q
in (
16.8
) (see Proposition
3.8
).
On the other hand, dealing with
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the following was obtained in
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.2]
: Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
, and
b>-1/q
. Let
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

(16.9)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}.

The limiting case
b=-1/q
in
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is also covered by
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.2]
, but the outcome does not coincide with the right-hand side of (
16.9
).
We refer to
[
DT18
]
for further results on logarithmic Besov spaces and
\widehat{GM}^{d}
functions.

Proof of Theorem
16.2
.

We shall use two important properties of function in
\widehat{GM}^{d}
. First, the following description of the modulus of smoothness (cf. (
2.4
)) in terms of the Fourier transform for functions in the
\widehat{GM}^{d}
class (see
[
GT12
, Corollary 4.1 and
(7.6)]
)

(16.10)

\omega^{p}_{k}(f,t)_{p}\asymp t^{kp}\int_{0}^{1/t}u^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(u)du+\int_{1/t}^{\infty}u^{dp-d-1}F^{p}_{0}(u)du

where

(16.11)

k\in\mathbb{N}\quad\text{if}\quad d=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad k\quad\text{is even if}\quad d\geq 2.

Second, the Hardy-Littlewood-type estimate for functions
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
obtained in
[
GLT11
, Theorem 1]
(see also
[
GT12
, (4.10)]
) which asserts that

(16.12)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)dt\right)^{1/p},\qquad\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty.

For the one-dimensional case and monotone functions, see
[
Tit86
,
Boa72
,
Sa76
]
.

The case
s>0
and
b>0
:
Choose
k>s
with (
16.11
). Under these assumptions, it was already shown in (
6.6
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(16.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

We claim that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}

(16.14)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

Indeed, by (
16.10
) and a simple changes of variables, we get

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-248.96161pt\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{-(k-s)q}\left(\int_{0}^{u}v^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{q/p}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-241.84842pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq}\left(\int_{u}^{\infty}v^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{q/p}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-248.96161pt\asymp\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\hskip-241.84842pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-(k-s)r}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{1}^{t}v^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{r/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-241.84842pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{-(k-s)q}\left(\int_{t}^{u}v^{kp+dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{q/p}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-241.84842pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq}\left(\int_{u}^{\infty}v^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(v)\,dv\right)^{q/p}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

(16.15)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=:I+II+III+IV.

Next we show that

(16.16)

I+II\asymp I+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{sr+dr-dr/p}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}F_{0}^{r}(t)\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

and

(16.17)

I+III+IV\asymp I+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

Concerning (
16.16
), we make use of monotonicity property
F_{0}(x)\lesssim F_{0}(y)
for
y\leq x\leq 2y
(cf. (
16.2
)) so that

F_{0}(1)+II\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(k-s)r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{j}2^{l(k+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{l})\right)^{r/p}\right)^{1/r}

and thus Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
) implies

F_{0}(1)+II\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)r}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}F_{0}^{r}(2^{j})\right)^{1/r},

i.e., (
16.16
) holds. We note that the additional term
F_{0}(1)
appears in the previous estimates in order to get equivalence constants independent of
F_{0}
.

Similar ideas as above also work with (
16.17
). Specifically, applying monotonicity properties, we have

\displaystyle F_{0}(1)+III+IV

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\sum_{l=j}^{\infty}2^{-l(k-s)q}\left(\sum_{\nu=j}^{l}2^{\nu(kp+dp-d)}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\right)^{q/p}\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\sum_{l=j}^{\infty}2^{lsq}\left(\sum_{\nu=l}^{\infty}2^{\nu(dp-d)}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\right)^{q/p}\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Therefore Hardy’s inequalities (
B.3
) and (
B.4
) yield

F_{0}(1)+III+IV\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\sum_{\nu=j}^{\infty}2^{\nu(sq+dq-dq/p)}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r},

which gives the desired estimate (
16.17
).

Inserting (
16.16
) and (
16.17
) into (
16.15
) and using basic monotonicity properties (cf. (
16.2
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp I+

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{sr+dr-dr/p}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}F_{0}^{r}(t)\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-213.39566pt\asymp I+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

This proves (
16.14
).

In virtue of (
16.13
), (
16.12
) and (
16.14
), we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}+\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}

(16.18)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

Furthermore

(16.19)

\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

This is a simple consequence of monotonicity properties (cf. (
16.2
)), Hölder inequality (if
r\geq p
) and the fact that
\ell_{r}\hookrightarrow\ell_{p}
with
r<p
. Further details are left to the reader. As a combination of (
16.18
) and (
16.19
), we find

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}

(16.20)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

Let
\mu_{j}=2^{2^{j}},\,j\geq 0
. Applying Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) (recall that
b>0
)

\displaystyle F_{0}(1)+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-256.0748ptF_{0}(1)+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\left(\int_{\mu_{j}}^{\infty}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-256.0748pt\asymp F_{0}(1)+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\left(\int_{\mu_{j}}^{\mu_{j+1}-1}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

(16.21)

\displaystyle\hskip-256.0748pt\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\left(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

On the other hand, elementary monotonicity properties yield

(16.22)

\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}.

Putting together (
16.20
)–(
16.22
), the desired estimate (
16.8
) is achieved.

The case
s>0
and
b<0
:
The proof follows similar lines as the case
s>0
and
b>0
, but now relying on the characterization of
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
b<0
provided by (
6.8
), namely,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{t}^{1}(u^{-s}\omega_{k}(f,u)_{p})^{q}\frac{du}{u}\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}.

In this case, following similar arguments as in (
16.18
) and (
16.21
) (but using now (
B.3
)), one can prove

\displaystyle\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{dp-d-1}F_{0}^{p}(t)\,dt\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{(b-1/r)r}\left(\int_{1}^{t}u^{sq+dq-dq/p}F_{0}^{q}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

(16.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\left(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\right)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Further details are left to the reader.

The case
s\leq 0
:
We choose
\sigma
such that
\sigma<s
. Notice that
I_{\sigma}f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
(cf. (
8.1
) and Lemma
16.1
). According to Theorem
8.1
and the previous case, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|I_{\sigma}f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s-\sigma}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}(1+2^{-2j})^{\sigma p/2}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s-\sigma+d-d/p)q}(1+2^{2\nu})^{\sigma q/2}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

∎

Remark 16.4
.

Let us consider a slightly wider class (cf.
[
GLT11
,
LiT11
]
) of general monotone functions
\varphi(z)
which are locally of bounded variation, vanishes at
infinity, and there is a constant
c>1
depending on
\varphi

such that

(16.24)

\int_{z}^{\infty}|d\varphi(u)|\lesssim\int_{z/c}^{\infty}\frac{|\varphi(u)|}{u}du<\infty,\qquad z>0.

Noting that such functions satisfy the monotonicity condition (
16.3
), we claim that Theorem
16.2
holds in the case
\min\{q,r\}\geq p

if we consider the class formed
by all
radial functions
f(x)=f_{0}(|x|),x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
with the Fourier transform
F_{0}=\widehat{f}\geq 0

satisfying
conditions (
16.24
) and (
16.5
).

16.3.
Embeddings of
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

In Theorem
14.3
(see also Corollary
14.6
) a complete characterization of the embedding

(16.25)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

in terms of the involved parameters was provided. However, from the point of view of applications, it is of considerable interest to deal with sufficiently rich classes of functions (e.g. monotone-type functions) which enable to sharpen the general statement provided by (
16.25
). Accordingly, in this section we prove that Theorem
14.3
can be sharpened if we restrict ourselves to work with monotone-type functions. Specifically, the following result establishes necessary and sufficient conditions for which the embedding

(16.26)

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

holds and, in particular, it shows that the corresponding range of parameters is bigger than those related to (
16.25
).

Theorem 16.5
.

Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q<\infty,0<r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then (
16.26
) holds if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s>0
,

(ii)

s=0,\qquad p<q,\qquad 0<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(iii)

s=0,\qquad p<q,\qquad r\leq p,\qquad b=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(iv)

s=0,\qquad q\leq p,\qquad 0<r\leq\infty,\qquad b>0
.

Remark 16.6
.

The previous result shows a bigger range of parameters for the validity of (
16.26
) in comparison with the general embedding (
16.25
) treated in Theorem
14.3
. For instance, under the assumptions (ix) in Theorem
14.3
, the embedding (
16.25
) requires
b\geq 1/2-1/q
but (
16.26
) holds under the weaker condition
b\geq 1/p-1/q
if
p<q
and any
b>0
if
p\geq q
. Similar improvements can also be obtained under (x) in Theorem
14.3
.

Proof of Theorem
16.5
.

Sufficiency part:
Applying Theorem
16.2
and (
16.12
), the inequality

(16.27)

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad f\in\widehat{GM}^{d},

turns out to be equivalent

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

(16.28)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

where
F_{0}
is given by (
16.4
). In particular, (
16.27
) will be satisfied whenever

(16.29)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)q}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Assume
s>0
(i.e., (i) holds). Then (
16.29
) follows immediately from (
16.19
), (
16.21
) and (
16.23
).

Next we investigate the case
s=0
in (
16.29
). It is clear that

(16.30)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}=\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(1-1/p)dp}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{1/p}.

Assume
q\leq p
(i.e., (iv) holds), then

(16.31)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(1-1/p)dq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{p/q}\bigg)^{1/p}.

Therefore
r\leq p
and
b\geq 0
imply

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(1-1/p)dq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

On the other hand, if
r>p
and
b>0
, applying Hölder’s inequality in (
16.31
), the previous estimate (modulo replacing
\leq
by
\lesssim
) is also achieved. Hence (
16.29
) holds.

Suppose now
q>p
(i.e., the cases (ii) and (iii)). Using Hölder’s inequality in (
16.30
), we have

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(1/p-1/q)p}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(1-1/p)dq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{p/q}\bigg)^{1/p}.

There are two possibilities. Firstly, if
r\leq p
and
b\geq 1/p-1/q
then

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(1-1/p)dq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Secondly, the range
r>p
and
b>1/p-1/q
follows from Hölder’s inequality.

Neccessary part:
Next we construct counterexamples showing that (
16.27
) (or equivalently, (
16.28
)) becomes false if none of the conditions (i)–(v) is satisfied.

Necessary condition
s\geq 0
in (
16.27
):
For
s<0
, we let

F_{0}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(0,1],\\
t^{-d+d/p-\varepsilon},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(1,\infty),\end{array}\right.

where
s<\varepsilon<0
and

(16.32)

f(x)=f_{0}(|x|),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\text{with}\quad f_{0}\quad\text{given by \eqref{3.4new+}}.

Note that
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
since
F_{0}\in GM
. In light of Theorem
16.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s-\varepsilon)q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}2^{2^{j}(s-\varepsilon)r}\right)^{1/r}<\infty

and, on the other hand, by (
16.12
),

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\gtrsim\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-\varepsilon p-1}dt\right)^{1/p}=\infty.

Necessary condition
b\geq 1/p-1/q
in (
16.27
) with
s=0
and
p<q
:
If
b<1/p-1/q
, we let

F_{0}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(0,1],\\
t^{-d+d/p}(1+\log t)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(1,\infty),\end{array}\right.

where
\max\{b+1/q,1/q\}<\varepsilon<1/p
and the corresponding
f
defined by (
16.32
). According to Theorem
16.2
and (
16.12
), we derive

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{0}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}\nu^{-\varepsilon q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b-\varepsilon+1/q)r}\right)^{1/r}<\infty

and

\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\gtrsim\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{-\varepsilon p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/p}=\infty,

respectively.

Necessary condition
b>1/p-1/q
in (
16.27
) with
s=0
and
p<\min\{q,r\}
:
According to the previous case, it only remains to deal with the limiting value
b=1/p-1/q
. This can be done in a similar fashion as above by taking

F_{0}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(0,1],\\
t^{-d+d/p}(1+\log t)^{-1/p}(1+\log(1+\log t))^{-\varepsilon},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(1,\infty),\end{array}\right.

where
1/r<\varepsilon<1/p
.

Necessary condition
b\geq 0
in in (
16.27
) with
s=0
:
Evaluating (
16.28
) (with
s=0
) for the decreasing function
F_{0}
given by the characteristic function
\chi_{(0,2^{2^{N}})},\,N\geq 2
, we have

L+2^{2^{N}d(1-1/p)}\lesssim L+2^{2^{N}d(1-1/p)}2^{Nb}

where
L^{p}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}<\infty
. Hence

2^{-2^{N}d(1-1/p)}L+1\lesssim 2^{-2^{N}d(1-1/p)}L+2^{Nb},\qquad N\geq 2,

and, taking limits as
N\to\infty
, we conclude that necessarily
b\geq 0
.

∎

Specializing Theorem
16.5
with
q=r
we obtain the following result for classical Besov spaces.

Corollary 16.7
.

Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q<\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then

B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

holds if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied

(i)

s>0
,

(ii)

s=0,\qquad p<q,\qquad b>\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}
,

(iii)

s=0,\qquad p\geq q,\qquad b>0
.

An alternative approach to the previous result was proposed in
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.10]
. There the additional restriction
b\neq 0
(which naturally arises dealing with truncated function spaces) does not appear.

16.4.
Embeddings between
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

The full characterization of the embeddings

(16.33)

T^{b_{0}}_{r_{0}}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b_{1}}_{r_{1}}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

was obtained in Theorem
10.1
. Next we show that, working with general monotone functions,
\hookrightarrow
can be sharpened by
=
in (
16.33
). Before we state the precise statement, we introduce the following notation: for
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d},\,f(x)=f_{0}(|x|)
, and any
\tau>0
, set

J_{f}(\tau):=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}t^{d\tau-d-1}F_{0}^{\tau}(t)\,dt\bigg)^{1/\tau},

where
F_{0}
is defined by (
16.4
). Notice that, by (
16.2
),

(16.34)

J_{f}(\tau)\lesssim J_{f}(\kappa)\quad\text{for}\quad\kappa<\tau.

Theorem 16.8
.

Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p_{0}<p_{1}<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,
and
s_{0},s_{1},b\in\mathbb{R}
.

(i)

Assume that

(16.35)

s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=s_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}.

Let
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
. If
J_{f}(p_{0})<\infty
then

(16.36)

f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff f\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(ii)

Conversely, if

\widehat{GM}^{d}\cap T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\widehat{GM}^{d}\cap T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

then (
16.35
) holds.

Proof.

(i): Under the assumption given in (
16.35
), we obviously have

\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{s_{0}q+dq-dq/p_{0}-1}F_{0}^{q}(u)\,du=\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{s_{1}q+dq-dq/p_{1}-1}F_{0}^{q}(u)\,du

and thus (
16.36
) follows immediately from Theorem
16.2
and (
16.34
).

(ii): We shall proceed by contradiction, i.e., if we assume that (
16.35
) is false then
\widehat{GM}^{d}\cap T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\neq\widehat{GM}^{d}\cap T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Indeed, suppose
s_{0}-d/p_{0}>s_{1}-d/p_{1}
and let

F_{0}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1,&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(0,1],\\
t^{-s_{1}-d+d/p_{1}-\varepsilon},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(1,\infty),\end{array}\right.

where
0<\varepsilon<s_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}-s_{1}+\frac{d}{p_{1}}
and
f
defined by (
16.32
). Let

I_{i}=\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{br}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{s_{i}q+dq-dq/p_{i}-1}F_{0}^{q}(u)\,du\right)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}\quad\text{for}\quad i=0,1.

Elementary computations show that
I_{1}<\infty
but
I_{0}=\infty
. Applying Theorem
16.2
we arrive at the desired contradiction. The case
s_{0}-d/p_{0}<s_{1}-d/p_{1}
can be treated analogously.

∎

Remark 16.9
.

The assumption
J_{f}(p_{0})<\infty
in Theorem
16.8
(i) is necessary. To see this we consider the function
f
with (
16.32
) and

F_{0}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}t^{-\beta},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(0,1],\\
t^{-s_{1}-d+d/p_{1}-\varepsilon},&\quad\text{if}\quad t\in(1,\infty),\end{array}\right.

where
\varepsilon>0
and
d-\frac{d}{p_{0}}<\beta<d-\frac{d}{p_{1}}
. By Theorem
16.2
, we have
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}\cap T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
but
f\not\in T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

16.5.
Characterizations of spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})
.

The analogue of Theorem
16.2
for periodic functions and
GM
sequences (cf. (
16.7
)) reads as follows.

Theorem 16.10
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Let
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
be such that

f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)

with
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nonnegative general monotone sequences.
Then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu(s+1-1/p)q}(a_{2^{\nu}}+b_{2^{\nu}})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality that
f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}\cos nx
with nonnegative
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in GM
.

By Corollary
8.3
,

(16.37)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu sq}\bigg\|\sum_{m=2^{\nu}}^{2^{\nu+1}-1}a_{m}\cos mx\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

Furthermore, since
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in GM
, the following estimates hold

(16.38)

a_{2^{\nu+1}}2^{\nu(1-1/p)}\lesssim\bigg\|\sum_{m=2^{\nu}}^{2^{\nu+1}-1}a_{m}\cos mx\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim a_{2^{\nu}}2^{\nu(1-1/p)},

cf.
[
Ti07
]
. Combining (
16.37
) and (
16.38
) we achieve the desired result.
∎

Remark 16.11
.

Relying on Theorem
16.10
, all the results given in Sections
16.3
and
16.4
admit counterparts in the periodic setting. Further details are left to the interested reader.

16.6.
Characterizations of spaces
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

The goal of this section is to establish the characterization for Lipschitz spaces.

Theorem 16.12

(
Characterization of Lipschitz norms for
\widehat{GM}^{d}
functions).

Let
\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
, and
b<-1/q
. Assume that
f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

(16.39)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Remark 16.13
.

(i) If the right-hand side of (
16.39
) is finite then
f\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, i.e.,

(16.40)

\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})<\infty

(see (
16.12
)). Indeed, invoking Hölder’s inequality if
q\geq p
and the fact that
\ell_{q}\hookrightarrow\ell_{p}
if
q<p
, we can estimate, for
s>0
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
,

(16.41)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)q}(1+j)^{bq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/q}.

Furthermore, basic monotonicity properties and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) yield

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)q}(1+j)^{bq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=1}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Combining this estimate and (
16.41
),

(16.42)

\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Then the convergence of (
16.40
) results as a combination of (
16.42
) and (
16.39
).

(ii) The borderline case
b=0
and
q=\infty
in the previous theorem refers to the Sobolev space
H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\text{Lip}^{s,0}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(see (
2.10
)). This case was already investigated in
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.8]
, namely,

\|f\|_{H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{j})\right)^{1/p},\quad f\in\widehat{GM}^{d}.

Proof of Theorem
16.12
.

We make use of the following description (see
[
GT12
, Corollary 4.1]
) of the modulus of smoothness in terms of the Fourier transform for functions in the
\widehat{GM}^{d}
class

\omega_{s}(f,t)_{p}\asymp t^{s}\left(\int_{0}^{1/t}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{1/p}+\left(\int_{1/t}^{\infty}u^{dp-d}F^{p}_{0}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{1/p}.

Therefore,

(16.43)

\left(\int_{0}^{1}(t^{-s}(1-\log t)^{-b}\omega_{s}(f,t)_{p})^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}=I+II,

where

I=\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{0}^{1/t}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

and

II=\left(\int_{0}^{1}t^{-sq}(1-\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{1/t}^{\infty}u^{dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}.

A simple change of variables yields that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{0}^{t}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\int_{0}^{1}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{1}^{t}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

where we have also used that
b<-1/q
. Note that, by (
B.3
),

\displaystyle F_{0}(1)+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{1}^{t}u^{sp+dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim I

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

(16.44)

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Furthermore, a simple change of variables, basic monotonicity properties, (
B.4
) lead to

\displaystyle F_{0}(1)+II

\displaystyle=F_{0}(1)+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{sq}(1+\log t)^{bq}\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{dp-d}F_{0}^{p}(u)\frac{du}{u}\right)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jsq}(1+j)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}2^{k(dp-d)}F_{0}^{p}(2^{k})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(s+d-d/p)q}(1+j)^{bq}F_{0}^{q}(2^{j})\bigg)^{1/q}

(16.45)

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}.

According to (
16.43
), (
16.44
), (
16.45
), we estimate

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}

(16.46)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{1}(t^{-s}(1-\log t)^{b}\omega_{s}(f,t)_{p})^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j(p-1)d}F_{0}^{p}(2^{-j})\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\hskip-170.71652pt+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(s+d-d/p)p}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

Finally, the desired characterization (
16.39
) follows from (
16.12
) and (
16.46
).
∎

The next result is a direct consequence of Theorems
16.2
and
16.12
.

Corollary 16.14
.

Let
s>0,\frac{2d}{d+1}<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
. Then

\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{s}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}.

In particular (cf. Proposition
3.8
)

\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}=B^{s,b+1/p}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\widehat{GM}^{d}.

All the results from this section admit periodic counterparts in terms of general monotone sequences. For example
(see Theorem
16.12
)

Theorem 16.15
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
b<-1/q
. Let
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
be such that

f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)

with
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nonnegative general monotone sequences.
Then

\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu(s+1-1/p)q}(a_{2^{\nu}}+b_{2^{\nu}})^{p}\bigg)^{q/p}\right)^{1/q}.

17.
Some applications of truncated function spaces

17.1.
Trace operator

Let us consider the trace operator
Tr
, which restricts to
the hyperplane

\mathbb{R}^{d-1}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:x=(x^{\prime},0)\}

in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
for
d\geq 2
, that is,

(\text{Tr }f)(x)=(\text{Tr }_{\{x_{d}=0\}}f)(x)=f(x^{\prime},0).

For a rigorous interpretation of this operator, we refer to
[
Tri83
, Section 2.7.2]
.

Let

(17.1)

1\leq p\leq\infty,\,0<q\leq\infty\qquad\text{and}\qquad s>1/p.

Under these assumptions, it is well known that the trace
Tr
is
continuous

(17.2)

\text{Tr }:B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

and admits a right inverse
Ex
which is a
bounded linear operator from
B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
onto

B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Importantly, one can construct
Ex
to be a common extension operator for all spaces
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with (
17.1
). Further details may be found in
[
Tri20
, Section 2.2, pp. 29–38]
.

The borderline case
s=1/p
is of special interest. Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
0<q\leq 1
, then

(17.3)

\text{Tr }:B^{1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

and there exists an extension operator which is bounded but not
linear. This result has a substantial history. The case
p=2
and
q=1
was investigated by Agmon and
Hörmander
[
AH76
]
in their study of the asymptotic properties of the solutions of differential
equations; cf. also
[
N51
]
. For
1\leq p<\infty
and
q=1
the result was stated by
Peetre
[
Pe75
]
and a more general assertion covering anisotropic spaces may be found in Burenkov and Gol’dman
[
BuG79
]
. The extension to
0<q\leq 1
(and more generally,
0<p<\infty
and
0<q\leq\min\{1,p\}
) was obtained by Frazier and Jawerth
[
FJ85
]
via atomic decompositions. However
there does not exist a bounded linear extension operator from

L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
into
B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, see
[
Pe75
]
and
[
BuG79
]
. This is in contrast to (
17.2
).

The trace embedding (
17.3
) with
p=1
can be complemented via the famous Gagliardo’s theorem
[
Ga57
]
which establishes that

(17.4)

\text{Tr}:\text{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})\to L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and every function in
L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
has a bounded extension to
\text{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})
, the space of
bounded variation functions
on the upper half-space
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(0,\infty)
. Analogous statements hold when
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+}
is replaced by a bounded Lipschitz domain
\Omega
. In the same vein as (
17.3
), Peetre
[
Pe79
]
showed that there is no a bounded linear extension operator from
L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
\text{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}_{+})
.

Both (
17.3
) and (
17.4
) tell us that integrability properties of functions are not enough to construct bounded linear extension operators. However, it seems to be quite plausible that working with functions having at least rough smoothness the situation may improve. Indeed, very recently Malý, Shanmugalingam and Snipes
[
MSS18
]
showed the validity of the previous assertion dealing with the Dini space
\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}
(cf. (
2.6
)). To be more precise, in the more general setting of metric spaces equipped with a doubling measure and
\Omega
a bounded domain satisfying certain regularity assumptions, they proved the existence of a bounded linear extension operator

(17.5)

\text{Ex}:\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}(\partial\Omega)\to\text{BV}(\Omega)

such that
\text{Tr}\circ\text{Ex}=\text{id}
(identity operator). As a consequence of this result, a (nonlinear) extension operator from
L_{1}(\partial\Omega)
to
\text{BV}(\Omega)
can be constructed (cf. (
17.4
)).

Comparing now (
17.4
) with (
17.5
), one observes that the non-linearity of bounded extension operators can be circumvented by restricting the target space in (
17.4
) (i.e. substituting
\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}
for
L_{1}
), however both (
17.4
) and (
17.5
) are formulated in terms of
BV
(in particular, the trace of
BV
function is not necessarily Dini of type
\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}
). This leads us to ask whether one can improve (
17.5
). Let us also mention that related results to (
17.5
) for
L_{p}
-functions may be found in
[
KW20
]
.

The main result of this section gives a substantial improvement of (
17.5
) in terms of truncated Besov spaces. Namely, we establish the following

Theorem 17.1
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

(i)

the operator
\emph{Tr}
acts boundedly from

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into

\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
,

(ii)

there exists a linear extension operator
\emph{Ex}
which is
continuous from
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

onto
T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\emph{Tr}\circ\emph{Ex}=\emph{id}.

In light of Proposition
3.8
(i) and Theorem
17.1
with
q=1
, optimal bounded linear extension operators for
\mathbf{B}^{0}_{p,1}
are given in terms of the classical logarithmic Besov spaces
B^{1/p,1}_{p,1}
, i.e., a logarithmic derivative is gained. To be more precise,

Corollary 17.2
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\infty
and
b>-1
. Then

(i)

the operator
\emph{Tr}
acts boundedly from

B^{1/p,b+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into

\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
,

(ii)

there exists a linear extension operator
\emph{Ex}
which is
continuous from
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

onto
B^{1/p,b+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\emph{Tr}\circ\emph{Ex}=\emph{id}.

We write down Corollary
17.2
with
p=1
and
b=0
.

Corollary 17.3
.

The operator
Tr
acts boundedly from
B^{1,1}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
and there exists a bounded linear extension operator

(17.6)

\emph{Ex}:\mathbf{B}^{0}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\to B^{1,1}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

with
\emph{Tr}\circ\emph{Ex}=\emph{id}.

Note that

B^{1,1}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subsetneq B^{1}_{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subsetneq W^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subsetneq\text{BV}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Therefore, Corollary
17.3
drastically improves (
17.5
).

The method of proof of (
17.5
) in
[
MSS18
]
relies, among other tools, on partitions of unity related to Whitney coverings. On the other hand, the proof of Theorem
17.1
uses completely different machinery, specifically, we will make a strong use of several techniques already studied in previous sections: limiting interpolation (cf. Section
4
), approximation techniques (Jackson’s inequality) and certain decomposition methods for truncated Besov spaces (cf. Section
5
).

Proof of Theorem
17.1
.

(i): We first observe that trace operator is well defined on
T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Indeed, if
p<\infty
and
q<\infty
then
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
9.2
)) so that the the trace operator
Tr
on
this space should be understood in a classical sense via completion. If
p=\infty
then, by Theorem
15.1
(since
b+1/q>0
),
T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{\infty,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is formed only by continuous functions and
Tr
makes sense pointwise. If
q=\infty
then
T^{b}_{\infty}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,b>0,
is embedded into
B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, see Proposition
3.10
(i). Since traces are independent of the source spaces and of the target spaces (cf.
[
Tri08
, Remark 6.62]
), one can define the trace on
T^{b}_{\infty}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via restriction of the trace on
B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Let
s>1/p
. Applying the limiting interpolation method with
\theta=0
(cf. (
4.6
)) to (
17.2
)-(
17.3
) we obtain

(17.7)

\text{Tr }:(B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}\rightarrow(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),B^{s-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))_{(0,b),q}.

Next we determine the interpolation spaces appearing in
(
17.7
). It follows from Theorem
4.2
(i) (recall
b>-1/q
) that

(B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Concerning the target space in
(
17.7
), we invoke the interpolation formula (
4.38
) (see also Remark
4.13
) so that

(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),B^{s-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))_{(0,b),q}=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}).

Hence

\text{Tr }:T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}).

(ii): Let
f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
. Recall that
V_{t}f,\,t>0
, denotes the de la Valleé Poussin means (cf. (
5.18
)). Let
\mu_{k}:=2^{2^{k}},\,k\geq 0
. Therefore
f
admits the linear decomposition

(17.8)

f=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}R_{k}f\qquad\text{(convergence in
$L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})$)}

where
R_{k}:=V_{\mu_{k+1}}-V_{\mu_{k}}
for
k\geq 1
and
R_{0}:=V_{2}
. Indeed, it follows from (
5.17
) and the Jackson’s inequality (see e.g.
[
N75
, 5.2.1]
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|f-\sum_{k=0}^{N}R_{k}f\Big\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

\displaystyle=\|f-V_{\mu_{N+1}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\lesssim E_{\mu_{N+1}}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\lesssim\omega_{1}\Big(f,\frac{1}{\mu_{N+1}}\Big)_{p}

and
\omega_{1}(f,t)_{p}\to 0
as
t\to 0+
since
f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
.

Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

(17.9)

\text{supp }\widehat{\psi}\subseteq[-1,1]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\psi(0)=1.

Let us define the linear operator

(17.10)

\text{Ex}f(x)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d}),\quad x=(x^{\prime},x_{d}),\quad x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1},\quad x_{d}\in\mathbb{R}.

Note that
R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d})
is an entire function of exponential type
\mu_{k}
. Assume momentarily that (
17.10
) is well-defined for
f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
. Then invoking the representation theorems in
T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
given in (
5.14
) and (
5.9
), respectively, together with Fubini’s theorem, we get

\displaystyle\|\text{Ex}f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\|R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d})\|_{B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(17.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\mu_{k}^{q/p}\|R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d})\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\mu_{k}^{q/p}\|R_{k}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}^{q}\|\psi(\mu_{k}\cdot)\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\|R_{k}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Applying the triangle inequality, the best approximation property of the de la Valleé Poussin means (cf. (
5.17
)) and the Jackson’s inequality, we find

\displaystyle\|R_{k}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|f-V_{\mu_{k+1}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}+\|f-V_{\mu_{k}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{\mu_{k}}(f)_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\lesssim\omega_{1}\Big(f,\frac{1}{\mu_{k}}\Big)_{p}

and thus, making use of the monotonicity properties of the modulus of smoothness,

(17.12)

\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\|R_{k}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\omega_{1}\Big(f,\frac{1}{\mu_{k}}\Big)_{p}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}.

We can combine (
17.11
) and (
17.12
) to establish

\|\text{Ex}f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}

which shows that
Ex
is bounded from

\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
into

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Furthermore, it is clear from (
17.8
)–(
17.10
) that
\text{Tr }\circ\text{Ex }=\text{id}.

It remains to show that (
17.10
) is well-defined for
f\in\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
. Indeed, we will prove that the series given in the right-hand side of (
17.10
) converges in
B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).
This assertion is an immediate consequence of

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d})\|_{B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+\frac{1}{q})q}\|R_{k}f(x^{\prime})\,\psi(\mu_{k}x_{d})\|_{B^{1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

and (
17.11
)-(
17.12
). The validity of the previous estimate is an application of Hölder’s inequality if
q\geq 1
and the fact that
\ell_{q}\hookrightarrow\ell_{1}
if
q<1
.
∎

Unlike the intriguing case
s=1/p
considered in Theorem
17.1
, a description of traces for
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
s>1/p
follows easily just from the interpolation properties studied in Section
4.1
. More precisely, we establish the following result.

Theorem 17.4
.

Let

(17.13)

1\leq p\leq\infty,\qquad 0<q,r\leq\infty,\qquad s>\frac{1}{p}\qquad\text{and}\qquad b\neq 0.

Then

(i)

the operator
\emph{Tr}
acts boundedly from

T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto

T^{b}_{r}B^{s-\frac{1}{p}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
,

(ii)

there exists a linear extension operator
\emph{Ex}
which is
continuous from
T^{b}_{r}B^{s-\frac{1}{p}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

onto
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\emph{Tr}\circ\emph{Ex}=\emph{id}.

Proof.

Assume first
b>0
. According to (
17.2
), given any
\varepsilon>0
,

(17.14)

\text{Tr }:B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\text{Tr }:B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow B^{s+\varepsilon-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}).

Moreover, there exists a linear operator
Ex
such that

(17.15)

\text{Ex }:B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\longrightarrow B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\text{Ex }:B^{s+\varepsilon-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\longrightarrow B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\text{Tr }\circ\text{Ex }=\text{id}.

Applying limiting interpolation to (
17.14
) and (
17.15
), we derive

(17.16)

\text{Tr }:(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}\longrightarrow(B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),B^{s+\varepsilon-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}

and

(17.17)

\text{Ex }:(B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),B^{s+\varepsilon-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}\longrightarrow(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

Furthermore, in virtue of Theorem
4.2
(i), we have

(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

(B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),B^{s+\varepsilon-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}),

which combined with (
17.16
) and (
17.17
) give

\text{Tr }:T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

and

\text{Ex }:T^{b}_{r}B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\longrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The case
b<0
can be obtained in an analogous fashion, but now invoking part (ii) in Theorem
4.2
with
s_{0}=s-\varepsilon
and
0<\varepsilon<s-1/p
.
∎

Remark 17.5
.

The extension operator
Ex
in Theorem
17.4
is common for all spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with (
17.13
).

Remark 17.6
.

The method of proof of Theorem
17.4
still works with limiting case
b=0
, however the outcome is now formulated in terms of the spaces
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
; cf. (
3.5
) and Remark
4.5
. Specifically, if
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty
and
s>1/p
, then

\text{Tr }:T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longrightarrow T^{*}_{r}B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

and there exists a linear extension operator
Ex
satisfying

\text{Ex }:T^{*}_{r}B^{s-1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})\longrightarrow T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\text{Tr }\circ\text{Ex }=\text{id}.

Specialising Theorem
17.4
with
r=q
and taking into account Proposition
3.8
(i), we are able to extend
17.2
to the setting of
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces.

Corollary 17.7
.

Let

1\leq p\leq\infty,\qquad 0<q\leq\infty,\qquad s>\frac{1}{p}\qquad\text{and}\qquad b\in\mathbb{R}.

Then

(i)

the operator
\emph{Tr}
acts boundedly from

B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto

B^{s-\frac{1}{p},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
,

(ii)

there exists a linear extension operator
\emph{Ex}
which is
continuous from
B^{s-\frac{1}{p},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})

onto
B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\emph{Tr}\circ\emph{Ex}=\emph{id}.

Remark 17.8
.

The previous result may be viewed as the non-limiting case (i.e.,
s>1/p
) of Corollary
17.2
. In particular, this shows an interesting switch from
B^{0,b}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
as expected trace scale of Besov spaces with critical smoothness
s=1/p
to the more surprising scale given by spaces
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
(see (
2.11
)).

Remark 17.9
.

All the results from this subsection can be extended to deal with traces on hyperplanes of dimension
1,2,\ldots,d-2
. But this is a technical matter and will not be done in detail here. We just mention that the corresponding extension of Theorem
17.1
reads as follows: Let
d>m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\text{Tr}_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}=\text{Tr}
. Let
1\leq p\leq\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then
\text{Tr}:T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{\frac{d-m}{p}}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and there exists a bounded linear extension operator
\text{Ex}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{\frac{d-m}{p}}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\text{Tr }\circ\text{Ex }=\text{id}.

17.2.
Embeddings with critical smoothness

The goal of this section is to apply the theory of truncated Besov spaces developed in previous sections in order to improve classical embedding theorems in the critical case
s=d/p
. We divide our analysis into two parts. First, we shall concentrate on Trudinger-type inequalities (i.e., the case
b<-1/q
). Second, we turn our attention to embeddings into Hölder-type spaces (i.e., the case
b\geq-1/q
). Before we go further, we briefly recall the definition of Lorentz–Zygmund spaces.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
stands for the
Lorentz–Zygmund space
formed by all measurable functions
f
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that

(17.18)

\|f\|_{L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}[t^{1/p}(1-\log t)^{b}f^{*}(t)]^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
). As usual,
f^{*}
stands for the
non-increasing rearrangement of
f
. We shall consider the rearrangement which is left-continuous. It can be expressed uniquely by the equality

(17.19)

f^{*}(t)=\sup_{|E|=t}\inf_{x\in E}|f(x)|

(see
[
CR71
]
). A basic reference for Lorentz–Zygmund spaces is
[
BR80
]
. This scale contains many classical spaces as distinguished elements: Letting
p=q
in
L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
one obtains the
Zygmund space

L_{p}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and if, additionally,
b=0
then
L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. On the other hand, setting
b=0
in
L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, one recovers
Lorentz spaces

L_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. Dealing with the limiting value
p=\infty
, the additional assumption
b<-1/q
(
b\leq 0
if
q=\infty
) is required in order to get meaningful spaces. In particular, it is well known that
L_{\infty}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=L_{\infty,\infty}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is the classical
Orlicz space

\text{exp}\,L^{-\frac{1}{b}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
of exponentially integrable functions, cf.
[
BR80
, Theorem 10.3]
and
[
BS88
, Chapter 4, Lemma 6.2]
.

Trudinger’s embeddings: the case
b<-1/q
.
To avoid technical issues, we will switch momentarily to function spaces defined on the
d
-dimensional torus
\mathbb{T}^{d}
. We refer to Remark
17.12
below for further details.

The celebrated Trudinger’s inequality
[
Tru67
,
Yu61
]
can be rephrased in terms of the following embeddings

(17.20)

W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\log L)_{-1/p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\qquad p\in(1,\infty).

This result has shown to be very useful in the study of elliptic and parabolic equations and Gaussian curvatures on sphere. Furthermore, (
17.20
) is sharp within the class of Orlicz spaces, but it can be improved when dealing with the larger class of Lorentz–Zygmund spaces (
17.18
). Indeed, the Maz’ya–Hansson–Brezis–Wainger embedding claims that

(17.21)

W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,p}(\log L)_{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

cf.
[
BreWain80
,
Han79
,
Maz11
]
. Further, it is plain to see that (cf.
[
BR80
, Theorem 9.5]
)

L_{\infty,p}(\log L)_{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\log L)_{-1/p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Accordingly (
17.21
) yields an improvement of (
17.20
).

In fact, not only the target space in (
17.20
) can be sharpened but also the domain space can be sharpened in terms of Besov spaces. Indeed, the following improvement of (
17.21
) holds, for
0<p<\infty
and
1<q\leq\infty
,

(17.22)

B^{d/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d});

cf.
[
Tri01
, Theorem 13.2]
,
[
Ha07
, Theorem 8.16]
and the references within. Note that, by Franke–Jawerth embeddings (
13.1
) (recall that Littlewood–Paley theorem asserts that
W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=F^{d/p}_{p,2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\,p>1
),

W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{d/p_{0}}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\qquad p<p_{0},

so that (
17.22
) sharpens (
17.21
).

The embedding (
17.22
) can be placed naturally in the more general setting of Besov spaces of logarithmic smoothness. Namely, if
0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
then

(17.23)

B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

In particular, setting
b=-1
in (
17.23
) gives back (
17.22
).
The embedding (
17.23
) together with its generalizations have been the object of intensive research. In this regard, we only mention here
[
DRS79
]
,
[
CaMou04b
]
,
[
CaFar06
]
,
[
CaLeo06
]
,
[
Mar08
]
and
[
MNP08
]
. In particular, it is well known that (
17.23
) is optimal within the scale of Besov spaces of logarithmic smoothness. However, as a byproduct of the following result, we can conclude that the known embedding (
17.23
) can be sharpened with the help of the truncated Besov spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.

Theorem 17.10
.

Let
0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
. Then

(17.24)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Furthermore, the embedding is optimal in the following sense: let
0<u\leq\infty
, then

(17.25)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\iff u\leq 1.

Before we proceed with the proof of this theorem, we show that (
17.24
) is a non-trivial improvement of (
17.23
) (and in particular (
17.22
)).

Remark 17.11
.

Note that as a consequence of the periodic counterpart of Corollary
10.5
, we derive

B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})+B^{d/p,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{1,q\}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Thus, by (
17.24
),

B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})+B^{d/p,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{1,q\}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

which implies (
17.23
). In fact, by Proposition
3.8
, (
17.24
) coincides with (
17.23
) if
q=1
. However, if
q\neq 1
then (
17.24
) gives a non-trivial improvement of (
17.23
) since one can construct
f\in T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
such that
f\not\in B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. The construction of these extremal functions can be done using the orthonormal system
\{\Psi^{j,L,\text{per}}_{G,m}\}
in
L_{2}(\mathbb{T^{d}})
obtained from standard periodization arguments for (cf. (
7.2
))

\Psi^{j,L}_{G,m}(x):=2^{(j+L)d/2}\prod^{n}_{r=1}\psi_{G_{r}}(2^{j+L}x_{r}-m_{r}),

where
L\in\mathbb{N}
is a fixed parameter such that
\text{supp }\Psi^{j,L}_{G,m}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|<1/2\}
. Here
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j}
and
m\in\mathbb{P}^{d}_{j}=\{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:0\leq m_{r}<2^{j+L}\}
. We refer the interested reader to
[
Tri08
, Section 1.3.2]
for further details. Then obvious modifications show that the periodic counterpart of Theorem
7.5
holds (in particular the related sequence spaces are defined in the same fashion as (
7.8
) but now
m
runs over the index set
\mathbb{P}^{d}_{j}
). Assume
q>1
and let

(17.26)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{P}^{d}_{j}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-(j+L)d/2}\Psi^{j,L,\text{per}}_{G,m}

where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{-k\varepsilon},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
b+\frac{1}{q}<\varepsilon<b+1
. According to Theorem
7.5
, we have

\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q-\varepsilon)q}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty

and, by Theorem
7.1
(more precisely, its periodic counterpart),

\|f\|_{B^{d/p,b+1}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1-\varepsilon)q}\right)^{1/q}=\infty.

Assume now
q<1
. Then let
f
be given by (
17.26
) with

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
b+\frac{1}{q}+1<\varepsilon<b+\frac{2}{q}
. Invoking again Theorems
7.1
and
7.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}\bigg)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b-\varepsilon+1+1/q)q}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty

and

\|f\|_{B^{d/p,b+1/q}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{(b+1/q-\varepsilon)q}\right)^{1/q}=\infty.

Proof of Theorem
17.10
.

Applying the limiting interpolation method (
4.6
) with
\theta=1
to the classical embeddings

(17.27)

B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

and

B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,1\}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

we derive

(17.28)

(B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,1\}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}\hookrightarrow(L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}.

On the one hand, invoking Theorem
4.2
(ii) (to be more precise, its periodic counterpart), we have

(17.29)

(B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,1\}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

On the other hand, since (cf.
[
BL76
, Theorem 5.2.1]
)

(17.30)

K(t,f;L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{t^{p}}(f^{*}(u))^{p}\,du\bigg)^{1/p},

we can apply basic monotonicity properties and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) so that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}t^{-q/p}(1-\log t)^{bq}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(f^{*}(u))^{p}\,du\bigg)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jq/p}(1+j)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}(f^{*}(2^{-k}))^{p}2^{-k}\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{bq}(f^{*}(2^{-j}))^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}((1-\log t)^{b}f^{*}(t))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}=\|f\|_{L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

This estimate together with (
17.28
) and (
17.29
) enable us to conclude that

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Next we concentrate on the sharpness assertion (
17.25
). If
u\leq 1
then

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

(cf. Theorem
10.1
) and thus the embedding given in (
17.25
) follows trivially from (
17.24
). Conversely, we assume that

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

holds true and we will show that necessarily
u\leq 1
. Indeed, suppose that
u>1
and let

(17.31)

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},G\in G^{j},m\in\mathbb{P}^{d}_{j}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}2^{-(j+L)d/2}\Psi^{j,L,\text{per}}_{G,m}

where

(17.32)

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
b+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{u}<\varepsilon<b+\frac{1}{q}+1
. According to the periodic counterpart of Theorem
7.5
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon u}\bigg)^{q/u}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q-\varepsilon+1/u)q}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty.

Given
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, by (
17.19
), (
17.31
) and (
17.32
),

(17.33)

f^{*}(2^{-kd})\geq\inf_{x\in Q_{k,(0,\ldots,0)}}|f(x)|\gtrsim\sum_{j=0}^{k}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}

and thus

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{\infty,q}(\log L)_{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}((1+k)^{b}f^{*}(2^{-kd}))^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}\bigg)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq-\varepsilon q+q}\bigg)^{1/q}=\infty.

This yields the desired contradiction.
∎

Remark 17.12
.

As already mentioned at the beginning of this section, Theorem
17.10
is stated for periodic functions. In particular, we make use of the fact that the couple
(L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
is ordered (i.e.,
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
) when we apply the limiting interpolation method in (
17.28
); see Section
4.1
. Working with functions defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the proof of Theorem
17.10
can be easily adapted to obtain the following local result: Let
0<p<\infty,0<q,u\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
. Then

\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}((1-\log t)^{b}f^{*}(t))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

if and only if
u\leq 1
.

Embedding into Hölder spaces: the case
b>-1/q
. In this section we study
\mathbf{B}_{\infty,q}^{0,b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
spaces, which sometimes denoted by
\Lambda_{\infty,q}^{(1-\log t)^{-b}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and called Hölder spaces, see
[
Ha07
]
,
[
MNP09
]
,
[
MNS11
]
,
[
MNS14
]
. Embedding theorems into these spaces have been intensively studied in recent years (occasionally, under the name of continuity envelopes and even in the setting of generalized smoothness) in the above mentioned papers and the references within. In this direction, the best known embedding theorem reads as follows: let
0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-\frac{1}{q}
, then

(17.34)

B^{d/p,b+\frac{1}{\min\{1,q\}}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}_{\infty,q}^{0,b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, the embedding (
17.34
) is optimal within the scale of classical spaces
B^{d/p,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Next we show that (
17.34
) can be improved applying the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. To be more precise, we establish the following

Theorem 17.13
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

(17.35)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, the embedding is optimal in the following sense: let
0<u\leq\infty
, then

(17.36)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff u\leq 1.

Remark 17.14
.

In view of Theorem
10.5
, one has

B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+B^{d/p,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{1,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and thus (
17.35
) implies, in particular, (
17.34
). Furthermore, the embedding
B^{d/p,b+1/\min\{1,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is strict if
q\neq 1
(by Proposition
3.8
, these spaces coincide of
q=1
). Indeed, counterexamples given in Remark
17.11
work.

Proof of Theorem
17.13
.

It follows from (
17.27
) (where
\mathbb{T}^{d}
is now replaced by
\mathbb{R}^{d}
) that

B^{d/p+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

To be more precise, by Proposition
8.6
,

\|f\|_{W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\nabla f\|_{B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{B^{d/p+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Applying limiting interpolation with
\theta=0
(cf. (
4.6
)) we infer

(17.37)

(B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{d/p+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}\hookrightarrow(L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}.

According to Theorem
4.2
, the left-hand side space can be equivalently characterized as

(17.38)

(B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{d/p+1}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

On the other hand, the well-known formula (see e.g.
[
BS88
, Chapter 5, Theorem 4.12]
)

K(t^{k},f;L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{k}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp t^{k}\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\omega_{k}(f,t)_{\infty}

for
t\in(0,1)
implies

(17.39)

(L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W^{1}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Putting together (
17.37
)–(
17.39
) we obtain

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

i.e., (
17.35
) holds.

Conversely, we let

(17.40)

T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for some
0<u\leq\infty
. Then necessarily
u\leq 1
. Indeed, assume momentarily that (
17.40
) holds for some
u>1
. Define

(17.41)

f=\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\,2^{-jd/2}\,\Psi^{j}_{G,m},

where

(17.42)

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
\max\{b+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{u},1\}<\varepsilon<b+\frac{1}{q}+1
. By Theorem
7.5
,

\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q-\varepsilon+1/u)q}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty.

We may assume, without loss of generality, that
\psi_{M}(0)=\max_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\psi_{M}(z)
(in particular,
\psi_{M}(0)>0
) and there exists
c\in(0,1)
(depending on
\psi_{M}
) such that
\psi_{M}(z)\leq c\,\psi_{M}(0)
for all
z\geq 2
. Let
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Given
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we can estimate
\omega_{1}(f,2^{-k})_{\infty}
(cf. (
2.4
)) as follows (cf. (
7.2
))

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(f,2^{-k})_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq|f(0)-f(2^{-k}e_{1})|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}\,(\psi_{M}(0))^{d}-\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}\,\psi_{M}(2^{j-k})(\psi_{M}(0))^{d-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle=(\psi_{M}(0))^{d-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}(\psi_{M}(0)-\psi_{M}(2^{j-k}))

\displaystyle\geq(\psi_{M}(0))^{d-1}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}(\psi_{M}(0)-\psi_{M}(2^{j-k}))

\displaystyle\geq(1-c)(\psi_{M}(0))^{d}\sum_{j=k+1}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\asymp(1+k)^{-\varepsilon+1}.

Therefore, one can estimate

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big((1+k)^{b}\omega_{1}(f,2^{-k})_{\infty}\big)^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq-\varepsilon q+q}\bigg)=\infty.

This yields the desired contradiction of (
17.40
) if
b>-1/q
.
∎

Remark 17.15
.

It remains to investigate the borderline setting
b=-1/q
in Theorem
17.13
. In this case, the method of proof proposed for Theorem
17.13
still works, but the expected spaces
T_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
do not arise. Instead, the smaller spaces
T^{*}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
3.5
)) play a key role. To be more precise, if
0<p,q<\infty
and
0<u\leq\infty
then

T^{*}_{q}B^{d/p}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/q}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff u\leq 1.

The proof of the implication
\Rightarrow
follows similar ideas as in Theorem
17.13
but now relying on Remark
7.7
. More precisely, consider
f
given by (
17.41
) with (
17.42
) and
\varepsilon=1
.

17.3.
Sharp embeddings into
L_{\infty}

It is the well-known fact that the Sobolev space
W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,p>1,
is not formed by bounded functions. As already mentioned in Section
17.2
, this defect can be overcome if we replace
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by the slightly larger exponential class (cf. (
17.20
)). Another alternative is to fix
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as a target space and restrict the domain space
W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to the smaller Lorentz-Sobolev space
W^{d/p}L_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf.
[
Ste81
]
for a precise statement; see also
[
DS84
]
). Next we propose a third methodology in terms of Besov spaces
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
using the embeddings for truncated Triebel-Lizorkin spaces.

Theorem 17.16
.

Let
1<p<\infty
and
d/p\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}_{p,1}^{0,-1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

According to Theorem
15.4
,

(17.43)

T^{1/p^{\prime}}_{1}F^{d/p}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Furthermore, by Propositions
8.7
and
3.8
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{1/p^{\prime}}_{1}F^{d/p}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{T^{1/p^{\prime}}_{1}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{T^{1/p^{\prime}}_{1}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(17.44)

\displaystyle\asymp\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

It follows from (
17.43
) and (
17.44
) that

\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Thus the proof will be finished once we show

(17.45)

\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Indeed, according to (
2.6
) (with
k=d/p
)

\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{-1/p}\omega_{d/p}(f,t)_{p}\frac{dt}{t}

and the estimates
\omega_{d/p}(f,t)_{p}\lesssim t^{d/p}\|f\|_{W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(cf. (
2.10
)), we get via (
8.6
)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,-1/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{d/p}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{d/p}f}{\partial x_{l}^{d/p}}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{B}_{p,1}^{0,-1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Hence (
17.45
) holds.
∎

17.4.
Sobolev-type embeddings in the subcritical case

In this section we investigate optimal Sobolev-type embeddings in the sub-critical case
\sigma_{p}\leq s<\frac{d}{p}
. Recall that
\sigma_{p}=d(\frac{1}{p}-1)_{+}
. This question
is nowadays completely understood for Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces (with generalized smoothness) when
\sigma_{p}<s<\frac{d}{p}
, see
[
DRS79
]
,
[
BriMou03
]
,
[
CaMou04a
]
,
[
CaFar06
]
,
[
CaLeo06
]
,
[
Ha07
]
,
[
Mar08
]
,
[
MNP08
]
and the references within.

In the borderline case
s=\sigma_{p}
and classical spaces
A^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,A\in\{B,F\}
, the optimality (in terms of growth envelopes) of the related Sobolev inequalities may be found in
[
V10
]
(with
[
Ha07
]
as a forerunner). However, to the best of our knowledge, the corresponding extension to generalized smoothness (even for the prototype given by logarithmic smoothness) remains as an open problem. This question was explicitly stated by Triebel
[
Tri12
]
, where first results in this direction were obtained. Before we proceed further, we recall that
A^{\sigma_{p},b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is formed by distributions, but not necessarily regular distributions (cf. Theorems
14.1
and
14.2
). In particular, if
1<p<\infty
then

B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{1}^{\text{\text{loc}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}b\geq 0&\text{ if }&0<q\leq\min\{2,p\},\\
b>1/p-1/q&\text{ if }&1<p\leq 2\quad\text{and}\quad p<q\leq\infty,\\
b>1/2-1/q&\text{ if }&2<p<\infty\quad\text{and}\quad 2<q\leq\infty.\end{array}\right.

Therefore, under the above assumptions on the parameters, it makes sense to study Sobolev-type embeddings related to
B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. To the best of our knowledge, the sharpest result so far appears in
[
D17
]
(in particular, improving earlier results in
[
Tri12
]
). Namely, if
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>\frac{1}{\min\{2,p,q\}}-\frac{1}{q}
then

(17.46)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b-\frac{1}{\min\{2,p,q\}},q}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Here, for
0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b\geq-1/q\,(b>0\text{ if }q=\infty)
, by
L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)
we mean the
small Lebesgue space
equipped with

(17.47)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}:=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{bq}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(f^{*}(u))^{p}\,du\bigg)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

(where the usual change is made if
q=\infty
); see the recent survey paper
[
FFG18
]
and the extensive list of references given there. Note that
b\geq-1/q
is natural; otherwise, the space
L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)
coincides with
L_{p}(0,1)
. Clearly
L^{(p,b,p}(0,1)=L_{p}(\log L)_{b+1/p}(0,1)
if
b>-1/p
, but in general the spaces
L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)
with
p\neq q
does not fit into the scale of Lorentz–Zygmund spaces (
17.18
). However, there are some known relations between these two scales of function spaces, e.g.,

L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b+1/\min\{p,q\}}(0,1)\hookrightarrow L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)\hookrightarrow L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b+1/\max\{p,q\}}(0,1)

provided that
0<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Applying these relations, one immediately gets from (
17.46
) (and under the same assumptions on the parameters as there) that

\|f\|_{L_{p,q}(\log L)_{b-\frac{1}{\min\{2,p,q\}}+\frac{1}{\max\{p,q\}}}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(cf.
[
D17
, Corollary 3.2]
). However, the methodology in
[
D17
]
, which is based on extrapolation techniques, is not strong enough to achieve the optimality of (
17.46
) in the full range of parameters. Below, we propose a novel methodology relying on the spaces
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
which gives an answer to the Triebel’s question and, in particular, improves (
17.46
).

Before we state our result, it is worthwhile to mention that the corresponding question for
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
2.6
)) has been now completely solved as a combination of the papers
[
CGO08
,
CGO11
,
D16
,
NO20
]
and the references given there. In this regard, let us emphasize that
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\neq B^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and, in fact, there are substantial distinctions between these two Besov spaces of smoothness near zero. A detailed account on this point may be found in
[
DT18
]
.

Theorem 17.17
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

(17.48)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The embedding is optimal in the following sense. Let
0<u\leq\infty
. If
p\leq 2
then

(17.49)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\iff u\leq p.

Moreover, if
2<\min\{p,u\}
then the inequality given in (
17.49
) is not true whenever one of the following conditions holds:

(i)

b\in[-\frac{1}{q},-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{u})
,

(ii)

b=-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{u}
and
q>2
.

Remark 17.18
.

Note that (
17.48
) gives a non-trivial improvement of (
17.46
) since

B^{0,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+B^{0,b+1/q}_{p,\min\{2,p,q\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

provided that
b>-1/q
(cf. Theorem
10.5
) and one can construct
f
such that

(17.50)

f\in T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{but}\quad f\not\in B^{0,b+1/\min\{2,p,q\}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

whenever
q\neq\min\{2,p\}
. Indeed, if
q<\min\{2,p\}
then we let

f=\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\,2^{-jd/2}\,\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{jd/p}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
b+\frac{1}{\min\{2,p\}}+\frac{1}{q}<\varepsilon<b+\frac{2}{q}
. Applying Theorems
7.1
and
7.5
, it is plain to see that
f
satisfies the desired claim (
17.50
). On the other hand, if
q>\min\{2,p\}
then we consider
f
as given above with
