\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{\frac{2^{k}d}{p}}2^{-k\varepsilon},&\quad j=2^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\\
&\qquad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
b+\frac{1}{q}<\varepsilon<b+\frac{1}{\min\{2,p\}}
.

Proof of Theorem
17.17
.

We start by showing (
17.48
). We wish to interpolate by the limiting method with
\theta=0
the well-known embeddings

(17.51)

B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\qquad\text{and}\qquad B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

However, when we try to accomplish this task, a first technical issue already appears, namely, the limiting interpolation method
(A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,b),q}
as given in (
4.6
) is only defined for ordered couples and the particular choice
(A_{0},A_{1})=(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
is not ordered. To overcome this obstruction, we shall modify the original definition of the limiting interpolation (
4.6
) to make it available for general couples of quasi-Banach spaces. Specifically, let
(A_{0},A_{1})
be a quasi-Banach couple (not necessarily ordered) and let
0<q\leq\infty
and
b,\eta\in\mathbb{R}
, we introduce the limiting interpolation space
(A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}
as the set of all those
f\in A_{0}+A_{1}
such that

(17.52)

\|f\|_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}}:=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}((1-\log t)^{b}K(t,f))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}+\bigg(\int_{1}^{\infty}((1+\log t)^{\eta}K(t,f))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

is finite (where the usual change has to be made if
q=\infty
). Here we shall assume
\eta<-1/q
so that
(A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}\neq\{0\}
. Note that working with ordered couples
(A_{0},A_{1})
, since
K(t,f)\asymp\|f\|_{A_{0}}\asymp K(1,f)
for
t>1
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(A_{0},A_{1})_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}((1-\log t)^{b}K(t,f))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}+K(1,f)

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}((1-\log t)^{b}K(t,f))^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

and thus one recovers the original definition (cf. (
4.6
)).

Let
\eta<-1/q
. Applying the limiting interpolation method (
17.52
) to (
17.51
) we get

(17.53)

(B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}\hookrightarrow(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}.

Next we compute these interpolation spaces. On the one hand, it follows from (
4.6
) and (
17.30
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{bq}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(f^{*}(u))^{p}\,du\bigg)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

(17.54)

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}.

On the other hand, since
B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, it follows from Theorem
4.2
that

\displaystyle(B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,(b,\eta)),q}

\displaystyle=(B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),B^{d/p}_{p,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}

(17.55)

\displaystyle=T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

As a combination of (
17.53
)-(
17.55
) we establish

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Next we deal with (
17.49
). More precisely, we will show that if
p\leq 2
and the inequality

(17.56)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}\lesssim\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

holds, then necessarily
u\leq p
. To do this, we shall assume
u>p
and then we will arrive at a contradiction. Indeed, we let

f=\sum^{\infty}_{j=0}\sum_{G\in G^{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lambda^{j,G}_{m}\,2^{-jd/2}\,\Psi^{j}_{G,m}

where

\lambda^{j,G}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2^{jd/p}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon},&\quad j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\quad m=(0,\ldots,0),\quad G=(M,\ldots,M),\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

and
\max\{b+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{u},\frac{1}{p}\}<\varepsilon<b+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}
(recall
b>-1/q
). By Theorem
7.5
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{j=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon u}\bigg)^{q/u}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{k(b+1/q-\varepsilon+1/u)q}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty.

On the other hand, by basic monotonicity properties and (
17.33
), we find that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(f^{*}(2^{-jd}))^{p}2^{-jd}\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(2^{jd/p}(1+j)^{-\varepsilon})^{p}2^{-jd}\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+k)^{bq-\varepsilon q+q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}=\infty.

It remains to show the optimality of (
17.56
) in the range
2<\min\{p,u\}
. Next we prove that (
17.56
) fails to be true if one of the conditions (i) or (ii) holds.

Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\backslash\{0\}
be a fixed function with (
14.17
) and let
f
be the function whose Fourier series is lacunary of the form (
14.18
). According to (
14.19
),

(17.57)

\|f\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}B^{0}_{p,u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|\psi\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\left(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}|\lambda_{\nu}|^{u}\bigg)^{q/u}\right)^{1/q}.

On the other hand, by monotonicity properties and the Hardy–Littlewood inequality for rearrangements (cf.
[
BS88
, Chapter 2, Lemma 2.1, p. 44]
), we can estimate

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}t^{q/p}(1-\log t)^{bq}\bigg(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}(f^{*}(u))^{p}\,du\bigg)^{q/p}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(f^{*}(t))^{p}\,dt\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\\
|E|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\bigg(\int_{E}|f(x)|^{p}\,dx\bigg)^{1/p}

which implies, by the Zygmund property for lacunary Fourier series (see, e.g.,
[
Gra08
, Theorem 3.7.4]
), that

(17.58)

\|f\|_{L^{(p,b,q}(0,1)}\gtrsim\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

where the implicit constant depends on
\psi
.

It follows from (
17.56
)–(
17.58
) that

(17.59)

\bigg(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}|\lambda_{\nu}|^{u}\bigg)^{q/u}\right)^{1/q}

for all
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. However, in general, this inequality fails to be true. If
b\in[-\frac{1}{q},-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{u})
(i.e., (i) holds), we consider the sequence
\lambda_{j}=(1+j)^{-\varepsilon}
where
b+\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1}{q}<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
. Therefore the right-hand side of (
17.59
) is finite since

\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(1+\nu)^{-\varepsilon u}\bigg)^{q/u}\asymp\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q-\varepsilon+1/u)q}<\infty

but the corresponding left-hand side can be estimated as

\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}|\lambda_{j}|^{2}=\sum_{j=3}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-2\varepsilon}=\infty.

If
b=-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{u}
and
q>2
(i.e., the condition (ii) is fulfilled) then we can take
\lambda_{j}=(1+j)^{-1/2}(1+\log(1+j))^{-\beta}
with
\beta\in(\frac{1}{q},\frac{1}{2})
in the above argument.
∎

Remark 17.19
.

One can also deal with the limiting value
b=-1/q
in Theorem
17.17
, after replacing the space
T_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{2,p\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by the smaller one
T^{*}_{q}B^{0}_{p,\min\{p,2\}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(cf. (
3.5
)).

17.5.
Bourgain–Brezis–Mironescu formulas for Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

A well-known defect of the fractional Sobolev seminorms

\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\ \bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+sp}}\,dx\,dy\bigg)^{1/p},\quad s\in(0,1),\quad p\in[1,\infty),

(see also (
6.13
)) is that they do not converge to the classical Sobolev seminorm
\|\nabla f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
as
s\to 1-
. This defect can be fixed under a certain normalization of the seminorms
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
. Namely, a celebrated result by Bourgain–Brezis–Mironescu
[
BBM01
]
asserts that

(17.60)

\lim_{s\to 1-}(1-s)^{1/p}\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=c_{d,p}\,\|\nabla f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

for
p\in(1,\infty)
. Here, the explicit value of the constant
c_{d,p}
is known, depending only on
d
and
p
. This result has been extended in many different ways and in several contexts, e.g., Milman showed in
[
Mil05
]
that (
17.60
) is a special case of a more general phenomenon based on interpolation theory and this method can be applied, in particular, to extend (
17.60
) to Besov seminorms of higher order (cf.
[
KMX05
]
).

The aim of this section is to show that the family of truncated norms on Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces satisfy the Bourgain–Brezis–Mironescu phenomenon, in the sense that under a certain normalization, one can attain the classical Besov norm
\|\cdot\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
via limits. In this regard, it is important to mention that the chosen norms on
T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
play a key role. Indeed, there are equivalence constants depending on the involved parameters when comparing different quasi-norms. In this case, the correct normalizations of truncated norms are provided by
\|\cdot\|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and
\|\cdot\|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(cf. Proposition
3.5
).

Theorem 17.20
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty,0<r<\infty
and
-\infty<s<\infty
. Then, for every
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(17.61)

\lim_{b\to 0-}(1-2^{br})^{1/r}\,\|f\|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

and if, additionally,
p<\infty
then

(17.62)

\lim_{b\to 0-}(1-2^{br})^{1/r}\,\|f\|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Remark 17.21
.

The philosophy behind the existence of the limits in (
17.61
) and (
17.62
) relies on the facts that

(17.63)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}<\infty\implies f=0

and

(17.64)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}<\infty\implies f=0

(cf. Remark
3.7
). Thus, given a non-zero function
f
, the blow-up of the functionals in (
17.63
) and (
17.64
) can be compensated with the decay given by the factor
1-2^{br}
as
b\to 0-
. Note that a similar phenomenon can not be expected for
b\to 0+
, since
T^{*}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
T^{*}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are non-trivial spaces, cf. Remark
3.7
.

Proof of Theorem
17.20
.

We start by proving (
17.61
). Let
\varepsilon>0
. Accordingly, there exists
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for every
j\geq j_{0}
,

(17.65)

\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}-\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}<\varepsilon.

Assume
b<0
. We have

\displaystyle|(1-2^{br})\,\|f\|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\bigg|\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}-\frac{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}-\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+\bigg|\bigg(\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}-\frac{1}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\bigg)\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}

(17.66)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=:I+II+III.

To estimate
I
, we can invoke (
17.65
) so that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg[\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}-\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg]

(17.67)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}=\varepsilon\,2^{j_{0}br}.

Concerning
II
, we can estimate

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}-\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\bigg)\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

(17.68)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{jbr}}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}=(1-2^{j_{0}br})\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

With the aim of estimate
III
, we consider an auxiliary parameter
b_{0}>0
(without loss of generality we may assume
b_{0}>-b
) such that

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}(1+\nu)^{b_{0}q}(1+\nu)^{-b_{0}q}\|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})\|f\|_{B^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{j(b+b_{0})r}

(17.69)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})(1-2^{j_{0}(b+b_{0})r})}{1-2^{(b+b_{0})r}}\,\|f\|_{B^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

Combining now (
17.66
)–(
17.69
) we achieve

\displaystyle|(1-2^{br})\,\|f\|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-156.49014pt\varepsilon\,2^{j_{0}br}+(1-2^{j_{0}br})\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}+\frac{2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})(1-2^{j_{0}(b+b_{0})r})}{1-2^{(b+b_{0})r}}\,\|f\|_{B^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

Taking limits on both sides of the previous inequality as
b\to 0-
we conclude that

\lim_{b\to 0-}|(1-2^{br})\,\|f\|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|\leq\varepsilon.

The proof of (
17.62
) follows similar ideas as above. Let
\varepsilon>0
and
b<0
. Choose
j_{0}
such that

(17.70)

\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}-\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}<\varepsilon.

We have

\displaystyle|(1-2^{br})\,\|f\|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\bigg|\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|^{r}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\frac{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg[\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|^{r}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+\bigg|\bigg(\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}-\frac{1}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\bigg)\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+\frac{1}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{jbr}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|^{r}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(17.71)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=:J+JJ+JJJ.

To estimate
J
, we can make use of (
17.70
):

(17.72)

J\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{jbr}=\varepsilon\,2^{j_{0}br}.

On the other hand, elementary computations lead to

(17.73)

JJ=(1-2^{j_{0}br})\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

It remains to estimate
JJJ
. We can proceed as follows

\displaystyle JJJ

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{b_{0}r}}{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}}\,\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{j(b+b_{0})r}\bigg\|\bigg(\sum_{\nu=0}^{2^{j}}2^{\nu sq}(1+\nu)^{-b_{0}q}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\bigg\|^{r}_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})\|f\|_{F^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}-1}2^{j(b+b_{0})r}

(17.74)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})(1-2^{j_{0}(b+b_{0})r})}{1-2^{(b+b_{0})r}}\,\|f\|_{F^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

Putting together (
17.71
)–(
17.74
), we achieve

\displaystyle|(1-2^{br})\,\|f\|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\leq\varepsilon\,2^{j_{0}br}+(1-2^{j_{0}br})\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}+\frac{2^{b_{0}r}(1-2^{br})(1-2^{j_{0}(b+b_{0})r})}{1-2^{(b+b_{0})r}}\,\|f\|_{F^{s,-b_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

The proof is finished by taking limit as
b\to 0
in the last estimate.
∎

We can also get Bourgain--Brezis--Mironescu formulas for the standard Besov norms given by differences

|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{1/q}

in terms of truncated Besov norms. Here
0<s<k,\,k\in\mathbb{N},
and
0<p,q\leq\infty
. To be more precise, consider the truncated Besov norms (cf. (
6.4
))

|f|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\bigg(\int_{t<|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/r},

then the following holds.

Theorem 17.22
.

Let
0<s<k,\,k\in\mathbb{N},0<p,q\leq\infty
and
0<r<\infty
. Then, for every
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\lim_{b\to 0-}\,(-br)^{1/r}|f|^{*}_{T^{b}_{r}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Remark 17.23
.

The interesting case in Theorem
17.22
occurs when
q\neq r
, otherwise a simple application of Fubini’s theorem gives

|f|^{*q}_{T^{b}_{q}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=-\frac{1}{bq}\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}(1-\log|h|)^{bq}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh

and thus

\lim_{b\to 0-}\,(-bq)^{1/q}|f|^{*}_{T^{b}_{q}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof of Theorem
17.22
.

Given any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
t_{0}\in(0,1)
such that

(17.75)

|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}-\bigg(\int_{t<|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}<\varepsilon

for
t\in(0,t_{0})
. Let
b<0
. Note that

(17.76)

\int_{0}^{t_{0}}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\frac{dt}{t}=-\frac{(1-\log t_{0})^{br}}{br}.

Therefore

\displaystyle|(-br)|f|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt(-br)\int_{0}^{t_{0}}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\bigg|\bigg(\int_{t<|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}-\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\bigg|\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+(1-(1-\log t_{0})^{br})\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+(-br)\int_{t_{0}}^{1}(1-\log t)^{br-1}\bigg(\int_{t<|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\frac{dt}{t}

(17.77)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=:\mathcal{I}+\mathcal{II}+\mathcal{III}.

By (
17.75
) and (
17.76
),

(17.78)

\mathcal{I}\leq\varepsilon(1-\log t_{0})^{br}.

To estimate
\mathcal{III}
, we choose
b_{0}>0
satisfying
b_{0}>-b
, thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{III}

\displaystyle\leq(-br)\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}(1-\log|h|)^{-b_{0}q}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}\int_{t_{0}}^{1}(1-\log t)^{(b+b_{0})r-1}\frac{dt}{t}

(17.79)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-b((1-\log t_{0})^{(b+b_{0})r}-1)}{b+b_{0}}\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}(1-\log|h|)^{-b_{0}q}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}.

Inserting (
17.78
) and (
17.79
) into (
17.77
), we achieve

\displaystyle|(-br)|f|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\varepsilon(1-\log t_{0})^{br}+(1-(1-\log t_{0})^{br})|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}

(17.80)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt-\frac{b((1-\log t_{0})^{(b+b_{0})r}-1)}{b+b_{0}}\bigg(\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-sq-d}(1-\log|h|)^{-b_{0}q}\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\,dh\bigg)^{r/q}.

Note that the last integral is convergent since
\|\Delta^{k}_{h}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim|h|^{k}\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{k}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(see e.g.
[
Ste70
, Proposition 3, p. 139]
) and
k>s
. Taking now successive limits in (
17.80
) first as
b\to 0-
and then an
\varepsilon\to 0
, we get

\lim_{b\to 0-}(-br)|f|^{*r}_{T^{b}_{r}\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=|f|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{r}.

∎

17.6.
Fourier series

We consider the Fourier coefficients of
f:\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{C},\,f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

\widehat{f}(m)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}f(x)e^{-im\cdot x}\,dx,\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},

and the Fourier transform

\mathfrak{F}(f):=\{\widehat{f}(m)\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}.

The mapping properties of the Fourier transform of Besov functions are well known, cf.
[
Zy02
]
,
[
Pe76
]
and
[
Pi81
]
. In more detail, if
1\leq p\leq 2,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
then

(17.81)

\mathfrak{F}:B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

By Remark
10.4
, note that the best possible result is given by
p=2
, i.e.,

\mathfrak{F}:B^{d(\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{2})}_{2,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\qquad 0<u<2.

In particular, for
u=q
, we recover the classical Szász result on absolute convergence of Fourier series. Moreover, if
u=q=1
then we arrive at the Bernstein result:

\mathfrak{F}:B^{d/2}_{2,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Here
\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
is the
Lorentz sequence space
endowed with the quasi-norm

(17.82)

\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}:=\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(n^{1/u}\lambda_{n}^{*})^{q}\frac{1}{n}\bigg)^{1/q}<\infty

(with the usual modification if
q=\infty
). As usual,
\{\lambda_{n}^{*}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
denotes the
non-increasing rearrangement
of the sequence
\{|\lambda_{m}|\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
relative to
\lambda=\{\lambda_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
. In particular, if
u=q
then
\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})=\ell_{u}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Note that (
17.82
) corresponds to the discrete version of (
17.18
).

Dealing with Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, it is an immediate consequence of embeddings (
13.1
) and (
17.81
) that

(17.83)

\mathfrak{F}:F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})

provided that
1\leq p<2,0<q\leq\infty,s>0
and
\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
.

Fourier transform on
T^{b}_{r}A^{s}_{p,q}
.
Our next goal is to investigate the mapping properties of the Fourier transform in the setting of truncated function spaces. To proceed with, we first need to introduce truncated counterparts of Lorentz spaces
\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.

Definition 17.24
.

Let
0<u<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty
and
-\infty<b<\infty
. The truncated Lorentz sequence space
T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
is formed by all those
\lambda=\{\lambda_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
such that

\|\lambda\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}=\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\bigg(\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}<\infty

(with the standard modifications if
q=\infty
and/or
r=\infty
).

Remark 17.25
.

Note that (cf. (
17.82
))

(17.84)

\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}.

Then the definition of
T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
via appropriate truncations of (
17.84
) imitates the definition of truncated function spaces given in Definition
3.1
.

Clearly
T^{b}_{q}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})=\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, the
Lorentz–Zygmund sequence space
,

\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}:=\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(n^{1/u}(1+\log n)^{b}\lambda_{n}^{*})^{q}\frac{1}{n}\bigg)^{1/q},

see (
17.18
). In particular,
\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{0}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})=\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. More generally, the relationships between
T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and
\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
are given in the following

Proposition 17.26
.

Let
0<u<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty
and
-\infty<b<\infty
. Then

(17.85)

\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b-1/r+1/\min\{q,r\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\hookrightarrow\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b-1/r+1/\max\{q,r\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})

and

(17.86)

\ell_{u,\min\{q,r\}}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\hookrightarrow\ell_{u,\max\{q,r\}}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Proof.

We will only prove the first embeddings in (
17.85
) and (
17.86
); the corresponding second one follows similar ideas. Assume first
r\geq q
. By Hölder’s inequality, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\bigg(\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q}\bigg)^{1/q}\lesssim 2^{j(1/q-1/r)}\bigg(\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}.

Accordingly

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b-1/r+1/q)r}\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{r}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\asymp\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(2^{n/u}(1+n)^{b-1/r+1/q}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{r}\right)^{1/r}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b-1/r+1/q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}.

On the other hand, by Minkowski’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(2^{n/u}(1+n)^{b}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q}\right)^{1/q}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}.

Secondly, suppose
r\leq q
. Then

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jbr}\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{r}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\asymp\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(2^{n/u}(1+n)^{b}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{r}\right)^{1/r}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}.

∎

The next result shows that (
17.81
) can be extended to the truncated setting.

Theorem 17.27
.

Let
1\leq p\leq 2,0<q,r\leq\infty,s>0,b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
and
\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. Then

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

As a consequence

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b-1/r+1/\max\{q,r\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\cap\ell_{u,\max\{q,r\}}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Remark 17.28
.

(i) By Theorem
10.1
, the optimal result in Theorem
17.27
is

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}B^{d(\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{2})}_{2,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\qquad 0<u<2.

(ii) Letting
q=r
in Theorem
17.27
one recovers (cf.
[
DRS79
, Corollary 7.3(i)]
)

\mathfrak{F}:B^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),

which is an extension of (
17.81
).

The proof of Theorem
17.27
relies on limiting interpolation techniques. In particular, the next result shows that the spaces
T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
can be generated from classical Lorentz spaces
\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
via limiting interpolation, i.e., it establishes the analogue of Theorems
4.2
and
4.8
for Lorentz sequence spaces.

Theorem 17.29
.

Let
0<u_{0}<u_{1}<\infty,0<q_{0},q_{1},r\leq\infty,b_{1}>0
and
b_{0}<0
. Then (with equivalence of quasi-norms)

T^{b_{i}}_{r}\ell_{u_{i},q_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})=(\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(1-i,b_{i}-1/r),r}\qquad\text{for}\qquad i\in\{0,1\}.

Remark 17.30
.

The assumption
u_{0}<u_{1}
guarantees that the pair
(\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))
is ordered (i.e.,
\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\hookrightarrow\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
).

Proof of Theorem
17.29
.

For simplicity, we set
K(t,\lambda)=K(t,\lambda;\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))
for
t>0
and
\lambda\in\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Let
1/\alpha=1/u_{0}-1/u_{1}
. According to the Holmstedt’s formula
[
H70
, Theorem 4.2]

K(t,\lambda)\asymp t\bigg(\int_{0}^{t^{-\alpha}}(\xi^{1/u_{0}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}+\bigg(\int_{t^{-\alpha}}^{\infty}(\xi^{1/u_{1}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{1}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{1/q_{1}}

where

\lambda^{*}(\xi)=\lambda_{n}^{*}\qquad\text{if}\qquad\xi\in(n-1,n],\qquad n\in\mathbb{N}.

By (
4.6
) and a simple change of variables, we get

\displaystyle\|\lambda\|_{(\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(1-i,b_{i}-1/r),r}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(t^{-1+i}(1-\log t)^{b_{i}-1/r}K(t,\lambda))^{r}\frac{dt}{t}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\asymp\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-ir/\alpha}(1+\log t)^{(b_{i}-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(\xi^{1/u_{0}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{r/q_{0}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt+\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{(1-i)r/\alpha}(1+\log t)^{(b_{i}-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{t}^{\infty}(\xi^{1/u_{1}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{1}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{r/q_{1}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

(17.87)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=:I+II.

Assume
i=1
. By basic monotonicity properties and Hardy’s inequality (
B.4
) (note that
b_{1}>0
)

\lambda_{1}^{*}+II\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r}\bigg(\sum_{n=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}(2^{n/u_{1}}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{r/q_{1}}\right)^{1/r}=\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r}\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}.

On the other hand, by Hardy’s inequality (
B.3
),

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-r/\alpha}(1+\log t)^{(b_{1}-1/r)r}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}(\xi^{1/u_{0}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{r/q_{0}}\frac{dt}{t}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}(\xi^{1/u_{0}}\lambda^{*}(\xi))^{q_{0}}\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\bigg)^{1/q_{0}}+\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jr/\alpha}(1+j)^{(b_{1}-1/r)r}\bigg(\sum_{n=0}^{j}(2^{n/u_{0}}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q_{0}}\bigg)^{r/q_{0}}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}[2^{j/u_{1}}(1+j)^{b_{1}-1/r}\lambda_{2^{j}}^{*}]^{r}\bigg)^{1/r}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{jb_{1}r}\bigg(\sum_{n=2^{j}}^{\infty}(2^{n/u_{1}}\lambda_{2^{n}}^{*})^{q_{1}}\bigg)^{r/q_{1}}\bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\asymp\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r}\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}

where the last step follows from (
B.4
).
Hence (
17.87
) implies

\|\lambda\|_{(\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(1-i,b_{i}-1/r),r}}\asymp\|\lambda\|_{T^{b_{1}}_{r}\ell_{u_{1},q_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}.

The case
i=0
can be carried out similarly and is left to the interested reader.
∎

We are now ready to give the proof of

Proof of Theorem
17.27
.

Assume first
b>0
. Let
s_{0}>s
and
1/u_{0}=s_{0}/d+1/p^{\prime}
(and so
u_{0}<u
). It follows from (
17.81
) that

\mathfrak{F}:B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathfrak{F}:B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u_{0},q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Applying the limiting interpolation method (
4.6
) with
\theta=0
, we derive

(17.88)

\mathfrak{F}:(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}\to(\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}.

On the one hand, by Theorem
4.2
,

(17.89)

(B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

and, on the other hand, by Theorem
17.29
(with
i=1
),

(17.90)

(\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u_{0},q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(0,b-1/r),r}=T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Combining (
17.88
)–(
17.90
) we arrive at

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

The case
b<0
follows similar ideas as above, but now taking
s_{0}\in(0,s)
and invoking the limiting interpolation method (
4.6
) with
\theta=1
related to the couple
(B^{s_{0}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
.
∎

Combining Theorem
17.27
and the Franke–Jawerth embedding obtained from Theorem
13.1
,

T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\hookrightarrow T^{b}_{r}B^{s_{0}}_{p_{0},p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

for
p<p_{0}
and
s-d/p=s_{0}-d/p_{0}
, we derive the analogue of (
17.83
) for truncated Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.

Corollary 17.31
.

Let
1\leq p<2,0<q,r\leq\infty,s>0,b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
and
\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. Then

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

As a consequence

\mathfrak{F}:T^{b}_{r}F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,r}(\log\ell)_{b-1/r+1/\max\{p,r\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\cap\ell_{u,\max\{p,r\}}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

In the remainder of this section we study the sharpness of Theorem
17.27
: First, we extend the range of the integrability
p
and second, we obtain two-sided estimates for the Fourier transform. To avoid delicate issues, we will focus only on the case
d=1
.

Theorem 17.32
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q,r\leq\infty,s\in\mathbb{R},b\in\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
and
\frac{1}{u}=s+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. Let
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
be such that

f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)

with
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nonnegative general monotone sequences. Then

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{F}(f)\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z})}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
b_{n}=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. According to Theorem
16.10
, we have

(17.91)

\|f\|_{T^{b}_{r}B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}2^{\nu(s+1-1/p)q}a_{2^{\nu}}^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}.

To complete the proof, it only remains to verify that

(17.92)

\|\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|_{T^{b}_{r}\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z})}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu/u}a_{2^{\nu}})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r},

that is

(17.93)

\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu/u}a_{2^{\nu}}^{*})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}\asymp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{kbr}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu/u}a_{2^{\nu}})^{q}\bigg)^{r/q}\right)^{1/r}

for nonnegative sequences
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in GM
. Indeed, since
a_{k}\lesssim a_{n}
for
n\leq k\leq 2n
(cf. (
16.2
)), it is plain to see that
a_{n}^{*}\gtrsim a_{n}
. This gives the estimate
\gtrsim
in (
17.93
). On the other hand, the estimate
\lesssim
in (
17.93
) is a consequence of
[
HLP52
, Theorem 368, p. 261]
, which implies

\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu/u}a_{2^{\nu}}^{*})^{q}\leq\sum_{\nu=2^{k}-1}^{2^{k+1}-2}(2^{\nu/u}a_{2^{\nu}})^{q}.

∎

Fourier transform on
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}
.
As a distinguished example of Corollary
17.31
, we determine the mapping properties of the Fourier transform acting on Lipschitz spaces (cf. Proposition
3.8
).

Corollary 17.33
.

Let
1<p<2,0<q\leq\infty,s>0,b<-1/q
and
\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. Then

\mathfrak{F}:\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{u,p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

In particular

\mathfrak{F}:\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\cap\ell_{u,\max\{p,q\}}(\log\ell)_{b+1/q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})

and if, in addition,
p=q
then

\mathfrak{F}:\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,p}(\log\ell)_{b+1/p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

The optimality of the previous result can be shown as a combination of Theorem
16.15
and (
17.92
).

Corollary 17.34
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,s>0,b<-1/q
and
\frac{1}{u}=s+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}
. Let
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
be such that

f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)

with
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nonnegative general monotone sequences. Then

\|f\|_{\emph{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{F}(f)\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{u,p}(\mathbb{Z})}.

Fourier transform on
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}
.
Let
1\leq p\leq 2,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. The mapping properties of Fourier transform on
\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})
were already investigated by DeVore, Riemenschneider and Sharpley
[
DRS79
]
via weak-type interpolation techniques. Namely, they obtained (cf.
[
DRS79
, Corollary 7.3]
)

\mathfrak{F}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})\to\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z}).

However, this result is not sharp. Indeed, applying reiteration formulas for limiting approximation spaces, it was shown in
[
CD15a
, Theorem 5.1]
that

(17.94)

\mathfrak{F}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})\to\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p^{\prime},q\}}(\mathbb{Z}).

Note that
\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p^{\prime},q\}}(\mathbb{Z})\subsetneq\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b}(\mathbb{Z})
if
q\neq\infty
and
p\neq 1
.

Next we apply the theory of truncated spaces in order to get a significant improvement of (
17.94
) (even in the classical scale formed by Lorentz–Zygmund spaces).

Theorem 17.35
.

Let
1<p\leq 2,0<q\leq\infty
and
b>-1/q
. Then

\mathfrak{F}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

In particular (cf. Proposition
17.26
)

\mathfrak{F}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\cap\ell_{p^{\prime},\max\{p,q\}}(\log\ell)_{b+1/q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Remark 17.36
.

In general
\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p,q\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\subsetneq\ell_{p^{\prime},q}(\log\ell)_{b+1/\max\{p^{\prime},q\}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, so that the previous result sharpens (
17.94
).

Proof of Theorem
17.35
.

According to the Paley inequality (see e.g.
[
Pe76
, Chapter 6]
)

\mathfrak{F}:L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})

and (
17.81
)

\mathfrak{F}:B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\qquad\frac{1}{u}=\frac{s}{d}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}.

By limiting interpolation

(17.95)

\mathfrak{F}:(L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}\to(\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}.

Since
L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, Theorem
4.8
and Proposition
3.8
give

(17.96)

(L_{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}F^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})=\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

On the other hand, by Theorem
17.29
,

(17.97)

(\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\ell_{u,q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}))_{(0,b),q}=T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

Putting together (
17.95
)–(
17.97
),

\mathfrak{F}:\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

∎

The optimality assertion related to Theorem
17.35
reads as follows.

Corollary 17.37
.

Let
1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty,
and
b>-1/q
. Let
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{T})
be such that

f(x)\sim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(a_{n}\cos nx+b_{n}\sin nx)

with
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nonnegative general monotone sequences. Then

\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\|\mathfrak{F}(f)\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z})}.

Proof.

It follows from (
16.9
) (more precisely, its periodic counterpart given in
[
DT18
, Theorem 4.27]
) that

\|f\|_{\mathbf{B}^{0,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(p-1)}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}.

On the other hand, by (
17.92
),

\|\mathfrak{F}(f)\|_{T^{b+1/q}_{q}\ell_{p^{\prime},p}(\mathbb{Z})}\asymp\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\bigg(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}-1}2^{\nu(p-1)}F_{0}^{p}(2^{\nu})\bigg)^{q/p}\bigg)^{1/q}.

∎

Appendix A
Fourier-analytical description of Lipschitz spaces

This appendix complements characterizations of Lipschitz spaces
\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
obtained in
[
DHT20
]
. To be more precise, we deal here with their Fourier-analytical descriptions.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
s>0,1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
. Then

(A.1)

\|f\|_{\emph{\text{Lip}}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s2}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{1/q}.

Proof.

We have

(A.2)

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}}\asymp\|((\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(\cdot))\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{2}),L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{2}^{s}))_{(1,b),q}}.

Indeed, this interpolation formula follows from the well-known facts that
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are retracts of
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{2})
and
L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\ell_{2}^{s})
, respectively, with co-retraction operator
\mathfrak{J}(f)=((\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}(\cdot))
(see (
4.10
)).

Now according to Lemma
4.10
(ii), we derive

(A.3)

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}}\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s2}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{1/q}.

On the other hand, the
K
-functional relative to the pair
(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
can be characterized through modulus of smoothness (cf.
[
W79
, (4.2)]
and
[
KT20b
, (1.36)]
), more precisely, for
t\in(0,1)
,

K(t^{s},f;L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\asymp t^{s}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\omega_{s}(f,t)_{p}

which automatically yields

(A.4)

\|f\|_{(L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),H^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{(1,b),q}}\asymp\|f\|_{\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Putting together (
A.3
) and (
A.4
), we achieve

\|f\|_{\text{Lip}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{j(b+1/q)q}\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\nu=2^{j}-1}^{2^{j+1}-2}2^{\nu s2}|(\varphi_{\nu}\widehat{f})^{\vee}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{1/q}.

∎

We present another characterization of Lipschitz norm in terms of
Fourier means. Let
\Psi_{n}(f)
stand for any of the following
means:

(1)

the
\ell_{r}
-
Fourier means
given by

\widehat{S_{n,r}}f(\xi):=\chi_{\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{\ell_{r}}\leq n\}}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi),\qquad r=1,\infty.

Here,
\chi_{\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\|\xi\|_{\ell_{r}}\leq n\}}
denotes the characteristic function relative to the
\ell_{r}
-ball centered at the origin and radii
n
;

(2)

the
de la Vallée Poussin-type means

V_{n}f
(cf. (
5.18
); see also
[
KT20a
]
);

(3)

the
Riesz spherical means

R_{n}^{\beta,\delta}f
given by

\widehat{R_{n}^{\beta,\delta}f}(\xi)=\bigg(1-\bigg(\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{n}\bigg)^{\beta}\bigg)_{+}^{\delta}\widehat{f}(\xi)

for
\beta>0
and
\delta>d|\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}|-\frac{1}{2}
.

Theorem A.2
.

Let
s>0,1<p<\infty,0<q\leq\infty
and
b<-1/q
. Then,

\|f\|_{\emph{\text{Lip}}^{s,b}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\asymp\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{bq}\|(-\Delta)^{s/2}{\Psi_{2^{j}}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}\right)^{1/q}.

The proof immediately follows from Hardy’s inequalities and the following estimates

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{\nu=j+1}^{\infty}2^{-\nu s\tau}\|(-\Delta)^{s/2}{\Psi_{2^{\nu}}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\tau}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\omega_{s}(f,2^{-j})_{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{\nu=j+1}^{\infty}2^{-\nu s\theta}\|(-\Delta)^{s/2}{\Psi_{2^{\nu}}}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\theta}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\theta}},

where
\tau=\max(2,p)
and
\theta=\min(2,p)
, see

[
KT20a
, Theorem 6.3]
.

Appendix B
Some auxiliary inequalities

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\lambda_{j}=2^{-2^{j-1}}
for
j\geq 1
,
\lambda_{0}=1
and
R>0
. Let
F
be a nonnegative function defined on
(0,1)
. Then

(B.1)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\asymp\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}.

The corresponding multivariate result also holds: Let
F
be a nonnegative function defined on the unit ball
B(0,1)
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then

(B.2)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}<|h|<\lambda_{j}}F(h)\,dh\bigg)^{R}\asymp\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}<|h|<t}F(h)\,dh\bigg)^{R}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}.

Proof.

To show (
B.1
) we can proceed as follows. We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+2}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+2}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}.

Concerning the converse estimate, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}\,\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{\lambda_{j+1}}^{\lambda_{j}}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{\sqrt{t}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t}^{\sqrt{t}}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

\displaystyle\asymp\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{t^{2}}^{t}F(z)\,dz\bigg)^{R}\frac{dt}{t(1-\log t)}

where the last estimate follows from a simple change of variables. This completes the proof of (
B.1
). The proof of (
B.2
) follows along the same lines as (
B.1
).
∎

For the convenience of the reader, we collect below some Hardy-type inequalities invoked frequently in the paper. For complete treatments, we refer the reader to
[
KP03
]
,
[
OK90
]
, and
[
PST09
]
.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
\lambda>0,\,0<q\leq\infty,
and
-\infty<b<\infty
. Let
\xi_{j}\geq 0
for
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then, for every
j_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(B.3)

\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-j\lambda q}(1+j)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{k=j_{0}}^{j}\xi_{k}\bigg)^{q}\asymp\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-j\lambda q}(1+j)^{bq}\xi_{j}^{q}

and

(B.4)

\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{j\lambda q}(1+j)^{bq}\bigg(\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}\xi_{k}\bigg)^{q}\asymp\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\infty}2^{j\lambda q}(1+j)^{bq}\xi_{j}^{q}

where the hidden equivalence constants are independent of
j_{0}
.
